# Research and testing method

1754 claims from 202 sources. Each line links to the claim inside its source note.


## [[MT001 - 133 - Jan van Eck- Macro Forces Shape Markets]]
- [[MT001 - 133 - Jan van Eck- Macro Forces Shape Markets#^MT001-C01-2|MT001-C01-2]] — Academic finance (CAPM-style stock-to-market laws) treats markets as a self-contained system, which he rejects as a basis for building portfolios because markets sit inside a larger world.
- [[MT001 - 133 - Jan van Eck- Macro Forces Shape Markets#^MT001-C02-4|MT001-C02-4]] — Any 40-year chart or backtest carries a built-in bias from falling rates and falling inflation, so results must be read with that regime in mind.
- [[MT001 - 133 - Jan van Eck- Macro Forces Shape Markets#^MT001-C02-5|MT001-C02-5]] — Macro insights can start from company-level data points and conversations (e.g. firms trading with China in 1992), so bottom-up observation feeds macro views.
- [[MT001 - 133 - Jan van Eck- Macro Forces Shape Markets#^MT001-C05-4|MT001-C05-4]] — Because the world keeps changing, fixed rules are hard to write, so he relies on evidence across cycles to bias allocation one way or the other.
- [[MT001 - 133 - Jan van Eck- Macro Forces Shape Markets#^MT001-C08-1|MT001-C08-1]] — Valuation alone fails as a market signal (international markets are cheap yet need another driver), so valuation is less, not un-, important after the trend is framed.

## [[MT002 - 2024 Trend Following Recap- Key Insights from Top Traders - Systematic Investor 327]]
- [[MT002 - 2024 Trend Following Recap- Key Insights from Top Traders - Systematic Investor 327#^MT002-C02-5b|MT002-C02-5b]] — A single bad event like the August V-shape is not a reason to refit trend models, since the behaviour is known and refitting to one event overfits.
- [[MT002 - 2024 Trend Following Recap- Key Insights from Top Traders - Systematic Investor 327#^MT002-C05-1b|MT002-C05-1b]] — For a trend follower geopolitical views do not enter the model; prices already carry the effect, so market observation and trading should be kept separate.
- [[MT002 - 2024 Trend Following Recap- Key Insights from Top Traders - Systematic Investor 327#^MT002-C07-6b|MT002-C07-6b]] — Open question whether alternative (less liquid) markets trend more strongly and implementably after costs; anecdotally, alternative-market managers had historic losses in 2024.
- [[MT002 - 2024 Trend Following Recap- Key Insights from Top Traders - Systematic Investor 327#^MT002-C08-2|MT002-C08-2]] — There is no statistically significant evidence that less liquid futures trend more; apparent evidence comes from shorter histories that coincide with strong trend periods.
- [[MT002 - 2024 Trend Following Recap- Key Insights from Top Traders - Systematic Investor 327#^MT002-C08-3b|MT002-C08-3b]] — Studies showing illiquid markets trend more are biased because their short histories coincide with good trend periods; compare markets over the same window.
- [[MT002 - 2024 Trend Following Recap- Key Insights from Top Traders - Systematic Investor 327#^MT002-C10-2b|MT002-C10-2b]] — Market efficiency can only be tested jointly with a valuation model, which is feasible in equities but not in commodities without a valuation anchor.
- [[MT002 - 2024 Trend Following Recap- Key Insights from Top Traders - Systematic Investor 327#^MT002-C11-3b|MT002-C11-3b]] — Rob has found no serious use for AI in systematic trading.
- [[MT002 - 2024 Trend Following Recap- Key Insights from Top Traders - Systematic Investor 327#^MT002-C12-1|MT002-C12-1]] — Machine learning models are only marginally better or worse than simple trend benchmarks, which are hard to beat.
- [[MT002 - 2024 Trend Following Recap- Key Insights from Top Traders - Systematic Investor 327#^MT002-C12-2|MT002-C12-2]] — AI is useful for aggregating data and basic coding as a cost saver, not for solving core quantitative problems.
- [[MT002 - 2024 Trend Following Recap- Key Insights from Top Traders - Systematic Investor 327#^MT002-C13-1|MT002-C13-1]] — Given poor, gamed investment data, AI will pick up and amplify biases, so it will not replace models.
- [[MT002 - 2024 Trend Following Recap- Key Insights from Top Traders - Systematic Investor 327#^MT002-C13-4b|MT002-C13-4b]] — Trend following makes no forecasts, only follows prices, so it cannot make prediction mistakes ('prices are primal').
- [[MT002 - 2024 Trend Following Recap- Key Insights from Top Traders - Systematic Investor 327#^MT002-C14-4|MT002-C14-4]] — Trend managers differ because of speed, market universe, purity of trend versus reversion or carry, and discretionary de-risking.
- [[MT002 - 2024 Trend Following Recap- Key Insights from Top Traders - Systematic Investor 327#^MT002-C15-2|MT002-C15-2]] — Small trend parameter or sector-weight differences produce large dispersion that can persist for long periods, one reason for choosing a simple top-down replication approach.
- [[MT002 - 2024 Trend Following Recap- Key Insights from Top Traders - Systematic Investor 327#^MT002-C15-3|MT002-C15-3]] — Classifying managers by style, timing and markets (STM) removes much apparent dispersion in trend following.
- [[MT002 - 2024 Trend Following Recap- Key Insights from Top Traders - Systematic Investor 327#^MT002-C15-4b|MT002-C15-4b]] — He does not see 2024 trend dispersion as meaningfully larger than usual; wide dispersion is the normal state.

## [[MT003 - 40 Years of Trading Lessons with Patrick Welton - Top Traders Unplugged - Ep.153]]
- [[MT003 - 40 Years of Trading Lessons with Patrick Welton - Top Traders Unplugged - Ep.153#^MT003-C06-2|MT003-C06-2]] — Decades ago a trader's interview came down to four questions: edge, where to exploit it, risk management or sizing, and how tails are protected.
- [[MT003 - 40 Years of Trading Lessons with Patrick Welton - Top Traders Unplugged - Ep.153#^MT003-C07-1|MT003-C07-1]] — A systematic approach should define four things: setup (the condition that raises probability), signal (entry and exit), sizing and symmetry (asymmetric payoff), and understanding each helps the trader stay with it.
- [[MT003 - 40 Years of Trading Lessons with Patrick Welton - Top Traders Unplugged - Ep.153#^MT003-C08-1b|MT003-C08-1b]] — Trend following is itself a form of replication (smoothing prices that reflect others' actions), so replicating another manager's P&L via regression is a small further step and a valid method.
- [[MT003 - 40 Years of Trading Lessons with Patrick Welton - Top Traders Unplugged - Ep.153#^MT003-C08-3|MT003-C08-3]] — Replicating the replicators gives better performance than the products because of a better smoothing coefficient, less churn and lower fees and cash management if fully funded.
- [[MT003 - 40 Years of Trading Lessons with Patrick Welton - Top Traders Unplugged - Ep.153#^MT003-C09-1b|MT003-C09-1b]] — Overall cost and manager selection risk are the two main failure points of professional allocators, which is why replication products appeal.
- [[MT003 - 40 Years of Trading Lessons with Patrick Welton - Top Traders Unplugged - Ep.153#^MT003-C10-3|MT003-C10-3]] — Showing investors a plain attribution of trend returns into carry, diffusion and reflexivity explains its zero correlation and positive convexity and persuades them.
- [[MT003 - 40 Years of Trading Lessons with Patrick Welton - Top Traders Unplugged - Ep.153#^MT003-C13-1|MT003-C13-1]] — A trend follower owning 200 of the 250 billion in industry capacity with the same approach would see the same performance, since size alone does not remove returns if methods are diversified.
- [[MT003 - 40 Years of Trading Lessons with Patrick Welton - Top Traders Unplugged - Ep.153#^MT003-C14-3|MT003-C14-3]] — Strategies with long holding periods that are insensitive to a one-day execution delay have more capacity and a larger margin of error than strategies that lose a fifth of P&L from a 15-minute delay.

## [[MT004 - 5% Rates, Bessent's Failed Gamble & What Comes Next w- Krishna Guha - The Real Eisman Play]]
- [[MT004 - 5% Rates, Bessent's Failed Gamble & What Comes Next w- Krishna Guha - The Real Eisman Play#^MT004-C05-2b|MT004-C05-2b]] — Over about 30 years since early Greenspan the Fed steadily moved to more forward guidance and strategy communication, so communication regimes differ across eras.

## [[MT005 - A Brief World Economic History For Gold, Silver, and Fiat Money]]
- [[MT005 - A Brief World Economic History For Gold, Silver, and Fiat Money#^MT005-C04-6|MT005-C04-6]] — Return data for Newmont for the early 1970s is missing from Bloomberg and would have to be bought from CRSP, so long-history mining and gold backtests need paid data sources.

## [[MT006 - A Challenging October for Trend Following - Systematic Investor 317]]
- [[MT006 - A Challenging October for Trend Following - Systematic Investor 317#^MT006-C02-3|MT006-C02-3]] — Dispersion of results among trend followers is large in 2024, so benchmark indices do not describe individual managers.
- [[MT006 - A Challenging October for Trend Following - Systematic Investor 317#^MT006-C03-3|MT006-C03-3]] — CTA benchmark indices are weighted toward the largest funds, so a wider equal-ish index (Top Traders Unplugged index) shows less gloom and the big-fund index misrepresents the group.
- [[MT006 - A Challenging October for Trend Following - Systematic Investor 317#^MT006-C04-1|MT006-C04-1]] — A trend-replication product shows large tracking error versus the CTA benchmark in 2024 (to the upside).
- [[MT006 - A Challenging October for Trend Following - Systematic Investor 317#^MT006-C06-3|MT006-C06-3]] — The benefit of a protective rule like a trend overlay may take 10-20 years to show, so discipline and a good starting date matter.
- [[MT006 - A Challenging October for Trend Following - Systematic Investor 317#^MT006-C06-4|MT006-C06-4]] — A backtest on three instruments risks regime bias, e.g. bonds had falling rates for about 37 of the past 40 years, so a bond trend result may not repeat; prefer broader diversification.
- [[MT006 - A Challenging October for Trend Following - Systematic Investor 317#^MT006-C10-3|MT006-C10-3]] — Most trend followers use few core approaches (about 5-10) with many parameter variations rather than 20 truly different systems.
- [[MT006 - A Challenging October for Trend Following - Systematic Investor 317#^MT006-C10-X1|MT006-C10-X1]] — Using a larger ensemble of uncorrelated systems reduces the dependence on hindsight-chosen parameters (less overfitting risk) than picking a few optimised systems.

## [[MT007 - A Financial Regime Shift Is Coming - Systematic Investor 332]]
- [[MT007 - A Financial Regime Shift Is Coming - Systematic Investor 332#^MT007-C09-1|MT007-C09-1]] — A recent paper on long-only trend following in equities found positive alpha almost vanished after transaction costs, depending on portfolio size, so institutional frictions must be modelled.
- [[MT007 - A Financial Regime Shift Is Coming - Systematic Investor 332#^MT007-C09-2|MT007-C09-2]] — Stress-test any model by its performance in past uncertainty-index spikes to bracket expected performance and understand why it works or fails.
- [[MT007 - A Financial Regime Shift Is Coming - Systematic Investor 332#^MT007-C10-2|MT007-C10-2]] — Allocators value managers who disclose performance in high-uncertainty periods to bracket expectations without claiming history will repeat.
- [[MT007 - A Financial Regime Shift Is Coming - Systematic Investor 332#^MT007-C10-X1|MT007-C10-X1]] — Building hypothetical stress data from distant analogues such as the 1930s trade wars is not robust, because data are poor and institutions differed; better to accept that anything is possible.
- [[MT007 - A Financial Regime Shift Is Coming - Systematic Investor 332#^MT007-C15-2|MT007-C15-2]] — Complexity theory (Santa Fe Institute) has not been turned into systematic trading models.
- [[MT007 - A Financial Regime Shift Is Coming - Systematic Investor 332#^MT007-C16-2|MT007-C16-2]] — Equal-weighting 20 years of history is questionable because the market rules and structure change; weight recent data differently.

## [[MT008 - AI Bubble Peak- - Systematic Investor - Ep.388]]
- [[MT008 - AI Bubble Peak- - Systematic Investor - Ep.388#^MT008-C08-X1|MT008-C08-X1]] — Regimes should be decomposed into types (price-driven/volatility regimes, monetary and fiscal policy-structural regimes, geopolitical/sentiment regimes) rather than a single label, and the edge comes from this decomposition.
- [[MT008 - AI Bubble Peak- - Systematic Investor - Ep.388#^MT008-C14-1|MT008-C14-1]] — Change-point detection and hidden Markov models detect breaks, though only after the break; macro awareness improves sensitivity.
- [[MT008 - AI Bubble Peak- - Systematic Investor - Ep.388#^MT008-C14-2|MT008-C14-2]] — Model markets as a network of causal connections and watch how connections change through time to get ahead of regime shifts.
- [[MT008 - AI Bubble Peak- - Systematic Investor - Ep.388#^MT008-C14-3|MT008-C14-3]] — Identifying causality is the main issue in finance, with AI causal-discovery challenges.
- [[MT008 - AI Bubble Peak- - Systematic Investor - Ep.388#^MT008-C15-1|MT008-C15-1]] — The factor zoo: factors found in past data often vanish out of sample, so seek causal factors and check models against data.
- [[MT008 - AI Bubble Peak- - Systematic Investor - Ep.388#^MT008-C15-2|MT008-C15-2]] — Research shows features and domain knowledge add more than model complexity in machine learning.
- [[MT008 - AI Bubble Peak- - Systematic Investor - Ep.388#^MT008-C15-3|MT008-C15-3]] — Machine learning works on stationary data but struggles on non-stationary adaptive markets (Lo).
- [[MT008 - AI Bubble Peak- - Systematic Investor - Ep.388#^MT008-C16-1|MT008-C16-1]] — Use simple models as benchmark; learn AI but do not over-optimise.

## [[MT009 - AI Can Build Your Portfolio—But Can You Trust It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.419]]
- [[MT009 - AI Can Build Your Portfolio—But Can You Trust It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.419#^MT009-C03-1|MT009-C03-1]] — Even identifying the current market state is difficult, never mind predicting it.
- [[MT009 - AI Can Build Your Portfolio—But Can You Trust It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.419#^MT009-C05-2|MT009-C05-2]] — Alpha Architect note tests faster look-backs in high VIX and slower in low VIX against a static window on five ETFs.
- [[MT009 - AI Can Build Your Portfolio—But Can You Trust It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.419#^MT009-C06-1|MT009-C06-1]] — VIX-triggered look-back switches are fragile: high-VIX periods in the nine-year extension amounted to only about three months.
- [[MT009 - AI Can Build Your Portfolio—But Can You Trust It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.419#^MT009-C06-3|MT009-C06-3]] — The paper finds no significant benefit from VIX-conditioned look-backs versus a 10-month static window, outside a 2020-2021 period.
- [[MT009 - AI Can Build Your Portfolio—But Can You Trust It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.419#^MT009-C06-X1|MT009-C06-X1]] — The Alpha Architect test is long-only relative (cross-sectional) momentum with monthly rebalancing, so its results do not transfer directly to directional time-series trend following, which takes long or short positions on each asset's own trend.
- [[MT009 - AI Can Build Your Portfolio—But Can You Trust It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.419#^MT009-C07-1|MT009-C07-1]] — After costs, top-two selection shows no difference between dynamic and fixed windows; an apparent edge came from November 2020 (vaccine month).
- [[MT009 - AI Can Build Your Portfolio—But Can You Trust It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.419#^MT009-C07-2|MT009-C07-2]] — Trigger-based rules have low statistical power since they rarely activate; prefer a continuous function.
- [[MT009 - AI Can Build Your Portfolio—But Can You Trust It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.419#^MT009-C08-3|MT009-C08-3]] — Faster models help at turning points, but dynamically deploying speed in time to cut exposure is the open question.
- [[MT009 - AI Can Build Your Portfolio—But Can You Trust It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.419#^MT009-C08-X1|MT009-C08-X1]] — Using volatility to classify regimes where trend reversals are more likely, and so to time trend following, is very hard and he cannot show statistically that it is worth doing.
- [[MT009 - AI Can Build Your Portfolio—But Can You Trust It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.419#^MT009-C09-1|MT009-C09-1]] — A timing indicator must be judged on false positives; calling 3 of 33 turning points tells nothing without them.
- [[MT009 - AI Can Build Your Portfolio—But Can You Trust It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.419#^MT009-C13-4|MT009-C13-4]] — LLM backtests suffer from look-ahead knowledge.
- [[MT009 - AI Can Build Your Portfolio—But Can You Trust It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.419#^MT009-C14-1|MT009-C14-1]] — Look-ahead bias in LLM research (knowledge in model parameters), plus data-mining as agents vote for the better-performing backtest.
- [[MT009 - AI Can Build Your Portfolio—But Can You Trust It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.419#^MT009-C14-2|MT009-C14-2]] — LLM outputs are non-reproducible, which blocks building a representative backtest.
- [[MT009 - AI Can Build Your Portfolio—But Can You Trust It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.419#^MT009-C14-X1|MT009-C14-X1]] — One fix for LLM look-ahead bias is to restrict backtests to model vintages available at each date, but older models may be too weak to run the analysis, so agentic allocation results must largely be taken at face value.

## [[MT010 - AI Could Change Markets Forever - Systematic Investor - Ep.417]]
- [[MT010 - AI Could Change Markets Forever - Systematic Investor - Ep.417#^MT010-C07-1|MT010-C07-1]] — Fitting rule weights per instrument is overfitting; fitting per asset class or pooled across all instruments does about as well.
- [[MT010 - AI Could Change Markets Forever - Systematic Investor - Ep.417#^MT010-C07-X1|MT010-C07-X1]] — Whether to use different rule weights (more carry, faster or slower trend) by asset class was partly decided by organisational politics in asset-class research teams; it should instead be treated as an empirical question tested on data.
- [[MT010 - AI Could Change Markets Forever - Systematic Investor - Ep.417#^MT010-C08-1|MT010-C08-1]] — Pooled (all instruments) and asset-class fits both work; with few instruments (e.g. 2 bonds, 3 equities, 1 FX) do not fit by asset class.
- [[MT010 - AI Could Change Markets Forever - Systematic Investor - Ep.417#^MT010-C08-2|MT010-C08-2]] — Most robust: average the individual, asset-class and global fits, which has about 0.98 to 0.99 correlation with global-only fitting, mattering more for small portfolios.
- [[MT010 - AI Could Change Markets Forever - Systematic Investor - Ep.417#^MT010-C08-3|MT010-C08-3]] — Some managers use global parameters with different models for diversification.
- [[MT010 - AI Could Change Markets Forever - Systematic Investor - Ep.417#^MT010-C09-1|MT010-C09-1]] — A matrix fit that allocates to fast trend in cheap, diversifying markets adds complexity but little value.
- [[MT010 - AI Could Change Markets Forever - Systematic Investor - Ep.417#^MT010-C09-2|MT010-C09-2]] — Default to no fitting; fit only with strong, robust, out-of-sample evidence.
- [[MT010 - AI Could Change Markets Forever - Systematic Investor - Ep.417#^MT010-C10-1|MT010-C10-1]] — Systematic trading means following the system; overriding signals makes the system impossible to test.
- [[MT010 - AI Could Change Markets Forever - Systematic Investor - Ep.417#^MT010-C10-2|MT010-C10-2]] — Ignoring system signals when inconvenient is the worst way to mix discretion and systematic.
- [[MT010 - AI Could Change Markets Forever - Systematic Investor - Ep.417#^MT010-C11-1|MT010-C11-1]] — Limit discretion to what and when to buy; make speed, exits and position size rule-based.
- [[MT010 - AI Could Change Markets Forever - Systematic Investor - Ep.417#^MT010-C11-4|MT010-C11-4]] — Discretionary exits are allowed only with a statistically significant track record of skill.
- [[MT010 - AI Could Change Markets Forever - Systematic Investor - Ep.417#^MT010-C13-1|MT010-C13-1]] — AI in quant research mainly risks overfitting, needing guard rails and non-AI code.
- [[MT010 - AI Could Change Markets Forever - Systematic Investor - Ep.417#^MT010-C15-1|MT010-C15-1]] — Revisit published research after about 10 years for out-of-sample evidence (Alpha Architect VIX and trend; Lasse Pedersen 100-year trend paper).
- [[MT010 - AI Could Change Markets Forever - Systematic Investor - Ep.417#^MT010-C16-2|MT010-C16-2]] — High volatility can mean 2008, 2020 or the tariff tantrum, with different optimal speeds, and one cannot know ahead which.
- [[MT010 - AI Could Change Markets Forever - Systematic Investor - Ep.417#^MT010-C16-4|MT010-C16-4]] — Rob will not implement VIX-based speed changes: prior toward simplicity, though the finding is not random data mining.
- [[MT010 - AI Could Change Markets Forever - Systematic Investor - Ep.417#^MT010-C16-X1|MT010-C16-X1]] — Rob reads the decade-later Alpha Architect update as showing that VIX-dependent trend speed still seems to work out of sample, while warning that the headline result hides much and should not be copied into a system without understanding it.
- [[MT010 - AI Could Change Markets Forever - Systematic Investor - Ep.417#^MT010-C17-2|MT010-C17-2]] — Quant equity managers differ and do not simply hold basic factors.

## [[MT011 - AI, Inflation, and the Portfolio That Refuses to Sit Still - Systematic Investor - Ep.399]]
- [[MT011 - AI, Inflation, and the Portfolio That Refuses to Sit Still - Systematic Investor - Ep.399#^MT011-C09-X1|MT011-C09-X1]] — Alan pushes back on the host's view that inflation stability rather than policy activity drives trend/macro returns: because rates respond to inflation, the two drivers are hard to separate empirically.
- [[MT011 - AI, Inflation, and the Portfolio That Refuses to Sit Still - Systematic Investor - Ep.399#^MT011-C10-4|MT011-C10-4]] — Per AQR's Jordan Brooks, portfolio construction is optimisation of objectives under constraints, and broader investable universe raises the opportunity set.
- [[MT011 - AI, Inflation, and the Portfolio That Refuses to Sit Still - Systematic Investor - Ep.399#^MT011-C15-1|MT011-C15-1]] — An unconstrained trend program is the best-ideas product; researchers generally do not want to constrain design, including equity exposure.

## [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387]]
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C12-5|MT013-C12-5]] — Backtests understate costs because they ignore being picked off by local traders.
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C12-X1|MT013-C12-X1]] — A futures-based CTA replication can deviate from the hedge funds it tracks by several hundred bp a year (about -500 bp in 2023, large outperformance in 2024-2025), driven by about four sources of variation.
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C13-4|MT013-C13-4]] — Liquid alternative funds returned 2-3% over 15 years with about 200 bp fees while equities rose about 14% a year.
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C14-2|MT013-C14-2]] — Liquid-alt category data may mix true hedge funds with quasi multi-asset funds labelled multi-strat, so classification matters.
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C15-2|MT013-C15-2]] — QIS/premium providers talk about engineering and research, not realised Sharpe ratios, so due diligence should ask for realised Sharpe over a stated period.
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C15-3|MT013-C15-3]] — Allocators should define the signal they want and implement it efficiently, not pick managers by narrative.
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C16-1|MT013-C16-1]] — A replication ETF has large tracking error to the index, so it is better viewed as an alternative-data trend strategy than as a cost saving.
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C16-2|MT013-C16-2]] — Standard Life GARS promised equity beta 0.2 with cash plus 5, a top-decile hedge fund result, and was unrealistic though it reached $90 billion.
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C16-3|MT013-C16-3]] — Risk premia backtests overstate Sharpe (G10 carry 1.2); live results drop about 75% from backtest.
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C16-4|MT013-C16-4]] — Backtested to live performance decay of risk premia products is about 75%.
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C16-5|MT013-C16-5]] — A multi-manager mutual fund of hedge fund managers cannot achieve the promised return objectives after costs and constraints.
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C16-X1|MT013-C16-X1]] — Strategies go in and out of favour, so 5-10 years of track record says little about the next 5-10 years; past underperformance does not predict future underperformance.
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C16-X2|MT013-C16-X2]] — Equity long-short funds with 0.4-0.5 equity beta need more stock-selection alpha than is realistic to overcome mutual fund costs, so they are unlikely to add value.
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C19-1|MT013-C19-1]] — QIS trend-replication products do not publish returns, so cannot be evaluated.
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C19-2|MT013-C19-2]] — Replication using different methods may not deliver the CTA outcome in all regimes; the last 10 years differ from the first 10 of this millennium.
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C19-3|MT013-C19-3]] — Due diligence on bank trend indices should ask when launched and how prior similar indices performed, as many allocators suspended belief.
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C19-4|MT013-C19-4]] — Calling trend a risk premium is misleading because different designers' trend indices differ greatly.
- [[MT013 - AI, Risk, and the Future of Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.387#^MT013-C20-2|MT013-C20-2]] — Two long-term trend models with correlation 0.8 can still be 20 points apart in a year, so manager risk is high.

## [[MT014 - Adapt or Perish- Why Systematic Investors Must Evolve in 2024 - Systematic Investor 325]]
- [[MT014 - Adapt or Perish- Why Systematic Investors Must Evolve in 2024 - Systematic Investor 325#^MT014-C04-3|MT014-C04-3]] — Sources of manager dispersion include portfolio composition, number of markets (below 100 vs 300-500), model speed and strategy purity.
- [[MT014 - Adapt or Perish- Why Systematic Investors Must Evolve in 2024 - Systematic Investor 325#^MT014-C05-5|MT014-C05-5]] — The range of manager returns in trend following is huge despite its conceptual simplicity.
- [[MT014 - Adapt or Perish- Why Systematic Investors Must Evolve in 2024 - Systematic Investor 325#^MT014-C06-1|MT014-C06-1]] — Medium-term trend means holding periods of two to six months, a very wide definition.
- [[MT014 - Adapt or Perish- Why Systematic Investors Must Evolve in 2024 - Systematic Investor 325#^MT014-C07-2|MT014-C07-2]] — SVB (2023) and August 2024 gave out-of-sample support for the quality long-short result.
- [[MT014 - Adapt or Perish- Why Systematic Investors Must Evolve in 2024 - Systematic Investor 325#^MT014-C12-1|MT014-C12-1]] — Investment bank annual S&P forecasts have poor accuracy, so non-predictive adaptive strategies are preferable.
- [[MT014 - Adapt or Perish- Why Systematic Investors Must Evolve in 2024 - Systematic Investor 325#^MT014-C14-2|MT014-C14-2]] — A simple mean-variance logic using the Sharpe of the traditional portfolio and of trend shows the value-add of trend.

## [[MT015 - Ajay Banga - President of World Bank - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment]]
- [[MT015 - Ajay Banga - President of World Bank - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment#^MT015-C08-1|MT015-C08-1]] — Past failures should be re-tested because assumptions may have changed or execution may have been poor; check whether assumptions are still valid.

## [[MT017 - Are Markets Becoming Living Systems- - Systematic Investor - Ep. 402]]
- [[MT017 - Are Markets Becoming Living Systems- - Systematic Investor - Ep. 402#^MT017-C08-5|MT017-C08-5]] — The Barometer (unchanged since 2007) is not a good trading tool but at month end it describes whether the month was a good or bad trend environment because of its look-back.
- [[MT017 - Are Markets Becoming Living Systems- - Systematic Investor - Ep. 402#^MT017-C11-3|MT017-C11-3]] — Microstructure research (Bouchaud, Gabaix, Cohen) finds most price movement cannot be linked to news; large moves occur without proportional catalysts.
- [[MT017 - Are Markets Becoming Living Systems- - Systematic Investor - Ep. 402#^MT017-C15-2|MT017-C15-2]] — The multiplier range is 3x to 7x depending on method; the structural fact is that it is much larger than classical finance assumed.
- [[MT017 - Are Markets Becoming Living Systems- - Systematic Investor - Ep. 402#^MT017-C15-4|MT017-C15-4]] — Brennan's 'factors of finance' research examines the statistical fingerprint of feedback across 68 futures markets over 40 years.
- [[MT017 - Are Markets Becoming Living Systems- - Systematic Investor - Ep. 402#^MT017-C15-X1|MT017-C15-X1]] — Caveat: the roughly 5x inelastic-markets flow multiplier comes from a small set of studies and needs further verification, though the direction (a large multiplier) is held with confidence.
- [[MT017 - Are Markets Becoming Living Systems- - Systematic Investor - Ep. 402#^MT017-C16-1|MT017-C16-1]] — Agent-based model: chartist (divergent, reinforcing) agents versus fundamentalist (convergent, balancing) agents with one knob for the chartist share from 0% to 100%.
- [[MT017 - Are Markets Becoming Living Systems- - Systematic Investor - Ep. 402#^MT017-C16-2|MT017-C16-2]] — With zero chartists the simulated market behaves like classical finance (Hurst about 0.54, excess kurtosis -0.2, no five-sigma events, no volatility clustering).
- [[MT017 - Are Markets Becoming Living Systems- - Systematic Investor - Ep. 402#^MT017-C16-4|MT017-C16-4]] — Hurst near 0.5 means no memory, above 0.5 means persistence; kurtosis measures fat tails; these two statistics are used as the feedback fingerprint.
- [[MT017 - Are Markets Becoming Living Systems- - Systematic Investor - Ep. 402#^MT017-C17-1|MT017-C17-1]] — Above the critical threshold the simulated market matches the fingerprint of 68 real futures markets across eight asset classes, 41 years, with no news or fundamentals.
- [[MT017 - Are Markets Becoming Living Systems- - Systematic Investor - Ep. 402#^MT017-C17-2|MT017-C17-2]] — Feedback alone is sufficient to generate the statistical signatures (trending, fat tails, volatility clustering), and is the central mechanism rather than a minor one.

## [[MT019 - Backtesting Can't Predict The Future - Systematic Investor 310]]
- [[MT019 - Backtesting Can't Predict The Future - Systematic Investor 310#^MT019-C04-1|MT019-C04-1]] — Risk-reducing research is acceptable only if it does not change the long-term return profile or cause style drift, otherwise it just lowers expectations.
- [[MT019 - Backtesting Can't Predict The Future - Systematic Investor 310#^MT019-C04-4|MT019-C04-4]] — Monthly industry statistics hide intra-month volatility, so a month with a severe shock can look benign in the record.
- [[MT019 - Backtesting Can't Predict The Future - Systematic Investor 310#^MT019-C05-X1|MT019-C05-X1]] — Third-party indicators and data feeds can silently stop updating (as the Trend Barometer app did), so systematic users such as replicators relying on others' data face data-reliability risk.
- [[MT019 - Backtesting Can't Predict The Future - Systematic Investor 310#^MT019-C06-1|MT019-C06-1]] — Backtests significantly introduce overfitting, with marked differences between backtest and walk-forward/out-of-sample results.
- [[MT019 - Backtesting Can't Predict The Future - Systematic Investor 310#^MT019-C06-2|MT019-C06-2]] — Live trading is on the right-hand side of the chart with no hindsight; backtests give hindsight advantage, as do macro causal narratives given after the event.
- [[MT019 - Backtesting Can't Predict The Future - Systematic Investor 310#^MT019-C06-3|MT019-C06-3]] — Backtests are useful for sensitivity tests across simulated regimes but should never be used to predict future expected returns because they are always overfit.
- [[MT019 - Backtesting Can't Predict The Future - Systematic Investor 310#^MT019-C06-4|MT019-C06-4]] — Selection bias: with ten parameter settings, choosing the best-looking stream uses hindsight selection and biases the forward expectation.
- [[MT019 - Backtesting Can't Predict The Future - Systematic Investor 310#^MT019-C07-1|MT019-C07-1]] — Choosing among alternatives with hindsight introduces statistical bias that cannot occur in live trading and should not be done in backtests.
- [[MT019 - Backtesting Can't Predict The Future - Systematic Investor 310#^MT019-C07-2|MT019-C07-2]] — A backtest overfits to noise and every past event as the full road map is known, whereas the future order of events is unknown.
- [[MT019 - Backtesting Can't Predict The Future - Systematic Investor 310#^MT019-C07-3|MT019-C07-3]] — Use walk-forward out-of-sample testing: develop in-sample (e.g. 1970-2000), then test untouched out of sample (2000-2024) to anticipate live drawdown and performance.
- [[MT019 - Backtesting Can't Predict The Future - Systematic Investor 310#^MT019-C07-4|MT019-C07-4]] — Once data is used for development and robustness testing it is tainted and cannot serve as out-of-sample.
- [[MT019 - Backtesting Can't Predict The Future - Systematic Investor 310#^MT019-C07-5|MT019-C07-5]] — An out-of-sample equity curve is close to live track records of other managers, unlike the glorious backtest.
- [[MT019 - Backtesting Can't Predict The Future - Systematic Investor 310#^MT019-C08-1|MT019-C08-1]] — Trend following depends on regime; in an unfavorable regime expect stagnation and drawdowns, so expect a volatile equity curve, not a straight line.
- [[MT019 - Backtesting Can't Predict The Future - Systematic Investor 310#^MT019-C08-2|MT019-C08-2]] — Out-of-sample drawdown is far larger (35-40%) than the backtest drawdown (5-10%), giving a realistic benchmark for maximum drawdown, CAGR and volatility.
- [[MT019 - Backtesting Can't Predict The Future - Systematic Investor 310#^MT019-C08-4|MT019-C08-4]] — An adaptive workflow periodically re-estimates in-sample and walks forward, and the result should be compared with the static model on the same period.
- [[MT019 - Backtesting Can't Predict The Future - Systematic Investor 310#^MT019-C09-1|MT019-C09-1]] — Compare three curves (backtest 'pink unicorn', out-of-sample, adaptive); the backtest is always the best but unrealistic.
- [[MT019 - Backtesting Can't Predict The Future - Systematic Investor 310#^MT019-C09-2|MT019-C09-2]] — Mean reversion modellers expect no out-of-sample deterioration after finding a pattern, but trend followers cannot, because trends arrive irregularly with favorable and unfavorable regimes.
- [[MT019 - Backtesting Can't Predict The Future - Systematic Investor 310#^MT019-C09-3|MT019-C09-3]] — Social-media backtest results are unlikely to reproduce; big managers use some walk-forward process.
- [[MT019 - Backtesting Can't Predict The Future - Systematic Investor 310#^MT019-C09-4|MT019-C09-4]] — In-sample development needs a lot of data for robustness; multi-market testing over 30 years and 100 markets provides it.
- [[MT019 - Backtesting Can't Predict The Future - Systematic Investor 310#^MT019-C10-1|MT019-C10-1]] — Selecting or changing models after seeing out-of-sample results contaminates the test with statistical bias; people underestimate how sensitive results are to overfitting.
- [[MT019 - Backtesting Can't Predict The Future - Systematic Investor 310#^MT019-C10-2|MT019-C10-2]] — Avoid data-mining packages that overfit and then remove it afterwards (Monte Carlo etc.); never touch out-of-sample data so you do not need to extract overfitting later.

## [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10]]
- [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10#^MT020-C02-1|MT020-C02-1]] — Eckhardt thought mechanizing trading (versus Dennis' 'weighted rules' view) was much harder than it seemed.
- [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10#^MT020-C02-2|MT020-C02-2]] — His approach was to build a theory of trading from the philosophy of science, formalizing pieces rigorously rather than a complete theory.
- [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10#^MT020-C03-1|MT020-C03-1]] — Like ballistics, a trading theory starts oversimplified (few factors) and improves as research adds relevant factors.
- [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10#^MT020-C07-3|MT020-C07-3]] — Summary statistics of signals (e.g. a moving average) throw away structure; keeping the 20-day high and low gives more information (position within the range).
- [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10#^MT020-C08-1|MT020-C08-1]] — Keeping all price-history structure is combinatorially impossible, so research must eliminate structure but keep what is important.
- [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10#^MT020-C08-2|MT020-C08-2]] — Volatility is a moving-average process, so moving averages suit volatility, but price is non-stationary and not an MA process, so moving averages and crossovers on price are counterproductive.
- [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10#^MT020-C08-3|MT020-C08-3]] — Fitting should capture permanent features and ignore transitory ones; some overfitting is inevitable, so ETC uses in-house techniques to estimate and minimize overfit.
- [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10#^MT020-C09-1|MT020-C09-1]] — Underfitting is also bad because it ignores worthwhile market information; some good traders lost all their money from overfitting.
- [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10#^MT020-C09-2|MT020-C09-2]] — The E-score combines many performance measures (a 'Super Value') and discounts systems that earned most profit in a short span (e.g. 2 of 20 years), guarding against false impressions of quality.
- [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10#^MT020-C09-3|MT020-C09-3]] — The Gauntlet is a barrage of tests designed to tear a system apart, showing where it fails, which is as important as knowing why it works, and removes emotion.
- [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10#^MT020-C09-4|MT020-C09-4]] — Systems can have a strong 2-4 month run then go flat, so scientific testing and emotion removal are needed.
- [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10#^MT020-C10-1|MT020-C10-1]] — The Gauntlet also tests the program code and finds subtle glitches that would otherwise be nearly impossible to find.
- [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10#^MT020-C11-4|MT020-C11-4]] — Replicating CTA indices by regression 'sounds like an invitation to really overfit'; futures price changes and trade results are radically non-normal.
- [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10#^MT020-C12-1|MT020-C12-1]] — Use robust, distribution-free statistics because futures returns are heavy tailed (Mandelbrot: possibly no variance); small-sample normal statistics give wrong answers, standard deviation and least-squares correlation are unreliable.
- [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10#^MT020-C12-2|MT020-C12-2]] — Robust statistics need 10-30 times more data, so ETC needs 10-20 years of data and cannot conclude anything from two or three years (that is overfitting).
- [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10#^MT020-C12-X1|MT020-C12-X1]] — Regression-based CTA-index replication products are likely to end badly.
- [[MT020 - Bill's Turtle Trading Journey, Modern Systematic Trading, and more! - Open Interest 10#^MT020-C12-X2|MT020-C12-X2]] — Evidence cited from Mandelbrot's cotton-price work: futures returns are fat-tailed on both sides and jump-prone, possibly a stable Paretian distribution without a defined variance.

## [[MT021 - Bonds Behaving Badly - Systematic Investor 309]]
- [[MT021 - Bonds Behaving Badly - Systematic Investor 309#^MT021-C04-6|MT021-C04-6]] — The host's Trend Barometer at 39 signals neutral to weak performance for trend followers.
- [[MT021 - Bonds Behaving Badly - Systematic Investor 309#^MT021-C09-3|MT021-C09-3]] — The claim that being long gold always helps in equity downturns or inflation spikes is not consistent in the data.
- [[MT021 - Bonds Behaving Badly - Systematic Investor 309#^MT021-C10-3|MT021-C10-3]] — Crisis alpha is gain during sustained market losses lasting weeks to months; a correction that reverses quickly is not a crisis and trend followers are too slow to benefit from it.
- [[MT021 - Bonds Behaving Badly - Systematic Investor 309#^MT021-C12-1|MT021-C12-1]] — Crisis alpha is something trend searches for, not a guaranteed hedge or put option; it appears only when the world moves in a drastically different direction.
- [[MT021 - Bonds Behaving Badly - Systematic Investor 309#^MT021-C13-4|MT021-C13-4]] — The VIX reading of about 65.7 on Aug 5 came from a very short window of option quotes, while the VIX futures were far lower, so the headline number may have overstated stress.
- [[MT021 - Bonds Behaving Badly - Systematic Investor 309#^MT021-C14-3|MT021-C14-3]] — The VIX calculation uses bid-ask quotes rather than only traded prices, which may need to be revisited.
- [[MT021 - Bonds Behaving Badly - Systematic Investor 309#^MT021-C14-4|MT021-C14-4]] — Trend models use price only and size positions by each market's own volatility rather than VIX, unless trading the VIX itself.
- [[MT021 - Bonds Behaving Badly - Systematic Investor 309#^MT021-C15-5|MT021-C15-5]] — AI would not have helped in the August shock; big moves are hard to predict or trade.
- [[MT021 - Bonds Behaving Badly - Systematic Investor 309#^MT021-C15-X6|MT021-C15-X6]] — Short stress episodes such as early August 2024 are useful out-of-sample tests for investors to assess how the strategies they hold or consider actually behave in a shock.

## [[MT022 - Bonds- The Voldemort of Asset Allocation - Systematic Investor 320]]
- [[MT022 - Bonds- The Voldemort of Asset Allocation - Systematic Investor 320#^MT022-C03-2|MT022-C03-2]] — In late 2022 the CTA 5-year returns and macro backdrop (volatile inflation, rising rates) looked ideal, yet performance did not follow, so a macro narrative does not reliably predict strategy returns.
- [[MT022 - Bonds- The Voldemort of Asset Allocation - Systematic Investor 320#^MT022-C05-2|MT022-C05-2]] — Nobody can call which strategies (alternative vs traditional, short vs long term) will work over the next three years, so the industry moved toward solutions and relationships.
- [[MT022 - Bonds- The Voldemort of Asset Allocation - Systematic Investor 320#^MT022-C07-3|MT022-C07-3]] — Fund marketing presents short outperformance as skill, which pulls in investors who are then disappointed, so the space does not grow.
- [[MT022 - Bonds- The Voldemort of Asset Allocation - Systematic Investor 320#^MT022-C08-1|MT022-C08-1]] — Better performance after a system change is more likely random, yet the change-and-it-works narrative resonates with investors.
- [[MT022 - Bonds- The Voldemort of Asset Allocation - Systematic Investor 320#^MT022-C08-X2|MT022-C08-X2]] — In contrast to managers who sell a 'we changed the system and it works' story, Beer keeps his replication process unchanged for eight-plus years and treats that stability as a virtue.
- [[MT022 - Bonds- The Voldemort of Asset Allocation - Systematic Investor 320#^MT022-C11-1|MT022-C11-1]] — Mutual fund and UCITS liquid alts returned about 2% a year over a decade after about 200 bp of fees, with high equity correlation (Wilshire data), a failed experiment.
- [[MT022 - Bonds- The Voldemort of Asset Allocation - Systematic Investor 320#^MT022-C11-2|MT022-C11-2]] — To evaluate a space you must include closed funds, not just today's biggest five, to avoid survivorship bias.
- [[MT022 - Bonds- The Voldemort of Asset Allocation - Systematic Investor 320#^MT022-C11-X7|MT022-C11-X7]] — The poor liquid-alts record was wrongly blamed on the liquid (mutual fund) wrapper, when the real cause was traditional managers pushing high-fee products they did not know how to run.
- [[MT022 - Bonds- The Voldemort of Asset Allocation - Systematic Investor 320#^MT022-C12-1|MT022-C12-1]] — Complexity does not meaningfully improve Sharpe in managed futures, so a simple ETF can work; the ETF wrapper does not hinder trend as it would an activist fund.
- [[MT022 - Bonds- The Voldemort of Asset Allocation - Systematic Investor 320#^MT022-C13-2|MT022-C13-2]] — A Sharpe difference of 0.1-0.2 between a mutual fund and an ETF takes about 10 years to prove, so track records for new products are weak evidence and narratives matter.
- [[MT022 - Bonds- The Voldemort of Asset Allocation - Systematic Investor 320#^MT022-C13-3|MT022-C13-3]] — Morningstar US systematic trend mutual funds correlate 98% with the SG CTA Index, so mutual funds deliver nearly identical performance.
- [[MT022 - Bonds- The Voldemort of Asset Allocation - Systematic Investor 320#^MT022-C14-2|MT022-C14-2]] — Replication bets that the factor positioning of the industry estimated on Monday is accurate enough for the following week, with a structural cost and fee advantage.
- [[MT022 - Bonds- The Voldemort of Asset Allocation - Systematic Investor 320#^MT022-C15-1|MT022-C15-1]] — The replication tracks CTA performance with about 90% correlation on monthly returns.
- [[MT022 - Bonds- The Voldemort of Asset Allocation - Systematic Investor 320#^MT022-C15-2|MT022-C15-2]] — Weekly win rate vs the industry is 53-54%, which compounds to the 60s monthly, 70s quarterly and about 80% yearly, like a poker player's slight edge.
- [[MT022 - Bonds- The Voldemort of Asset Allocation - Systematic Investor 320#^MT022-C15-3|MT022-C15-3]] — Competing replicators combine top-down replication with their own signals (150-day vs 20-day windows, more instruments) because backtests show better results, partly for commercial differentiation.
- [[MT022 - Bonds- The Voldemort of Asset Allocation - Systematic Investor 320#^MT022-C18-5|MT022-C18-5]] — Asset allocation models need judgment, haircuts and parameter constraints, so they contain much narrative and art as well as science.

## [[MT023 - Building A Hedge Fund Allocation - Systematic Investor 305]]
- [[MT023 - Building A Hedge Fund Allocation - Systematic Investor 305#^MT023-C08-4|MT023-C08-4]] — Replicators trading few markets avoided the choppiness and beat the indices they replicate in recent months.
- [[MT023 - Building A Hedge Fund Allocation - Systematic Investor 305#^MT023-C12-1|MT023-C12-1]] — Asness argued that diversified trend followers adding carry to smooth returns lose risk mitigation; the Rho paper tests this.
- [[MT023 - Building A Hedge Fund Allocation - Systematic Investor 305#^MT023-C13-4|MT023-C13-4]] — Long-term allocators think in quarterly horizons, so negative daily skew in slow trend may not matter; it depends on the investor.
- [[MT023 - Building A Hedge Fund Allocation - Systematic Investor 305#^MT023-C14-2|MT023-C14-2]] — Long-horizon investors should judge trend following on 5-20 year returns, not on 5-day protection.
- [[MT023 - Building A Hedge Fund Allocation - Systematic Investor 305#^MT023-C15-1|MT023-C15-1]] — Of about 1,000 hedge funds over 13 years, over half had equity correlation below 0.5; managed futures, commodity and macro were least correlated, all managed futures strategies under 0.5.
- [[MT023 - Building A Hedge Fund Allocation - Systematic Investor 305#^MT023-C15-2|MT023-C15-2]] — 30-40% of multi-strat and relative-value funds had equity correlation of 0.5 or higher, so these popular strategies are not necessarily strong diversifiers.
- [[MT023 - Building A Hedge Fund Allocation - Systematic Investor 305#^MT023-C15-4|MT023-C15-4]] — Clustering on correlation, drawdown resilience and alpha gave four groups: loss mitigation, equity diversifier, equity complement and equity substitute; managed futures fell into the first two.
- [[MT023 - Building A Hedge Fund Allocation - Systematic Investor 305#^MT023-C15-5|MT023-C15-5]] — Every strategy label contains funds in every cluster, so the label does not tell the role and manager-level dispersion matters.
- [[MT023 - Building A Hedge Fund Allocation - Systematic Investor 305#^MT023-C15-X6|MT023-C15-X6]] — In the GIC/JPMorgan study multi-strategy funds showed somewhat higher alpha than managed futures but also carried more equity beta.
- [[MT023 - Building A Hedge Fund Allocation - Systematic Investor 305#^MT023-C15-X7|MT023-C15-X7]] — Across clusters, the majority of hedge funds fell into the more equity-like groups and only about 40% were classified as diversifying funds.
- [[MT023 - Building A Hedge Fund Allocation - Systematic Investor 305#^MT023-C15-X8|MT023-C15-X8]] — The finding that commodity managers were even more represented than managed futures in the loss-mitigation cluster rests on only about 10 commodity funds, so it may reflect sample selection rather than a general property.
- [[MT023 - Building A Hedge Fund Allocation - Systematic Investor 305#^MT023-C16-3|MT023-C16-3]] — Manager selection requires understanding the strategy, because a past pattern (CTAs long bonds) reflects the market and can reverse or chop.
- [[MT023 - Building A Hedge Fund Allocation - Systematic Investor 305#^MT023-C16-5|MT023-C16-5]] — Terminating a manager in drawdown gives up the free ride back to the high-watermark, so the replacement must be better by more than the forgone incentive fee.

## [[MT024 - Building an Inflation-Proof Portfolio - Systematic Investor - Ep.384]]
- [[MT024 - Building an Inflation-Proof Portfolio - Systematic Investor - Ep.384#^MT024-C05-1|MT024-C05-1]] — The ERAS tool defines inflation regimes (high, rising, low, falling) to build an all-weather allocation that is consistent across inflation periods, since bonds are the worry.
- [[MT024 - Building an Inflation-Proof Portfolio - Systematic Investor - Ep.384#^MT024-C07-X1|MT024-C07-X1]] — Inflation-regime studies should use long history (good data back to the 1920s), because the Volcker-to-Yellen era of falling inflation does not represent the full range of regimes.
- [[MT024 - Building an Inflation-Proof Portfolio - Systematic Investor - Ep.384#^MT024-C08-4|MT024-C08-4]] — Commodity trend first appeared in a 100-year portfolio paper by a well-known volatility manager.
- [[MT024 - Building an Inflation-Proof Portfolio - Systematic Investor - Ep.384#^MT024-C09-2|MT024-C09-2]] — Each asset class should be traded slightly differently since physical attributes matter in commodities and supply shocks are irrelevant in fixed income.
- [[MT024 - Building an Inflation-Proof Portfolio - Systematic Investor - Ep.384#^MT024-C11-4|MT024-C11-4]] — Asset-class-specific trend trading beat generic trend run identically across asset classes in fixed income.
- [[MT024 - Building an Inflation-Proof Portfolio - Systematic Investor - Ep.384#^MT024-C12-1|MT024-C12-1]] — Allocators try to time allocations to strategies, which is very hard; a stable CPI+4% building block removes the need to predict inflation.
- [[MT024 - Building an Inflation-Proof Portfolio - Systematic Investor - Ep.384#^MT024-C13-1|MT024-C13-1]] — Rob Carver's blog shows a smile: trend Sharpe is about 0.8 whether the underlying Sharpe is +1 or -1, while spot-only and carry-only Sharpe do not explain trend performance.
- [[MT024 - Building an Inflation-Proof Portfolio - Systematic Investor - Ep.384#^MT024-C13-2|MT024-C13-2]] — Bonds, FX and metals harvest trend well, while equities cannot capture the underlying trend.
- [[MT024 - Building an Inflation-Proof Portfolio - Systematic Investor - Ep.384#^MT024-C13-3|MT024-C13-3]] — Single-market trend Sharpe of about 0.2 times a diversification factor of 3.5 gives a portfolio Sharpe of about 0.7.
- [[MT024 - Building an Inflation-Proof Portfolio - Systematic Investor - Ep.384#^MT024-C14-2|MT024-C14-2]] — Trend performance depends on positive autocorrelation of the underlying price (the mathematics of trend following), not just the presence of trends.
- [[MT024 - Building an Inflation-Proof Portfolio - Systematic Investor - Ep.384#^MT024-C14-3|MT024-C14-3]] — Equities show negative autocorrelation at times and horizons, so generic trend machinery earns less there.
- [[MT024 - Building an Inflation-Proof Portfolio - Systematic Investor - Ep.384#^MT024-C14-4|MT024-C14-4]] — Fixed income shows much higher autocorrelation, so trend harvests it better.
- [[MT024 - Building an Inflation-Proof Portfolio - Systematic Investor - Ep.384#^MT024-C14-X5|MT024-C14-X5]] — Git and Pedel's paper derives mathematically how much each 1% of positive return autocorrelation is worth to a trend follower, linking market autocorrelation directly to expected trend P&L.
- [[MT024 - Building an Inflation-Proof Portfolio - Systematic Investor - Ep.384#^MT024-C15-1|MT024-C15-1]] — Trend should incorporate each market's structural characteristics (physical nature, other players) while keeping trend's convexity, without a separate model per market.
- [[MT024 - Building an Inflation-Proof Portfolio - Systematic Investor - Ep.384#^MT024-C15-2|MT024-C15-2]] — Safi trades 100-150 markets but allocates meaningfully to about 50 at a time, based on characteristics such as carry that make a market trend-friendly.
- [[MT024 - Building an Inflation-Proof Portfolio - Systematic Investor - Ep.384#^MT024-C15-X6|MT024-C15-X6]] — The host challenges that bonds' high autocorrelation may simply reflect 40 years of falling rates; Git agrees autocorrelation varies over time and so allocates to only about 50 of 100-150 markets based on current characteristics.

## [[MT025 - Børge Brende - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management]]
- [[MT025 - Børge Brende - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management#^MT025-C09-5|MT025-C09-5]] — It is unclear whether AI requires trillions of dollars of investment, since good ideas have come from garages.

## [[MT026 - CTA Performance- Less is More- - Systematic Investor 319]]
- [[MT026 - CTA Performance- Less is More- - Systematic Investor 319#^MT026-C03-3|MT026-C03-3]] — One month of results (August 2024 short-term CTAs beating long-term) is not evidence for choosing a trend horizon; backtests need many years and economic cycles to avoid picking up temporary events.
- [[MT026 - CTA Performance- Less is More- - Systematic Investor 319#^MT026-C03-X1|MT026-C03-X1]] — One month of data can only be statistically meaningful for high-frequency strategies, because only they generate enough independent observations in a month; for slow strategies it is noise.
- [[MT026 - CTA Performance- Less is More- - Systematic Investor 319#^MT026-C07-1|MT026-C07-1]] — For slow trend following, test sensitivity by delaying fills by one or two days; if the Sharpe change is above about 0.01 the system is sensitive to delay and needs hourly data or slower trading, otherwise intraday moves and overnight gaps do not matter.
- [[MT026 - CTA Performance- Less is More- - Systematic Investor 319#^MT026-C12-2|MT026-C12-2]] — Exponential weighting avoids sharp jumps in estimated correlation when a spike leaves a fixed window, which matters most for short windows.
- [[MT026 - CTA Performance- Less is More- - Systematic Investor 319#^MT026-C13-2|MT026-C13-2]] — Do not dynamically reallocate capital between strategies or instruments on past performance because with decades of backtest and 10 years live data, differences are rarely statistically significant; simple allocation works as well as sophisticated optimisation which overfits.
- [[MT026 - CTA Performance- Less is More- - Systematic Investor 319#^MT026-C14-1|MT026-C14-1]] — Ranking about 100 instruments by backtested trend performance looks like a clear winner-loser spread, but with error bars all overlap, so selecting instruments or strategies by backtest results is mostly fitting noise.
- [[MT026 - CTA Performance- Less is More- - Systematic Investor 319#^MT026-C14-2|MT026-C14-2]] — Cocoa made no money for trend followers for 15 years then made the most in 18 months, illustrating that an instrument's past record is a poor guide to its future contribution.
- [[MT026 - CTA Performance- Less is More- - Systematic Investor 319#^MT026-C16-2|MT026-C16-2]] — Backtested trend performance rises about four to five times with more markets, implying roughly 16 to 25 independent bets of which only about half a dozen matter at any time.
- [[MT026 - CTA Performance- Less is More- - Systematic Investor 319#^MT026-C16-X2|MT026-C16-X2]] — Yavin's conclusion: a plain-vanilla trend CTA gains little from 300 markets; extra value comes from lower-correlation approaches, though Rob notes the author sells such a different CTA.
- [[MT026 - CTA Performance- Less is More- - Systematic Investor 319#^MT026-C17-1|MT026-C17-1]] — Looking at performance databases, there is no evidence that CTAs trading 300 to 400 markets do better than those trading about 100 or fewer.
- [[MT026 - CTA Performance- Less is More- - Systematic Investor 319#^MT026-C19-1|MT026-C19-1]] — Return-based replication, fitting positions to reproduce an index's returns, is the approach Rob objects to most.
- [[MT026 - CTA Performance- Less is More- - Systematic Investor 319#^MT026-C19-2|MT026-C19-2]] — Testing replication on randomly generated pseudo-CTAs and a pseudo index avoids overfitting to the last 30 years of real trends and reproduces features such as yearly dispersion.
- [[MT026 - CTA Performance- Less is More- - Systematic Investor 319#^MT026-C20-1|MT026-C20-1]] — The paper's saving estimate of 200bp management fee, 110bp performance fee and 275bp unnetted costs looks too high; it implies a ceiling Sharpe near one for replication savings over tracking error.
- [[MT026 - CTA Performance- Less is More- - Systematic Investor 319#^MT026-C21-1|MT026-C21-1]] — Return-based regression needs short windows to capture sign changes in CTA positions, but short windows with many futures regressors make regression infeasible, so the paper uses lasso.
- [[MT026 - CTA Performance- Less is More- - Systematic Investor 319#^MT026-C21-2|MT026-C21-2]] — The replication paper's results appear in-sample; a proper test would refit weekly, hold positions the next week and measure tracking out of sample, which always does worse than in-sample.
- [[MT026 - CTA Performance- Less is More- - Systematic Investor 319#^MT026-C22-2|MT026-C22-2]] — Tracking error is not the right metric for CTA replication because managers do not target a benchmark; live replication products show lower than expected correlation to the benchmark on daily data.
- [[MT026 - CTA Performance- Less is More- - Systematic Investor 319#^MT026-C22-3|MT026-C22-3]] — Return-only backward-looking replication cannot reproduce a CTA index's diversification, so marketing it as benchmark less fees is flawed.
- [[MT026 - CTA Performance- Less is More- - Systematic Investor 319#^MT026-C23-X1|MT026-C23-X1]] — A single CTA manager, even one in the index, has dispersion to the CTA index by construction, so tracking error against the index is not unique to replicators.

## [[MT027 - CTAs vs ETFs- The Showdown That Could Change Everything - Systematic Investor 339]]
- [[MT027 - CTAs vs ETFs- The Showdown That Could Change Everything - Systematic Investor 339#^MT027-C04-X1|MT027-C04-X1]] — Monthly return dispersion among the ~50 largest CTAs in February 2025 ran from about -9% to +3%, large in absolute terms but much smaller than dispersion among long/short equity managers.
- [[MT027 - CTAs vs ETFs- The Showdown That Could Change Everything - Systematic Investor 339#^MT027-C11-1|MT027-C11-1]] — CTAs invest research in regime detection to adjust risk across model sets or add fundamental or carry strategies when long-term trends are weak.
- [[MT027 - CTAs vs ETFs- The Showdown That Could Change Everything - Systematic Investor 339#^MT027-C11-2|MT027-C11-2]] — Omicron news in late 2021 caused a CTA worst day yet began one of the strongest runs (2022), showing regime filters cannot predict shocks and tinkering risks losing the strategy's value.
- [[MT027 - CTAs vs ETFs- The Showdown That Could Change Everything - Systematic Investor 339#^MT027-C11-X1|MT027-C11-X1]] — The SG Trend Indicator was built by picking the trend model whose returns best correlated with actual trend followers over the prior 5 years (a medium-term model); longer models matched less, but over the following decade managers drifted to longer horizons, so a fixed replicating model loses fit as the industry changes.
- [[MT027 - CTAs vs ETFs- The Showdown That Could Change Everything - Systematic Investor 339#^MT027-C12-X2|MT027-C12-X2]] — A benchmark or indicator model should be kept unchanged, because changing it forces either restating history or a structural break that prevents consistent statistical analysis.
- [[MT027 - CTAs vs ETFs- The Showdown That Could Change Everything - Systematic Investor 339#^MT027-C13-1|MT027-C13-1]] — Due diligence test for managers introducing new model features: ask why not earlier and for the best idea of each past period; innovations arrive because something wasn't working.
- [[MT027 - CTAs vs ETFs- The Showdown That Could Change Everything - Systematic Investor 339#^MT027-C13-2|MT027-C13-2]] — An index track record with an unrealistically high Sharpe from adding a QIS timing overlay (doubling Sharpe) usually hides an unavailable signal such as predicting the S&P; if real, one would just trade the market itself.
- [[MT027 - CTAs vs ETFs- The Showdown That Could Change Everything - Systematic Investor 339#^MT027-C13-3|MT027-C13-3]] — Rather than committee-picked time frames, models can recalibrate their lookback systematically; Niels also runs a yearly analysis of the best-performing time frame from a few weeks to about 2.5 years.
- [[MT027 - CTAs vs ETFs- The Showdown That Could Change Everything - Systematic Investor 339#^MT027-C19-3|MT027-C19-3]] — Allocators should wait for 3-5 years of live track record for new products from large firms rather than rush.
- [[MT027 - CTAs vs ETFs- The Showdown That Could Change Everything - Systematic Investor 339#^MT027-C20-X1|MT027-C20-X1]] — Asset-weighted hedge fund indices and AUM data can be dominated by one firm (some about 40% Bridgewater), so industry asset and return figures need checking for concentration before use.

## [[MT028 - CTAs- Innovation Meets Legacy & the BlackRock Effect - Systematic Investor 329]]
- [[MT028 - CTAs- Innovation Meets Legacy & the BlackRock Effect - Systematic Investor 329#^MT028-C07-1|MT028-C07-1]] — The replicator targets pre-trading-cost index returns because fees paid vary across investors, then compares itself to that over the next week.
- [[MT028 - CTAs- Innovation Meets Legacy & the BlackRock Effect - Systematic Investor 329#^MT028-C13-X1|MT028-C13-X1]] — Index return comparisons (e.g. the Bridge Alternatives managed-account index) may exclude implementation or platform fees and need minimums, so their returns overstate what an investor actually gets.
- [[MT028 - CTAs- Innovation Meets Legacy & the BlackRock Effect - Systematic Investor 329#^MT028-C14-1|MT028-C14-1]] — About 25% of SG CTA index managers exclude interest income, so index returns understate those of funds including it, a bias when comparing with replicators.
- [[MT028 - CTAs- Innovation Meets Legacy & the BlackRock Effect - Systematic Investor 329#^MT028-C14-X1|MT028-C14-X1]] — Tom challenges the 'unchanging index' claim: a replicator's principal-component weights shift and it tracks an index whose constituents and their models change yearly, so its implementation evolves much like a CTA's research process.
- [[MT028 - CTAs- Innovation Meets Legacy & the BlackRock Effect - Systematic Investor 329#^MT028-C15-X1|MT028-C15-X1]] — Multi-strategy pod shops with Sharpe ratios around 2 cannot be replicated, because replication works only where returns come from a few liquid factor exposures, as with trend CTAs.
- [[MT028 - CTAs- Innovation Meets Legacy & the BlackRock Effect - Systematic Investor 329#^MT028-C22-X1|MT028-C22-X1]] — Stated investor demand (e.g. everyone wants a top CTA in an Article 9 wrapper) is unreliable; real demand shows only when a concrete investable product is offered.

## [[MT029 - CTAs- The Good, The Bad, & The Misunderstood - Top Traders Unplugged 148]]
- [[MT029 - CTAs- The Good, The Bad, & The Misunderstood - Top Traders Unplugged 148#^MT029-C06-1|MT029-C06-1]] — A single market's trend Sharpe is only about 0.25, so tailoring implementation per asset class to raise it to 0.30 adds real value after diversification.
- [[MT029 - CTAs- The Good, The Bad, & The Misunderstood - Top Traders Unplugged 148#^MT029-C06-2|MT029-C06-2]] — Rob is skeptical that some asset classes trend better than others; no strong statistically significant difference exists and crypto has too short a history, though equity trend looks weaker than VIX or fixed income, possibly due to higher carry in fixed income.
- [[MT029 - CTAs- The Good, The Bad, & The Misunderstood - Top Traders Unplugged 148#^MT029-C08-X1|MT029-C08-X1]] — Rob rejects Yav's case for single-asset-class funds: specialised execution already exists inside a multi-asset CTA, and a generalised optimiser and risk model can handle differing diversification and distributions; he also calls Yav's sustained-imbalance theory of trends unproven.
- [[MT029 - CTAs- The Good, The Bad, & The Misunderstood - Top Traders Unplugged 148#^MT029-C09-1|MT029-C09-1]] — Rob's blog found no correlation between market liquidity and trend performance, though Yav argues the wrong liquidity is measured (the imbalance matters) and liquidity mainly caps diversification and capacity.
- [[MT029 - CTAs- The Good, The Bad, & The Misunderstood - Top Traders Unplugged 148#^MT029-C10-3|MT029-C10-3]] — Futures liquidity has two measures, volume and open interest (e.g. Korean stocks high volume low open interest, interest rate futures the reverse), which matter differently for position building and liquidation.
- [[MT029 - CTAs- The Good, The Bad, & The Misunderstood - Top Traders Unplugged 148#^MT029-C10-X1|MT029-C10-X1]] — Market size is not liquidity: the industry players who dominate a market and its transaction costs shape how it trades and how trend performs there, so a single 'how liquid' measure is misleading.
- [[MT029 - CTAs- The Good, The Bad, & The Misunderstood - Top Traders Unplugged 148#^MT029-C14-X1|MT029-C14-X1]] — Due-diligence test: asking whether a CTA pays its execution desk a performance fee reveals whether it treats execution as an alpha source or as a production line.
- [[MT029 - CTAs- The Good, The Bad, & The Misunderstood - Top Traders Unplugged 148#^MT029-C16-2|MT029-C16-2]] — Large CTAs spend about 35-40% of research effort on execution.
- [[MT029 - CTAs- The Good, The Bad, & The Misunderstood - Top Traders Unplugged 148#^MT029-C16-3|MT029-C16-3]] — Technology tightly integrated with research accelerates and cleans implementation and technologists spot issues researchers miss.
- [[MT029 - CTAs- The Good, The Bad, & The Misunderstood - Top Traders Unplugged 148#^MT029-C17-1|MT029-C17-1]] — Open-sourcing foundations (e.g. Arctic DB) attracts top technologists and improved code quality at AHL.
- [[MT029 - CTAs- The Good, The Bad, & The Misunderstood - Top Traders Unplugged 148#^MT029-C17-2|MT029-C17-2]] — Open-sourcing a trading system forces better code quality, testing and documentation.

## [[MT030 - Can Trend Following Be Saved- - Systematic Investor 348]]
- [[MT030 - Can Trend Following Be Saved- - Systematic Investor 348#^MT030-C08-X1|MT030-C08-X1]] — In the April 2025 reversals, results depended heavily on luck in rebalance timing and positioning just before policy headlines, so dispersion between managers in such periods says little about skill.
- [[MT030 - Can Trend Following Be Saved- - Systematic Investor 348#^MT030-C14-2|MT030-C14-2]] — Smart researchers found no evidence that alternative markets trend more; they mainly provide diversification.
- [[MT030 - Can Trend Following Be Saved- - Systematic Investor 348#^MT030-C14-3|MT030-C14-3]] — Allocators should ask managers to backtest their announced enhancements (more markets, vol controls, short-term models) against the model of 3 and 6 years ago to see if complexity adds value.
- [[MT030 - Can Trend Following Be Saved- - Systematic Investor 348#^MT030-C14-4|MT030-C14-4]] — Model overhauls can go either way: AQR rebuilt its models and is doing very well but from 2014 to 2020 underperformed the other 19 SG CTA constituents by 20%.
- [[MT030 - Can Trend Following Be Saved- - Systematic Investor 348#^MT030-C15-1|MT030-C15-1]] — Managers should be judged on relative performance rather than asked to justify individual model changes, since changes are made believing they are better and hypothetical comparisons are impractical.
- [[MT030 - Can Trend Following Be Saved- - Systematic Investor 348#^MT030-C15-3|MT030-C15-3]] — Replicators also over-promise: a 300-400bp outperformance claim ended as about 50bp after five years.

## [[MT031 - Cem Karsan on the Market’s Quiet Fragility - Systematic Investor - Ep.379]]
- [[MT031 - Cem Karsan on the Market’s Quiet Fragility - Systematic Investor - Ep.379#^MT031-C06-2|MT031-C06-2]] — Dividing max drawdown by annualized vol shows tail risk that Sharpe hides; smooth-looking strategies can have drawdown/vol ratios far larger than expected.
- [[MT031 - Cem Karsan on the Market’s Quiet Fragility - Systematic Investor - Ep.379#^MT031-C06-3|MT031-C06-3]] — Drawdowns are rare so a 5-10 year track record with a good Sharpe can hide large tail risk until it hits.
- [[MT031 - Cem Karsan on the Market’s Quiet Fragility - Systematic Investor - Ep.379#^MT031-C06-4|MT031-C06-4]] — Prefer a risk measure that penalizes bigger losses (Sortino or area under the drawdown curve) over Sharpe, which treats upside and downside vol alike.
- [[MT031 - Cem Karsan on the Market’s Quiet Fragility - Systematic Investor - Ep.379#^MT031-C09-X2|MT031-C09-X2]] — In a regime, the relative distribution of volatility across assets is far more predictable from basic assumptions than price direction, allowing decade-long vol calls.
- [[MT031 - Cem Karsan on the Market’s Quiet Fragility - Systematic Investor - Ep.379#^MT031-C11-2|MT031-C11-2]] — Base views on measurable structural drivers (generation, inequality, political views) to get conviction.
- [[MT031 - Cem Karsan on the Market’s Quiet Fragility - Systematic Investor - Ep.379#^MT031-C16-4|MT031-C16-4]] — Theory-first, data-second (not data mining) is the speaker's argument for the election-year findings.

## [[MT032 - China Open-Source, Compute Arms Race, Reordering Global Trade - BG2 w- Bill Gurley and Bra]]
- [[MT032 - China Open-Source, Compute Arms Race, Reordering Global Trade - BG2 w- Bill Gurley and Bra#^MT032-C03-2|MT032-C03-2]] — Reasoning models coupled with tools perform better than benchmarks show because benchmarks do not allow tool use.

## [[MT034 - Christine Lagarde - President of the European Central Bank - Podcast - In Good Company]]
- [[MT034 - Christine Lagarde - President of the European Central Bank - Podcast - In Good Company#^MT034-C07-X1|MT034-C07-X1]] — In 2022 one ECB dissenter warned that inflation was being underestimated but was not heeded, illustrating how group consensus and forward guidance delayed the policy response.

## [[MT035 - Coatue’s Laffont Brothers. AI, Public & VC Mkts, Macro, US Debt, Crypto, IPO's, & more - B]]
- [[MT035 - Coatue’s Laffont Brothers. AI, Public & VC Mkts, Macro, US Debt, Crypto, IPO's, & more - B#^MT035-C06-1|MT035-C06-1]] — Joining alternative data sets (100M credit card receipts per day plus email receipts) is where the value arises.
- [[MT035 - Coatue’s Laffont Brothers. AI, Public & VC Mkts, Macro, US Debt, Crypto, IPO's, & more - B#^MT035-C07-3|MT035-C07-3]] — Key question: whether GPU allocation share predicts future cloud revenue share (estimates +/-5-6% noisy).

## [[MT036 - Commodity Super Cycle Inbound! - Systematic Investor 294]]
- [[MT036 - Commodity Super Cycle Inbound! - Systematic Investor 294#^MT036-C01-3|MT036-C01-3]] — 0DTE backtests rest on only two or three years of data, and interpolating the surface before then is unreliable because the dynamics were different, so conclusions are weak.
- [[MT036 - Commodity Super Cycle Inbound! - Systematic Investor 294#^MT036-C04-3|MT036-C04-3]] — The COT managed-money category misses other funds' buying (it is blurred), so it is an incomplete measure of who is long.
- [[MT036 - Commodity Super Cycle Inbound! - Systematic Investor 294#^MT036-C05-3|MT036-C05-3]] — The same option trades that work in VIX also work in the storage-constrained commodities, found by transferring structures across analogous markets in a backtest.
- [[MT036 - Commodity Super Cycle Inbound! - Systematic Investor 294#^MT036-C08-X2|MT036-C08-X2]] — Host challenge: switching from value-style warehousing to trend following implies regime timing risk, and every option structure has a cost somewhere (Harry agrees there is always a cost).
- [[MT036 - Commodity Super Cycle Inbound! - Systematic Investor 294#^MT036-C09-4|MT036-C09-4]] — The same structures work across nearly all storable commodities, giving cross-sectional stability.
- [[MT036 - Commodity Super Cycle Inbound! - Systematic Investor 294#^MT036-C12-1|MT036-C12-1]] — The research process is: unconditional backtests across markets, check stability, identify the reason for the mispricing, then find regimes that favour the structure.

## [[MT037 - Crafting the Perfect Investment Portfolio in 2025 - Systematic Investor 331]]
- [[MT037 - Crafting the Perfect Investment Portfolio in 2025 - Systematic Investor 331#^MT037-C05-2|MT037-C05-2]] — Large dispersion between trend managers' returns is an argument that trend following is not a single beta.
- [[MT037 - Crafting the Perfect Investment Portfolio in 2025 - Systematic Investor 331#^MT037-C13-1|MT037-C13-1]] — Mean-variance theory says everyone holds one tangency portfolio levered to risk appetite, but real allocations differ due to beliefs about returns and constraints.
- [[MT037 - Crafting the Perfect Investment Portfolio in 2025 - Systematic Investor 331#^MT037-C14-2|MT037-C14-2]] — The AQR paper uses ten building blocks (equities, bonds, credit; commodities, diversifying long/short, risk mitigation via trend following; private equity, real assets, private credit) plus cash.
- [[MT037 - Crafting the Perfect Investment Portfolio in 2025 - Systematic Investor 331#^MT037-C14-3|MT037-C14-3]] — The paper proxies private equity with 1.2x Russell 2000 blended with a private equity index, giving 20% volatility versus about 15% for public equities.
- [[MT037 - Crafting the Perfect Investment Portfolio in 2025 - Systematic Investor 331#^MT037-C14-4|MT037-C14-4]] — Assumed Sharpe ratios: alternatives 0.3, commodities 0.24, trend (risk mitigation) 0.24, diversifying long/short 0.36; the trend assumption looks conservative.
- [[MT037 - Crafting the Perfect Investment Portfolio in 2025 - Systematic Investor 331#^MT037-C14-5|MT037-C14-5]] — Assumed long-term geometric returns: equities 6.9%, private equity 7.5%, risk mitigation 5.4%.
- [[MT037 - Crafting the Perfect Investment Portfolio in 2025 - Systematic Investor 331#^MT037-C14-6|MT037-C14-6]] — With broader building blocks the optimizer gives a more diversified portfolio than 60/40 at the same volatility.
- [[MT037 - Crafting the Perfect Investment Portfolio in 2025 - Systematic Investor 331#^MT037-C15-1|MT037-C15-1]] — At 60/40 volatility with 4% tracking error and higher fees the optimizer gives 11% commodities, 15% long/short, 16% trend following and 12% private equity (~54% alternatives).
- [[MT037 - Crafting the Perfect Investment Portfolio in 2025 - Systematic Investor 331#^MT037-C15-4|MT037-C15-4]] — With reasonable return assumptions alternatives reach 40-50% of the optimal portfolio.
- [[MT037 - Crafting the Perfect Investment Portfolio in 2025 - Systematic Investor 331#^MT037-C17-1|MT037-C17-1]] — The GIC/J.P. Morgan paper found integrating hedge funds into a 60/40 portfolio favours risk-mitigation strategies over steady strategies already captured by 60/40.
- [[MT037 - Crafting the Perfect Investment Portfolio in 2025 - Systematic Investor 331#^MT037-C17-2|MT037-C17-2]] — AQR's mean-variance approach ignores tails and drawdowns, so a robust view favours more risk mitigation than AQR's blend.

## [[MT038 - Crisis Alpha Is Not What Most Investors Think - Systematic Investor - Ep.400]]
- [[MT038 - Crisis Alpha Is Not What Most Investors Think - Systematic Investor - Ep.400#^MT038-C02-1|MT038-C02-1]] — Because researchers have run so many backtests, a pure out-of-sample test is impossible, so full-sample information should be used carefully, without polluting the strategy.
- [[MT038 - Crisis Alpha Is Not What Most Investors Think - Systematic Investor - Ep.400#^MT038-C02-2|MT038-C02-2]] — Extreme full-sample regularities (like a coin showing 20 heads) can support inference about a market structure even without a clean out-of-sample period.
- [[MT038 - Crisis Alpha Is Not What Most Investors Think - Systematic Investor - Ep.400#^MT038-C03-2|MT038-C03-2]] — When a market has a physical or institutional structure (storage, government auctions such as California carbon), full-sample information can legitimately inform strategy design.
- [[MT038 - Crisis Alpha Is Not What Most Investors Think - Systematic Investor - Ep.400#^MT038-C03-3|MT038-C03-3]] — One way to detect a too-good result is a forward-looking test: if the effect is real, using future information should earn more money.
