- [[MT079 - If Trend Following was an Olympic Sport - Systematic Investor 306#^MT079-C05-X1|MT079-C05-X1]] — A trend follower should add a market only if its diversification value exceeds its transaction costs and the opportunity cost of displacing other markets, so market selection is a trade-off rather than an obvious choice.
- [[MT079 - If Trend Following was an Olympic Sport - Systematic Investor 306#^MT079-C06-3|MT079-C06-3]] — When reviewing a market, check how it earned (one trade or many, choppiness) rather than whether it made money.
- [[MT079 - If Trend Following was an Olympic Sport - Systematic Investor 306#^MT079-C06-5|MT079-C06-5]] — Cocoa was unprofitable for trend followers for 15-20 years and then turned extremely profitable in 2023-24, so removing markets for past losses is dangerous.
- [[MT079 - If Trend Following was an Olympic Sport - Systematic Investor 306#^MT079-C06-X1|MT079-C06-X1]] — Because removing a market later is hard to decide well, the screening effort should go into adding markets carefully in the first place.
- [[MT079 - If Trend Following was an Olympic Sport - Systematic Investor 306#^MT079-C07-1|MT079-C07-1]] — An index excluding cocoa (Credit Suisse managed futures index) underperformed CTAs that held cocoa; few markets are dropped except for liquidity.
- [[MT079 - If Trend Following was an Olympic Sport - Systematic Investor 306#^MT079-C07-3|MT079-C07-3]] — Backtests are valuable not for performance or Sharpe (all look good) but for insight into the manager's care in research and treatment of transaction costs and realism.
- [[MT079 - If Trend Following was an Olympic Sport - Systematic Investor 306#^MT079-C08-1|MT079-C08-1]] — Due diligence should ask about backtest assumptions and costs to judge the manager's rigour.
- [[MT079 - If Trend Following was an Olympic Sport - Systematic Investor 306#^MT079-C08-2|MT079-C08-2]] — Anyone can build a good model; what matters is applying it across many markets, building portfolios and a full system.
- [[MT079 - If Trend Following was an Olympic Sport - Systematic Investor 306#^MT079-C08-3|MT079-C08-3]] — Move from backtest (laboratory) to factory: inputs, outputs, timing, costs, and execution must be realistic.
- [[MT079 - If Trend Following was an Olympic Sport - Systematic Investor 306#^MT079-C08-4|MT079-C08-4]] — Investors' Sharpe thresholds conflict (one rejects below 2, another above 1.25), so research process matters more than the numbers.
- [[MT079 - If Trend Following was an Olympic Sport - Systematic Investor 306#^MT079-C09-1|MT079-C09-1]] — Evaluate systematic managers by return generation, portfolio construction and risk management.
- [[MT079 - If Trend Following was an Olympic Sport - Systematic Investor 306#^MT079-C09-2|MT079-C09-2]] — Regression or model-based signals risk parameter instability, so simple rules are preferred.
- [[MT079 - If Trend Following was an Olympic Sport - Systematic Investor 306#^MT079-C11-2|MT079-C11-2]] — GIC/JPMorgan paper classifies hedge fund strategies into loss mitigation, equity diversifiers, equity complements and equity substitutes.
- [[MT079 - If Trend Following was an Olympic Sport - Systematic Investor 306#^MT079-C11-3|MT079-C11-3]] — Classify strategies by equity correlation, drawdown mitigation relative to equities, and alpha after beta.
- [[MT079 - If Trend Following was an Olympic Sport - Systematic Investor 306#^MT079-C13-1|MT079-C13-1]] — Being wedded to one model produces false alarms, e.g. post-GFC QE did not cause rampant inflation; entertain alternatives.
- [[MT079 - If Trend Following was an Olympic Sport - Systematic Investor 306#^MT079-C13-3|MT079-C13-3]] — Use prices as the null hypothesis and let prices speak before departing.
- [[MT079 - If Trend Following was an Olympic Sport - Systematic Investor 306#^MT079-C14-2|MT079-C14-2]] — A paper found LLMs add little to time series forecasting, so model building is harder than expected.
- [[MT079 - If Trend Following was an Olympic Sport - Systematic Investor 306#^MT079-C14-3|MT079-C14-3]] — Machine learning performs well in expansions but poorly in recessions because training data has few recession periods.
- [[MT079 - If Trend Following was an Olympic Sport - Systematic Investor 306#^MT079-C15-2|MT079-C15-2]] — Prices are public and discounted rapidly, so models and new techniques are valuable only if they turn the same public data into private insight others do not have.
- [[MT079 - If Trend Following was an Olympic Sport - Systematic Investor 306#^MT079-C15-3|MT079-C15-3]] — Adopting new techniques should be gradual, not a binary switch from old to new models.

## [[MT080 - Index vs. Mechanical Replication in Trend Following - Systematic Investor 337]]
- [[MT080 - Index vs. Mechanical Replication in Trend Following - Systematic Investor 337#^MT080-C05-3|MT080-C05-3]] — Co-movement differs from PCA: it explicitly adjusts for asset-class beta, though the two are related.
- [[MT080 - Index vs. Mechanical Replication in Trend Following - Systematic Investor 337#^MT080-C06-1|MT080-C06-1]] — The best trend window is idiosyncratic each year, so diversify signals across time horizons and methods to smooth capture of trend.
- [[MT080 - Index vs. Mechanical Replication in Trend Following - Systematic Investor 337#^MT080-C10-1|MT080-C10-1]] — Instead of expected returns, estimate a Sharpe ratio (e.g. 0.4-0.5) and multiply by the risk allocated (vol exposure) to get a long-term return estimate for trend following.
- [[MT080 - Index vs. Mechanical Replication in Trend Following - Systematic Investor 337#^MT080-C10-2|MT080-C10-2]] — Benchmarks starting in 2000 are arbitrary; long track records (50 years) are a better basis for expected Sharpe.
- [[MT080 - Index vs. Mechanical Replication in Trend Following - Systematic Investor 337#^MT080-C11-2|MT080-C11-2]] — Index replication infers index positions (regression-based) while mechanical replication is a simplified individual strategy (e.g. moving average crossovers).
- [[MT080 - Index vs. Mechanical Replication in Trend Following - Systematic Investor 337#^MT080-C11-3|MT080-C11-3]] — Pro of index replication: aggregated industry view with less manager-parameter-specific risk.
- [[MT080 - Index vs. Mechanical Replication in Trend Following - Systematic Investor 337#^MT080-C12-2|MT080-C12-2]] — Regression-based replication suffers misspecification and collinearity, giving chunky exposures less representative of actual positions.
- [[MT080 - Index vs. Mechanical Replication in Trend Following - Systematic Investor 337#^MT080-C12-3|MT080-C12-3]] — Pro of mechanical replication: risk management and direct reaction; con is a single parameter set and manager return dispersion.
- [[MT080 - Index vs. Mechanical Replication in Trend Following - Systematic Investor 337#^MT080-C12-5|MT080-C12-5]] — Combine mechanical and index replication ('informed regression', Bayesian-like) to lean on each when the other fails.
- [[MT080 - Index vs. Mechanical Replication in Trend Following - Systematic Investor 337#^MT080-C13-2|MT080-C13-2]] — The paper evaluates tracking and goodness of fit to the index, not returns.
- [[MT080 - Index vs. Mechanical Replication in Trend Following - Systematic Investor 337#^MT080-C14-1|MT080-C14-1]] — Combining both approaches substantially reduces tracking error over longer horizons, though it stays high.
- [[MT080 - Index vs. Mechanical Replication in Trend Following - Systematic Investor 337#^MT080-C14-X1|MT080-C14-X1]] — Replication methods should be judged on tracking error to the index, not on historical returns, because one can always select ex post the strategy with the best past return.

## [[MT081 - Inside America’s Economic Divide- AI, Tariffs, & The Fed - The Real Eisman Playbook Episod]]
- [[MT081 - Inside America’s Economic Divide- AI, Tariffs, & The Fed - The Real Eisman Playbook Episod#^MT081-C02-X1|MT081-C02-X1]] — The tariff hit to small businesses cannot be measured: the government shutdown halted official data, and even normal releases do not capture it, so the weak leg of the economy is largely invisible in the data.

## [[MT082 - Inside Calculo Capital- Crushing Commodity Markets with Machine Learning - Open Interest 1]]
- [[MT082 - Inside Calculo Capital- Crushing Commodity Markets with Machine Learning - Open Interest 1#^MT082-C06-6|MT082-C06-6]] — The system uses price only, with no weather input and no manual override of signals.
- [[MT082 - Inside Calculo Capital- Crushing Commodity Markets with Machine Learning - Open Interest 1#^MT082-C07-3|MT082-C07-3]] — Each day the open trade's candlestick and momentum features are matched to a database of historical trades with known T+1 and T+2 outcomes, then classified as keep or eliminate.
- [[MT082 - Inside Calculo Capital- Crushing Commodity Markets with Machine Learning - Open Interest 1#^MT082-C08-2|MT082-C08-2]] — Machine learning is not used for entry because predicting where the market moves is a guess; it is used only for exits, where it avoids learning noise about direction.
- [[MT082 - Inside Calculo Capital- Crushing Commodity Markets with Machine Learning - Open Interest 1#^MT082-C08-3|MT082-C08-3]] — Using ML only as a risk-off element avoids the overfitting problem of price prediction models.
- [[MT082 - Inside Calculo Capital- Crushing Commodity Markets with Machine Learning - Open Interest 1#^MT082-C08-X1|MT082-C08-X1]] — Machine learning applied to market prediction risks fitting noise, and Seibert jokes that a clever machine would probably just rediscover buying breakouts, i.e. become a trend follower.
- [[MT082 - Inside Calculo Capital- Crushing Commodity Markets with Machine Learning - Open Interest 1#^MT082-C09-X1|MT082-C09-X1]] — Calculo does not use forward-curve information (contango, backwardation, seasonality) as a signal and does not trade calendar spreads; only outright futures on price patterns.
- [[MT082 - Inside Calculo Capital- Crushing Commodity Markets with Machine Learning - Open Interest 1#^MT082-C10-4|MT082-C10-4]] — Smart-roll commodity ETFs have not outperformed the main total return indices.

## [[MT083 - Inside Q1’s Chaos - Systematic Investor - Ep.394]]
- [[MT083 - Inside Q1’s Chaos - Systematic Investor - Ep.394#^MT083-C04-3|MT083-C04-3]] — Large CTAs are not pure trend any more; carry and value sub-strategies may explain a good March, so trend should be compared like for like.
- [[MT083 - Inside Q1’s Chaos - Systematic Investor - Ep.394#^MT083-C04-4|MT083-C04-4]] — Below 70 to 80% trend content, a manager has other return drivers, so a dedicated trend mandate should be judged for how its non-trend part behaves.
- [[MT083 - Inside Q1’s Chaos - Systematic Investor - Ep.394#^MT083-C04-5|MT083-C04-5]] — Trend following should be a divergent strategy, continuing to bet on what is happening, as opposed to convergent strategies.
- [[MT083 - Inside Q1’s Chaos - Systematic Investor - Ep.394#^MT083-C04-X1|MT083-C04-X1]] — Dispersion between trend managers rises in months with very large moves because small design choices (weights, speed, markets) get amplified; the largest managers stay in a narrow band while mid-sized ones diverge, so one month's ranking says little about skill.
- [[MT083 - Inside Q1’s Chaos - Systematic Investor - Ep.394#^MT083-C05-2|MT083-C05-2]] — Manager performance differences come from speed, market set and trend vs non-trend share.
- [[MT083 - Inside Q1’s Chaos - Systematic Investor - Ep.394#^MT083-C06-4|MT083-C06-4]] — The paper builds portfolios with maximum diversification so no market dominates.
- [[MT083 - Inside Q1’s Chaos - Systematic Investor - Ep.394#^MT083-C08-1|MT083-C08-1]] — About 60 equity factors come from roughly 15 style factors times four regions, since regional versions are imperfectly correlated and count as separate markets (about 900 markets overall).
- [[MT083 - Inside Q1’s Chaos - Systematic Investor - Ep.394#^MT083-C08-2|MT083-C08-2]] — Number of markets is a poor measure of diversification: 30 stock indices are highly correlated while 30 commodities are not.
- [[MT083 - Inside Q1’s Chaos - Systematic Investor - Ep.394#^MT083-C08-3|MT083-C08-3]] — Optimal market mix depends on the objective: max standalone Sharpe vs a defensive crisis-alpha add-on.
- [[MT083 - Inside Q1’s Chaos - Systematic Investor - Ep.394#^MT083-C10-2|MT083-C10-2]] — Portable alpha replicates the index with swaps or futures and layers a separate alpha on top.
- [[MT083 - Inside Q1’s Chaos - Systematic Investor - Ep.394#^MT083-C10-4|MT083-C10-4]] — Historically, trend on top of beta outperformed with more consistency than a multi-strategy alpha.
- [[MT083 - Inside Q1’s Chaos - Systematic Investor - Ep.394#^MT083-C11-4|MT083-C11-4]] — Some trend styles (liquid markets, certain speeds) may suit portable alpha better; untested.
- [[MT083 - Inside Q1’s Chaos - Systematic Investor - Ep.394#^MT083-C14-1|MT083-C14-1]] — Simulation: a 0.6 Sharpe strategy at 15% vol (vs equities about 0.4 Sharpe) still had a 33% drawdown lasting years, so a long drawdown does not show a broken strategy.
- [[MT083 - Inside Q1’s Chaos - Systematic Investor - Ep.394#^MT083-C15-4|MT083-C15-4]] — The probability of a two-standard-deviation drawdown is about 21% after five years but almost 80% over 25 years for a 0.5 Sharpe, 10% vol strategy, so a short clean record is weak evidence.
- [[MT083 - Inside Q1’s Chaos - Systematic Investor - Ep.394#^MT083-C16-3|MT083-C16-3]] — Remove equity and trend exposures from macro managers' returns to measure idiosyncratic alpha.
- [[MT083 - Inside Q1’s Chaos - Systematic Investor - Ep.394#^MT083-C17-1|MT083-C17-1]] — Across macro managers, higher fees are only very weakly related to higher net returns.
- [[MT083 - Inside Q1’s Chaos - Systematic Investor - Ep.394#^MT083-C17-2|MT083-C17-2]] — Survivorship bias makes current manager indices look better; analysis should account for it.
- [[MT083 - Inside Q1’s Chaos - Systematic Investor - Ep.394#^MT083-C17-3|MT083-C17-3]] — Decompose macro managers against equities and trend (and bonds) and judge the alpha above cheap trend and equity exposure.
- [[MT083 - Inside Q1’s Chaos - Systematic Investor - Ep.394#^MT083-C18-X1|MT083-C18-X1]] — Portable alpha research has covered what it is and how to build it, but sizing of cash buffers and structuring around tail co-dependence between the alpha and the beta remain under-researched and are the key open design questions.

## [[MT084 - Inside the Regime-Adaptive Portfolio - Systematic Investor - Ep.377]]
- [[MT084 - Inside the Regime-Adaptive Portfolio - Systematic Investor - Ep.377#^MT084-C06-X1|MT084-C06-X1]] — Caveat on the San Francisco Fed 40-year cross-country tariff study: historical tariff changes were much smaller than the 2025 US tariffs, so its unemployment-then-inflation pattern may understate or misstate today's effects.
- [[MT084 - Inside the Regime-Adaptive Portfolio - Systematic Investor - Ep.377#^MT084-C13-1|MT084-C13-1]] — Total portfolio approaches still start from a reference portfolio embedded in old thinking, limiting flexibility.
- [[MT084 - Inside the Regime-Adaptive Portfolio - Systematic Investor - Ep.377#^MT084-C13-3|MT084-C13-3]] — A JP Morgan AM / GIC study showed standalone hedge fund optimisation picks convergent strategies, while a total portfolio view picks pure diversifiers (macro, trend, commodities).
- [[MT084 - Inside the Regime-Adaptive Portfolio - Systematic Investor - Ep.377#^MT084-C15-5|MT084-C15-5]] — The portfolio does not outperform in every period; 2015-2019 was best for 60/40, so it is built for robustness over the next 10 years rather than any scenario.
- [[MT084 - Inside the Regime-Adaptive Portfolio - Systematic Investor - Ep.377#^MT084-C16-1|MT084-C16-1]] — The approach did better even in the disinflationary period, making it a more robust method for the future.

## [[MT085 - Inside the mind of a legendary investor - Stan Druckenmiller - Highlights - In Good Compan]]
- [[MT085 - Inside the mind of a legendary investor - Stan Druckenmiller - Highlights - In Good Compan#^MT085-C02-4|MT085-C02-4]] — Early in his career he relied on charts and intuition, which became his pattern recognition.

## [[MT086 - Interest Rates & Trend Following - Systematic Investor 318]]
- [[MT086 - Interest Rates & Trend Following - Systematic Investor 318#^MT086-C03-4|MT086-C03-4]] — CTA replicators showed large tracking error against the CTA indices they benchmark in 2024.
- [[MT086 - Interest Rates & Trend Following - Systematic Investor 318#^MT086-C07-X2|MT086-C07-X2]] — Very long trend-following backtests are only credible for single-asset-class signals (e.g. bonds), because extending a full multi-asset futures portfolio back in time requires many assumptions about market infrastructure.
- [[MT086 - Interest Rates & Trend Following - Systematic Investor 318#^MT086-C08-2|MT086-C08-2]] — Testing trend performance conditional on positive stock-bond correlation is hard because the post-1990 sample has few such periods; longer data back to the 1970s is needed.
- [[MT086 - Interest Rates & Trend Following - Systematic Investor 318#^MT086-C11-1|MT086-C11-1]] — Investors should hold managed futures as a reactive strategy rather than try to time which trend will come; big regime changes are only recognised after the fact.
- [[MT086 - Interest Rates & Trend Following - Systematic Investor 318#^MT086-C13-6|MT086-C13-6]] — The limitation of the Quantica study: its data start in 1990, a period of mostly low cash rates, so evidence for long high-rate or high-inflation periods is thin.
- [[MT086 - Interest Rates & Trend Following - Systematic Investor 318#^MT086-C13-X1|MT086-C13-X1]] — Splitting regimes by the level of cash rates (Quantica) and by inflation (Bonds Behaving Badly) are related but distinct conditioning variables, so results from one need not carry over to the other.

## [[MT087 - Investing in the Age of AI, Uncertainty, and Data Overload - Systematic Investor - Ep.352]]
- [[MT087 - Investing in the Age of AI, Uncertainty, and Data Overload - Systematic Investor - Ep.352#^MT087-C01-1|MT087-C01-1]] — New ML/AI data vendors constantly pitch sentiment and alpha extraction; a systematic firm has to judge the value-add of such data and how long it will persist.
- [[MT087 - Investing in the Age of AI, Uncertainty, and Data Overload - Systematic Investor - Ep.352#^MT087-C02-1|MT087-C02-1]] — New data sources need evaluation for alpha and the mechanisms by which that alpha decays over time.
- [[MT087 - Investing in the Age of AI, Uncertainty, and Data Overload - Systematic Investor - Ep.352#^MT087-C02-2|MT087-C02-2]] — LLMs summarise papers well but fabricated references (wrong names and titles), so LLM outputs need verification.
- [[MT087 - Investing in the Age of AI, Uncertainty, and Data Overload - Systematic Investor - Ep.352#^MT087-C03-1|MT087-C03-1]] — Rejecting some data and technology innovations is as important as adopting the right ones for systematic managers.
- [[MT087 - Investing in the Age of AI, Uncertainty, and Data Overload - Systematic Investor - Ep.352#^MT087-C08-X1|MT087-C08-X1]] — Cutting trend exposure after losses is a form of timing trend that creates path dependency and an ambiguous benchmark (the reduced book or the full-risk book you should have held), which makes the rule hard to evaluate.
- [[MT087 - Investing in the Age of AI, Uncertainty, and Data Overload - Systematic Investor - Ep.352#^MT087-C09-2|MT087-C09-2]] — Dynamic de-leveraging after losses will look good on recent episodes through hindsight, but backtests would show cases where lower exposure to emerging trends hurt; it is a trade-off, not a free lunch.
- [[MT087 - Investing in the Age of AI, Uncertainty, and Data Overload - Systematic Investor - Ep.352#^MT087-C10-3|MT087-C10-3]] — The 12-14 months after 2024 revealed differences in risk budgeting and design among trend followers that look correlated in normal times.
- [[MT087 - Investing in the Age of AI, Uncertainty, and Data Overload - Systematic Investor - Ep.352#^MT087-C17-1|MT087-C17-1]] — Stance on the debate: do not react emotionally to a bad run but stay open to evolving the models.

## [[MT089 - Ken Tropin on How to Run a Successful Macro Hedge Fund by Bilal Hafeez, Macro Hive]]
- [[MT089 - Ken Tropin on How to Run a Successful Macro Hedge Fund by Bilal Hafeez, Macro Hive#^MT089-C02-2|MT089-C02-2]] — Tropin's definition: macro trades equities, FX, fixed income and commodities, directionally or relative value, over short or long horizons, typically directional in orientation.
- [[MT089 - Ken Tropin on How to Run a Successful Macro Hedge Fund by Bilal Hafeez, Macro Hive#^MT089-C05-3|MT089-C05-3]] — Edge in a macro fund: experience across cycles and events, steady conservative management, talent, culture and risk management.
- [[MT089 - Ken Tropin on How to Run a Successful Macro Hedge Fund by Bilal Hafeez, Macro Hive#^MT089-C06-3|MT089-C06-3]] — Strategies need continual innovation, as what worked 10 years ago will not always work.
- [[MT089 - Ken Tropin on How to Run a Successful Macro Hedge Fund by Bilal Hafeez, Macro Hive#^MT089-C10-2|MT089-C10-2]] — Recommended reading: Reminiscences of a Stock Operator and Market Wizards.

## [[MT090 - Ken Tropin on Macro Hive Podcast- Markets, Risk, AI-Driven Technology, and What Comes Next]]
- [[MT090 - Ken Tropin on Macro Hive Podcast- Markets, Risk, AI-Driven Technology, and What Comes Next#^MT090-C02-3|MT090-C02-3]] — Talent strategy: grow young talent (Renaissance model hired mathematicians, not other hedge funds); build larger discretionary teams (about 25 PM teams, some with 15 people); slower but more controllable.

## [[MT091 - Kenneth Rogoff - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management]]
- [[MT091 - Kenneth Rogoff - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management#^MT091-C06-2|MT091-C06-2]] — China's official 5% GDP growth is not credible alongside persistent deflation; true growth is probably around 2-3%, and the figures are manufactured.
- [[MT091 - Kenneth Rogoff - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management#^MT091-C08-1|MT091-C08-1]] — Academic macro models hard-wire the no-inflation assumption inherited from central-bank messaging, which biases research and consensus views.
- [[MT091 - Kenneth Rogoff - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management#^MT091-C08-7|MT091-C08-7]] — Inflation shocks cannot be timed any better than financial crises: one can describe the response after the shock but not forecast the date.
- [[MT091 - Kenneth Rogoff - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management#^MT091-C08-X1|MT091-C08-X1]] — His inflation forecast is a regime-level political-economy call (central banks will not stand firm when a shock arrives), not a point forecast of a specific shock sequence; he does not claim to know the trigger or the date.
- [[MT091 - Kenneth Rogoff - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management#^MT091-C12-4|MT091-C12-4]] — Secular stagnation and permanent low-rate views suffered recency bias; longer history showed the low-rate era would end.
- [[MT091 - Kenneth Rogoff - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management#^MT091-C14-3|MT091-C14-3]] — Post-financial-crisis outcomes (unemployment rise and duration, stock and housing declines) matched historical averages in Reinhart-Rogoff, so historical averages are good priors.
- [[MT091 - Kenneth Rogoff - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management#^MT091-C15-6|MT091-C15-6]] — GDP has long been a poor measure of the economy because it misses many things, and AI-driven labor change adds uncertainty about jobs.
- [[MT091 - Kenneth Rogoff - Podcast - In Good Company - Norges Bank Investment Management#^MT091-C16-4|MT091-C16-4]] — Rogoff's contrarian calls: real rates not low forever, China not growing to the moon, dollar's peak passed; he claims these are less fringe now.

## [[MT092 - Kenneth Tropin, Chairman and Founder of Graham, Featured on the Risk and Return Podcast]]
- [[MT092 - Kenneth Tropin, Chairman and Founder of Graham, Featured on the Risk and Return Podcast#^MT092-C08-X1|MT092-C08-X1]] — Track-record data point: last week's S3 ideas were three right, three wrong and three washes with zero overall return, i.e. hit rate of about 50% with no net gain.
- [[MT092 - Kenneth Tropin, Chairman and Founder of Graham, Featured on the Risk and Return Podcast#^MT092-C09-X1|MT092-C09-X1]] — Mapping battleground stocks requires separating active long positions from passive (index) longs, which was hard to classify and clean and was not available before 2021, so long/short ratios depend on data construction.

## [[MT093 - Lehman’s Legacy, ETF Performance & What Investors Still Get Wrong - Systematic Investor -]]
- [[MT093 - Lehman’s Legacy, ETF Performance & What Investors Still Get Wrong - Systematic Investor -#^MT093-C04-2|MT093-C04-2]] — Dawn Capital's paper compares evidence for trend following versus all major hedge fund strategies.
- [[MT093 - Lehman’s Legacy, ETF Performance & What Investors Still Get Wrong - Systematic Investor -#^MT093-C08-3|MT093-C08-3]] — Claims that trend following works better on some instruments than others usually lack statistical significance; checking 250 instruments will find a few significant by chance.
- [[MT093 - Lehman’s Legacy, ETF Performance & What Investors Still Get Wrong - Systematic Investor -#^MT093-C08-X1|MT093-C08-X1]] — Converting a foreign asset's price into home currency before trend following adds the exchange-rate trend to the asset's trend (e.g. a falling pound makes a US ETF look stronger), so the signal becomes a blend of two instruments rather than a clean trend in either.
- [[MT093 - Lehman’s Legacy, ETF Performance & What Investors Still Get Wrong - Systematic Investor -#^MT093-C10-1|MT093-C10-1]] — Investor (cash/dollar-weighted) returns differ from fund (percentage) returns unless the investor buys and holds with no flows.
- [[MT093 - Lehman’s Legacy, ETF Performance & What Investors Still Get Wrong - Systematic Investor -#^MT093-C10-X1|MT093-C10-X1]] — Investor (cash/dollar-weighted) return for a fund can be estimated from its annual reports and net flows in and out, then compared with the headline percentage return; the flow-weighted aggregate is what investors actually earned.
- [[MT093 - Lehman’s Legacy, ETF Performance & What Investors Still Get Wrong - Systematic Investor -#^MT093-C11-4|MT093-C11-4]] — When faced with an anecdote a quant should test whether it is generally true rather than extrapolate.
- [[MT093 - Lehman’s Legacy, ETF Performance & What Investors Still Get Wrong - Systematic Investor -#^MT093-C13-1|MT093-C13-1]] — Manager skill persistence can be tested through return autocorrelation: positive means good managers stay good, negative means mean reversion.
- [[MT093 - Lehman’s Legacy, ETF Performance & What Investors Still Get Wrong - Systematic Investor -#^MT093-C13-2|MT093-C13-2]] — Every strategy (CTAs, equity value) has long underperformance spells that provoke the 'has everything changed' question.
- [[MT093 - Lehman’s Legacy, ETF Performance & What Investors Still Get Wrong - Systematic Investor -#^MT093-C13-X1|MT093-C13-X1]] — Evidence on whether active-manager performance persists (the persistence scorecard debate) is unsettled; a critique of the scorecard analysis does not settle the question either way.
- [[MT093 - Lehman’s Legacy, ETF Performance & What Investors Still Get Wrong - Systematic Investor -#^MT093-C14-1|MT093-C14-1]] — The default allocator approach for blending trend with 60/40 is to use the full history (Rob's backtest runs back to 1970) with equal weight; a backfill trend index can be built by replication using futures prices.
- [[MT093 - Lehman’s Legacy, ETF Performance & What Investors Still Get Wrong - Systematic Investor -#^MT093-C14-2|MT093-C14-2]] — Choosing to use only the last 2-3 years because trend did badly cannot be backtested; it smuggles in future information and would drive trend allocations to near zero.
- [[MT093 - Lehman’s Legacy, ETF Performance & What Investors Still Get Wrong - Systematic Investor -#^MT093-C15-1|MT093-C15-1]] — Persistence and autocorrelation of strategy returns are weak, so there is no reliable conditioning variable to forecast CTA performance over the next 12 months.
- [[MT093 - Lehman’s Legacy, ETF Performance & What Investors Still Get Wrong - Systematic Investor -#^MT093-C15-2|MT093-C15-2]] — Fitting on only 10, 5 or 3 years degrades out-of-sample results, nonlinearly: 50 years is good, 20-40 makes little difference, 2-3 is much worse.
- [[MT093 - Lehman’s Legacy, ETF Performance & What Investors Still Get Wrong - Systematic Investor -#^MT093-C15-3|MT093-C15-3]] — Using 20-40 years of data performs about as well as 50, so the loss only appears with very short samples.
- [[MT093 - Lehman’s Legacy, ETF Performance & What Investors Still Get Wrong - Systematic Investor -#^MT093-C15-X1|MT093-C15-X1]] — An allocation optimised on a very short window (e.g. the last 10 days) is mostly noise and would also cause constant switching and trading costs, showing that out-of-sample quality degrades as the fit window shrinks.
- [[MT093 - Lehman’s Legacy, ETF Performance & What Investors Still Get Wrong - Systematic Investor -#^MT093-C16-2|MT093-C16-2]] — Lower CTA index returns reflect lower index volatility, from over 25% in the 1970s-80s to under 5% now, so returns should be lower.
- [[MT093 - Lehman’s Legacy, ETF Performance & What Investors Still Get Wrong - Systematic Investor -#^MT093-C16-4|MT093-C16-4]] — Even if rolling trend returns degraded, the difference is not statistically significant, and with a low Sharpe strategy it could take another 50 years to know.
- [[MT093 - Lehman’s Legacy, ETF Performance & What Investors Still Get Wrong - Systematic Investor -#^MT093-C16-5|MT093-C16-5]] — Statistics cannot settle whether trend following has stopped working, so belief matters; the evidence on timing in and out of strategies is poor.
- [[MT093 - Lehman’s Legacy, ETF Performance & What Investors Still Get Wrong - Systematic Investor -#^MT093-C16-6|MT093-C16-6]] — The default should be buy and hold of assets, funds, strategies or factors unless one can convincingly show timing adds value.

## [[MT094 - Liberation Day, Tariffs, US v China Open Source, OpenAI, $CRWV, TikTok - BG2]]
- [[MT094 - Liberation Day, Tariffs, US v China Open Source, OpenAI, $CRWV, TikTok - BG2#^MT094-C15-3|MT094-C15-3]] — CoreWeave's income statement is hard to read because capex precedes revenue and depreciation schedules are uncertain.
- [[MT094 - Liberation Day, Tariffs, US v China Open Source, OpenAI, $CRWV, TikTok - BG2#^MT094-C17-2|MT094-C17-2]] — Depreciation should mirror the declining earning power of GPUs; six straight years is probably not the best fit (peers moved from four to six), the answer may lie in between.

## [[MT095 - Liquidity, Leverage, and the End of Safety - Systematic Investor - Ep.369]]
- [[MT095 - Liquidity, Leverage, and the End of Safety - Systematic Investor - Ep.369#^MT095-C08-3|MT095-C08-3]] — More retail (noise trader) activity distorts price signals, so models relying on price must adjust.
- [[MT095 - Liquidity, Leverage, and the End of Safety - Systematic Investor - Ep.369#^MT095-C13-4|MT095-C13-4]] — Market footprint (notional far above cash) not bid-ask cost drives CTA performance, so backtests must include impact.
- [[MT095 - Liquidity, Leverage, and the End of Safety - Systematic Investor - Ep.369#^MT095-C13-X1|MT095-C13-X1]] — Two schools exist on market breadth for trend followers: stay with a core of the most liquid markets, or add many smaller 'alternative' markets for hidden opportunities; the Quantica study tests which has paid.
- [[MT095 - Liquidity, Leverage, and the End of Safety - Systematic Investor - Ep.369#^MT095-C14-1|MT095-C14-1]] — The key research battle for a CTA is lowering market impact while growing AUM; signal identification is largely solved.
- [[MT095 - Liquidity, Leverage, and the End of Safety - Systematic Investor - Ep.369#^MT095-C16-4|MT095-C16-4]] — Trend following isolates each market's price signal and discards fundamentals and cross-market information, which is both its strength and a limitation.
- [[MT095 - Liquidity, Leverage, and the End of Safety - Systematic Investor - Ep.369#^MT095-C17-2|MT095-C17-2]] — Cross-market connections are nonlinear and episodic, requiring lots of data, computing power and machine learning; an edge could exist but is hard to find.
- [[MT095 - Liquidity, Leverage, and the End of Safety - Systematic Investor - Ep.369#^MT095-C17-3|MT095-C17-3]] — Cross-market ideas are not new (cross-sectional momentum, PCA), and relationships change, so the challenge is knowing when connectedness breaks down.
- [[MT095 - Liquidity, Leverage, and the End of Safety - Systematic Investor - Ep.369#^MT095-C18-1|MT095-C18-1]] — Portfolio construction should use causal relationships rather than correlation and integrate prediction with optimization; decision-focused learning optimizes and predicts together instead of sequentially.
- [[MT095 - Liquidity, Leverage, and the End of Safety - Systematic Investor - Ep.369#^MT095-C18-X1|MT095-C18-X1]] — Current CTA practice relies on correlation, hierarchical clustering or PCA across markets and cointegration tests to decide which markets to hold, which treats connectedness only as a diversification input, not a causal or predictive one.

## [[MT096 - Liquidity, Volatility, and a Dangerous Calm in Markets - Systematic Investor - Ep.355]]
- [[MT096 - Liquidity, Volatility, and a Dangerous Calm in Markets - Systematic Investor - Ep.355#^MT096-C06-1|MT096-C06-1]] — The Man paper distinguishes strategies that have decayed (pairs trading since the 2000s, fast trend following since the 70s-80s) from ones with tough but recoverable drawdowns (equity-factor long-short, trend following), arguing trend following is in the second group.
- [[MT096 - Liquidity, Volatility, and a Dangerous Calm in Markets - Systematic Investor - Ep.355#^MT096-C07-2|MT096-C07-2]] — A 0.5 Sharpe, 10% vol, zero-equity-correlation strategy has a nearly 80% probability of experiencing a 20% drawdown over 25 years, so the 2025 trend drawdown is within statistical expectation.
- [[MT096 - Liquidity, Volatility, and a Dangerous Calm in Markets - Systematic Investor - Ep.355#^MT096-C07-4|MT096-C07-4]] — Cutting exposure to trend following (or quant strategies) during drawdowns and waiting for recovery to re-enter loses a lot of return historically, so drawdown-based manager timing does not work.
- [[MT096 - Liquidity, Volatility, and a Dangerous Calm in Markets - Systematic Investor - Ep.355#^MT096-C07-5|MT096-C07-5]] — Even a 0.6 Sharpe, 15% vol strategy that grew money about eightfold over 30 years had a 32% drawdown and spent 9 years underwater, so long drawdowns are normal for good strategies.
- [[MT096 - Liquidity, Volatility, and a Dangerous Calm in Markets - Systematic Investor - Ep.355#^MT096-C08-3|MT096-C08-3]] — Probability of having experienced a two-standard-deviation drawdown is 79% over 25 years but only 21% over 5 years, so short track records understate drawdown risk and long track records are more informative.
- [[MT096 - Liquidity, Volatility, and a Dangerous Calm in Markets - Systematic Investor - Ep.355#^MT096-C09-1|MT096-C09-1]] — About 75% of managers include interest in reported returns; removing interest yields a larger drawdown (27-28%) that has not fully recovered since 2024, so index drawdown depends on whether interest is included.
- [[MT096 - Liquidity, Volatility, and a Dangerous Calm in Markets - Systematic Investor - Ep.355#^MT096-C09-4|MT096-C09-4]] — Strategies with short track records (e.g. last 5 years) look safer simply because they have not yet had time to hit the drawdowns that longer records show.

## [[MT097 - Living With Ed Seykota - Open Interest - Ep.19 BONUS]]
- [[MT097 - Living With Ed Seykota - Open Interest - Ep.19 BONUS#^MT097-C02-1|MT097-C02-1]] — Seykota is a technical discretionary trader who used custom software only to display charts; the Market Wizards impression that he was a systematic computerized trader is wrong.
- [[MT097 - Living With Ed Seykota - Open Interest - Ep.19 BONUS#^MT097-C02-X1|MT097-C02-X1]] — Drew's own systematic practice was to backtest every rule before implementing it and then follow the algorithms strictly, the opposite of Seykota's discretionary chart reading.
- [[MT097 - Living With Ed Seykota - Open Interest - Ep.19 BONUS#^MT097-C04-X1|MT097-C04-X1]] — Discretionary chart trading cannot responsibly be offered to investors because it has no backtest or statistical record to justify it, so Drew kept investor money in tested systems and confined discretionary trading to a disclosed personal account.
- [[MT097 - Living With Ed Seykota - Open Interest - Ep.19 BONUS#^MT097-C09-X1|MT097-C09-X1]] — End-of-day futures data downloads contained a lot of bad prints, so daily data cleaning (spotting bad data and finding correct values) was a necessary task before analysis.

## [[MT098 - Macro Investing - Understanding How And Why w- Cullen Roche (TIP486)]]
- [[MT098 - Macro Investing - Understanding How And Why w- Cullen Roche (TIP486)#^MT098-C03-X1|MT098-C03-X1]] — Because human responses to policy are emotional and unpredictable, economics and investing cannot be tested like chemistry or biology; mechanical models of flows must be treated as only half the answer and controlled-experiment confidence is unavailable.
- [[MT098 - Macro Investing - Understanding How And Why w- Cullen Roche (TIP486)#^MT098-C04-X1|MT098-C04-X1]] — There is no agreed definition of money (Austrians say gold, Keynesians a monetary aggregate such as M2, and economists switch among MZM, M1, M2, M3), so money-supply arguments depend heavily on which measure is chosen.

## [[MT099 - Major Market Moves for Trend Followers - Systematic Investor 308]]
- [[MT099 - Major Market Moves for Trend Followers - Systematic Investor 308#^MT099-C07-3|MT099-C07-3]] — Return dispersion between managers depends on the number of markets traded: managers with few markets are hit and miss on Nikkei/yen exposure.
- [[MT099 - Major Market Moves for Trend Followers - Systematic Investor 308#^MT099-C10-1|MT099-C10-1]] — A short-term manager exits by time-based or vol-based rules, not by trend change, so short-term managers are a different methodology from short-term trend followers.
- [[MT099 - Major Market Moves for Trend Followers - Systematic Investor 308#^MT099-C10-2|MT099-C10-2]] — The SG CTA Trend index behaves like an exponentially weighted signal with about a 3-month half-life; Quantica showed a roughly 66-day half-life signal correlates highly with the index over 10-15 years.
- [[MT099 - Major Market Moves for Trend Followers - Systematic Investor 308#^MT099-C10-4|MT099-C10-4]] — Look-backs are not necessarily symmetrical for entry and exit: a slow entry (e.g. three months to build) does not imply slow exits, and managers can design faster reaction in a medium-term system.
- [[MT099 - Major Market Moves for Trend Followers - Systematic Investor 308#^MT099-C11-2|MT099-C11-2]] — Many CTAs are no longer pure trend, which explains performance divergences; Baltas adds mean-reversion and carry components that helped (e.g. quickly going long Japan after losses).

## [[MT101 - Market Narratives and the Unpredictable Alpha - Systematic Investor 322]]
- [[MT101 - Market Narratives and the Unpredictable Alpha - Systematic Investor 322#^MT101-C01-X1|MT101-C01-X1]] — A single equity-risk-premium chart should not be trusted alone; judge valuation from an ensemble of measures, which disagree in level but here all point the same way (ERP declining).
- [[MT101 - Market Narratives and the Unpredictable Alpha - Systematic Investor 322#^MT101-C02-1|MT101-C02-1]] — The simple inverse-P/E equity risk premium ignores earnings growth, so growth-adjusted ERP measures differ in level though similar in direction.
- [[MT101 - Market Narratives and the Unpredictable Alpha - Systematic Investor 322#^MT101-C03-X3|MT101-C03-X3]] — Part of the VIX decline before the election was mechanical, a result of how the VIX is constructed (its fixed forward window), not only a fall in uncertainty.
- [[MT101 - Market Narratives and the Unpredictable Alpha - Systematic Investor 322#^MT101-C04-X6|MT101-C04-X6]] — In Mark's own research the Bloom news-based policy uncertainty indices have some usefulness but are a mixed, noisy signal because they bounce around a lot.
- [[MT101 - Market Narratives and the Unpredictable Alpha - Systematic Investor 322#^MT101-C08-X11|MT101-C08-X11]] — Classic trend following is market-agnostic and diversified (any trending, tradable symbol, often 50+ markets), so a crypto-only trend program departs from the usual many-market principle.
- [[MT101 - Market Narratives and the Unpredictable Alpha - Systematic Investor 322#^MT101-C10-3|MT101-C10-3]] — A trend follower should look first at prices while treating other information as supplementary.
- [[MT101 - Market Narratives and the Unpredictable Alpha - Systematic Investor 322#^MT101-C12-1|MT101-C12-1]] — Kind learning environments have clear cause-effect feedback; markets are wicked (inconsistent relationships), so experience can mislead (Hogarth).
- [[MT101 - Market Narratives and the Unpredictable Alpha - Systematic Investor 322#^MT101-C12-2|MT101-C12-2]] — Trend following avoids hard causal inference in macro and relies on observed price persistence; machine-learning approaches suffer when learned relationships break down.
- [[MT101 - Market Narratives and the Unpredictable Alpha - Systematic Investor 322#^MT101-C15-X15|MT101-C15-X15]] — News-narrative signals are costly to build (large data and processing effort), so for most managers they are at best an adjunct to price trends rather than a core signal.
- [[MT101 - Market Narratives and the Unpredictable Alpha - Systematic Investor 322#^MT101-C17-2|MT101-C17-2]] — Corey Hoffstein-type replication can mimic the BTOP 50 by bundling strategies, but dispersion among trend followers makes replicating skill hard; interest from swap providers and allocators.

## [[MT102 - Market Patterns Work and Jeffrey Hirsch Explains Why - The Real Eisman Playbook Ep 56]]
- [[MT102 - Market Patterns Work and Jeffrey Hirsch Explains Why - The Real Eisman Playbook Ep 56#^MT102-C04-4|MT102-C04-4]] — Aronson tested 6,200+ black-box systems against a null hypothesis of no predictive power and all failed; the best-six-months strategy tested out of sample since 1987 passed.
- [[MT102 - Market Patterns Work and Jeffrey Hirsch Explains Why - The Real Eisman Playbook Ep 56#^MT102-C08-3|MT102-C08-3]] — Almanac uses five disciplines together: seasonality, fundamentals, technicals, monetary policy and sentiment.
- [[MT102 - Market Patterns Work and Jeffrey Hirsch Explains Why - The Real Eisman Playbook Ep 56#^MT102-C08-X6|MT102-C08-X6]] — Seasonal patterns are average tendencies that events can override and that shift over time, so they must be tracked and re-checked rather than applied mechanically.
- [[MT102 - Market Patterns Work and Jeffrey Hirsch Explains Why - The Real Eisman Playbook Ep 56#^MT102-C08-X7|MT102-C08-X7]] — Keep a written record of losing trades and dead indicators (an 'indicator graveyard') and learn from them.
- [[MT102 - Market Patterns Work and Jeffrey Hirsch Explains Why - The Real Eisman Playbook Ep 56#^MT102-C11-X11|MT102-C11-X11]] — Track record: the 2010 super-boom forecast of Dow 38,820 by 2025 (from about 10,000) was exceeded, with the Dow now around 45,000-46,000; Yale Hirsch's 1977 forecast was Dow 3,420 (6x the 1974 low of 670).

## [[MT103 - Market Predictions, Rates & Inflation, DOGE, CES, AI Compute - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad G]]
- [[MT103 - Market Predictions, Rates & Inflation, DOGE, CES, AI Compute - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad G#^MT103-C02-1|MT103-C02-1]] — Annual process: review errors and omissions, forecast where the world will be next December, journal independently and have each team member do the same before setting starting positions.
- [[MT103 - Market Predictions, Rates & Inflation, DOGE, CES, AI Compute - BG2 w- Bill Gurley & Brad G#^MT103-C22-2|MT103-C22-2]] — Bill considers macro analysis too hard because of too many variables.

## [[MT105 - Markets Aren’t Random- The Drivers Behind Trend Formation - Systematic Investor - Ep.371]]
- [[MT105 - Markets Aren’t Random- The Drivers Behind Trend Formation - Systematic Investor - Ep.371#^MT105-C05-X2|MT105-C05-X2]] — Caveat: trend following's post-GFC difficulty was limited to a few years (Niels recalls roughly 2018-2020; 2014 was strong), so it was not a decade-long failure.
- [[MT105 - Markets Aren’t Random- The Drivers Behind Trend Formation - Systematic Investor - Ep.371#^MT105-C07-3|MT105-C07-3]] — Market events are conditional (each changes probability of the next), so markets are path dependent and non-ergodic; ensemble statistics do not describe an individual wealth path (Ole Peters).
- [[MT105 - Markets Aren’t Random- The Drivers Behind Trend Formation - Systematic Investor - Ep.371#^MT105-C08-3|MT105-C08-3]] — Market memory is better measured with the Hurst exponent and tail properties than Gaussian tools.
- [[MT105 - Markets Aren’t Random- The Drivers Behind Trend Formation - Systematic Investor - Ep.371#^MT105-C09-2|MT105-C09-2]] — Markets resemble weather: deterministic but unpredictable beyond a short horizon, interacting agents with no central governor produce periods of order and sudden large events.
- [[MT105 - Markets Aren’t Random- The Drivers Behind Trend Formation - Systematic Investor - Ep.371#^MT105-C09-X1|MT105-C09-X1]] — Caveat: Rich does not claim markets are exactly fractal; the fractal market hypothesis is only the closest available account of fat tails and power-law behaviour, not a definitive answer.
- [[MT105 - Markets Aren’t Random- The Drivers Behind Trend Formation - Systematic Investor - Ep.371#^MT105-C10-1|MT105-C10-1]] — Build systems like an engineer designs a bridge: robust, resilient, not overfit or over-optimized rather than relying on Gaussian statistical protection.
- [[MT105 - Markets Aren’t Random- The Drivers Behind Trend Formation - Systematic Investor - Ep.371#^MT105-C12-2|MT105-C12-2]] — Trends can be random, a segment of a mean-reverting cycle, or structural with serial correlation; only the structural kind (outliers) persists, so trends should not be treated as visual patterns.
- [[MT105 - Markets Aren’t Random- The Drivers Behind Trend Formation - Systematic Investor - Ep.371#^MT105-C13-X1|MT105-C13-X1]] — Caveat from Niels: mixing mean-reversion-into-trend and breakout systems in a behaviourally orthogonal ensemble makes Rich's approach less 'classic' trend following than what most allocators expect.
- [[MT105 - Markets Aren’t Random- The Drivers Behind Trend Formation - Systematic Investor - Ep.371#^MT105-C15-X1|MT105-C15-X1]] — Niels points out most managers use dynamic (volatility-based) sizing rather than closed-balance sizing, so the two are different design choices, not one right and one wrong; closed-balance sizing also lowers volatility.
- [[MT105 - Markets Aren’t Random- The Drivers Behind Trend Formation - Systematic Investor - Ep.371#^MT105-C16-X1|MT105-C16-X1]] — Niels concedes closed-balance, trade-by-trade sizing is simpler with fewer moving parts but contains discretionary elements (cutback rule), while dynamic sizing is more complex yet proven over 25-30 years of survival.

## [[MT106 - Markets Look Calm… But Something Feels Off... ft. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.39]]
- [[MT106 - Markets Look Calm… But Something Feels Off... ft. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.39#^MT106-C06-3|MT106-C06-3]] — When benchmarking a trader against CTA indices, compare both raw and volatility-adjusted returns, since risk targets differ.
- [[MT106 - Markets Look Calm… But Something Feels Off... ft. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.39#^MT106-C06-4|MT106-C06-4]] — Choose a benchmark denominated in the same currency as the account to remove currency noise.
- [[MT106 - Markets Look Calm… But Something Feels Off... ft. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.39#^MT106-C06-6|MT106-C06-6]] — A multi-signal book (trend plus carry, skew) lags pure trend in a good trend year but protected in the terrible prior year.
- [[MT106 - Markets Look Calm… But Something Feels Off... ft. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.39#^MT106-C07-7|MT106-C07-7]] — Realised slippage and costs were within one or two basis points of the prior year, validating the cost model.
- [[MT106 - Markets Look Calm… But Something Feels Off... ft. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.39#^MT106-C08-3|MT106-C08-3]] — CTAs differ mainly in speed, methodology details and the markets traded, not core method.
- [[MT106 - Markets Look Calm… But Something Feels Off... ft. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.39#^MT106-C09-2|MT106-C09-2]] — Small robust improvements add up: wide diversification, not trading too fast, accurate costs and avoiding overfit.
- [[MT106 - Markets Look Calm… But Something Feels Off... ft. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.39#^MT106-C09-3|MT106-C09-3]] — In CTA due diligence ask about operations and cost estimation, not the source of alpha.
- [[MT106 - Markets Look Calm… But Something Feels Off... ft. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.39#^MT106-C10-1|MT106-C10-1]] — Experienced quants prefer simple methods and know when they suffice.
- [[MT106 - Markets Look Calm… But Something Feels Off... ft. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.39#^MT106-C10-4|MT106-C10-4]] — Most uses of AI/LLMs on financial data go wrong because users do not understand them.
- [[MT106 - Markets Look Calm… But Something Feels Off... ft. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.39#^MT106-C11-2|MT106-C11-2]] — Performance gains come from tedious work: measuring costs and cleaning data to diversify into more markets.
- [[MT106 - Markets Look Calm… But Something Feels Off... ft. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.39#^MT106-C11-5|MT106-C11-5]] — Dunn's long-term performance with 1-2 PhDs beat firms with 50, so team size does not drive results.
- [[MT106 - Markets Look Calm… But Something Feels Off... ft. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.39#^MT106-C11-X1|MT106-C11-X1]] — The number of PhDs in a quant fund's deck is partly a marketing signal, not evidence of CTA quality, because slow trend following gains come from tedious cost and data work rather than headcount of advanced mathematicians.
- [[MT106 - Markets Look Calm… But Something Feels Off... ft. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.39#^MT106-C12-1|MT106-C12-1]] — Most quant staff effort in CTAs goes to risk management and other infrastructure, not signals.
- [[MT106 - Markets Look Calm… But Something Feels Off... ft. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.39#^MT106-C12-2|MT106-C12-2]] — Use identical forecast weights for every instrument to avoid overfitting.
- [[MT106 - Markets Look Calm… But Something Feels Off... ft. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.39#^MT106-C12-X1|MT106-C12-X1]] — What matters in a research team is not headcount: a few people who can handle very large datasets and approach problems differently (simplification, a different angle) can outperform firms with dozens of PhDs.
- [[MT106 - Markets Look Calm… But Something Feels Off... ft. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.39#^MT106-C12-X2|MT106-C12-X2]] — Diversity of thought (for example mixing economists with physicists and mathematicians) improves a research team; hiring only one discipline loses it.
- [[MT106 - Markets Look Calm… But Something Feels Off... ft. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.39#^MT106-C13-1|MT106-C13-1]] — Plan: statistically test whether instrument weights differ, then pool fitting across similar instruments such as US 5 and 10 year.
- [[MT106 - Markets Look Calm… But Something Feels Off... ft. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.39#^MT106-C13-X1|MT106-C13-X1]] — At AHL each asset-class team fitted its own signal weights, giving different weights per asset class; whether that adds value over identical or pooled weights is an open question Carver wants to test statistically.
- [[MT106 - Markets Look Calm… But Something Feels Off... ft. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.39#^MT106-C13-X2|MT106-C13-X2]] — A Man Group paper (recalled from an earlier episode) asked how fitted weights change when the objective function targets crisis performance instead of maximum Sharpe, showing the fitting objective is a design choice.
- [[MT106 - Markets Look Calm… But Something Feels Off... ft. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.39#^MT106-C14-2|MT106-C14-2]] — Allocation is buying risk with cash, so leverage can enlarge the pie instead of slicing a fixed one.
- [[MT106 - Markets Look Calm… But Something Feels Off... ft. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.39#^MT106-C14-X1|MT106-C14-X1]] — Equities have been very hard to beat for 15-20 years, so comparing long-only equities with trend following is the wrong frame; the better question is adding trend following on top of equities without selling them.
- [[MT106 - Markets Look Calm… But Something Feels Off... ft. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.39#^MT106-C17-2|MT106-C17-2]] — Gold's strong stats depend on the sample period; 1990s gold futures lost 53 percent, so test earlier periods.
- [[MT106 - Markets Look Calm… But Something Feels Off... ft. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.39#^MT106-C17-3|MT106-C17-3]] — Use equal forward-looking Sharpe ratios for assets in allocation rather than extrapolating the equity run.
- [[MT106 - Markets Look Calm… But Something Feels Off... ft. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.39#^MT106-C19-1|MT106-C19-1]] — Judge ETF correlation to SG CTA by comparing with correlation of random actual CTAs.
- [[MT106 - Markets Look Calm… But Something Feels Off... ft. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.39#^MT106-C19-X1|MT106-C19-X1]] — Caveat: ten years of managed-futures ETF history (13 funds, some very young) is too short to judge performance differences between CTA, replication and other ETFs statistically.
- [[MT106 - Markets Look Calm… But Something Feels Off... ft. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.39#^MT106-C19-X2|MT106-C19-X2]] — High correlation of a replication ETF to the SG CTA index is by construction and so is not evidence of quality; a non-replicating managed-futures ETF with no such mandate need not track it.
- [[MT106 - Markets Look Calm… But Something Feels Off... ft. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.39#^MT106-C20-2|MT106-C20-2]] — Crisis alpha needs a clear definition (episodes or threshold decline); the study doesn't say.
- [[MT106 - Markets Look Calm… But Something Feels Off... ft. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.39#^MT106-C20-3|MT106-C20-3]] — Ten years has too few crises to compare crisis alpha across funds.
- [[MT106 - Markets Look Calm… But Something Feels Off... ft. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.39#^MT106-C21-1|MT106-C21-1]] — Compare replication to CTAs only after adjusting for vol and interest income.
- [[MT106 - Markets Look Calm… But Something Feels Off... ft. Rob Carver - Systematic Investor - Ep.39#^MT106-C21-X2|MT106-C21-X2]] — Source bias: the authors of the ETF-vs-CTA article (RPM) run real CTA funds, as the host does, so conclusions favouring CTAs over replication need that discount.

## [[MT107 - Mastering Convexity! - Systematic Investor 333]]
- [[MT107 - Mastering Convexity! - Systematic Investor 333#^MT107-C05-X1|MT107-C05-X1]] — Traditional portfolio construction assumes a linear input-output world and so targets the straightest equity curve with the highest Sharpe ratio, which is inconsistent with non-linear markets where payoff shape matters more than smoothness.
- [[MT107 - Mastering Convexity! - Systematic Investor 333#^MT107-C07-1|MT107-C07-1]] — Using history to set expected returns blindsides investors, because the most material events arise from uncertainty absent from the sample.
- [[MT107 - Mastering Convexity! - Systematic Investor 333#^MT107-C08-3|MT107-C08-3]] — Models trained on history collapse in unprecedented environments; a backtest surviving does not ensure future survival.
- [[MT107 - Mastering Convexity! - Systematic Investor 333#^MT107-C10-2|MT107-C10-2]] — Sharpe, standard deviation and VaR fail because crises (1987, 2002, 2008, COVID) come from unforeseen events; risk is not volatility.
- [[MT107 - Mastering Convexity! - Systematic Investor 333#^MT107-C11-1|MT107-C11-1]] — Five flawed assumptions of traditional finance: historical risk, normality, persistent correlations, estimable returns, all volatility bad.
- [[MT107 - Mastering Convexity! - Systematic Investor 333#^MT107-C11-2|MT107-C11-2]] — Taleb: risk is about possible outcomes, not probabilities from the past.
- [[MT107 - Mastering Convexity! - Systematic Investor 333#^MT107-C14-2|MT107-C14-2]] — Trend following should be judged on thousands of trades, not individual outcomes.
- [[MT107 - Mastering Convexity! - Systematic Investor 333#^MT107-C14-X2|MT107-C14-X2]] — The philosophy of trend following is long-horizon compounding with patience; using it for other purposes (as an allocation inside traditional portfolios, or managing outside AUM) adds concerns, which Rich calls the business of trend following as distinct from trend following itself.

## [[MT108 - Mastering Trend Following, Arbitrage, and Carry with J8 Capital - Open Interest 12]]
- [[MT108 - Mastering Trend Following, Arbitrage, and Carry with J8 Capital - Open Interest 12#^MT108-C03-3|MT108-C03-3]] — Decomposition keeps transparency on where returns come from and lets managers refine each risk driver separately.
- [[MT108 - Mastering Trend Following, Arbitrage, and Carry with J8 Capital - Open Interest 12#^MT108-C03-4|MT108-C03-4]] — Each sub-strategy has one degree of freedom and simple parameters, so the overall product is complex but its parts are simple.
- [[MT108 - Mastering Trend Following, Arbitrage, and Carry with J8 Capital - Open Interest 12#^MT108-C05-X1|MT108-C05-X1]] — The exclusion of equity indices applies only to J8's slow trend program; equity index futures can make sense when traded differently (the short-term Redwood system trades them), so the redundancy argument is horizon-specific.
- [[MT108 - Mastering Trend Following, Arbitrage, and Carry with J8 Capital - Open Interest 12#^MT108-C06-1|MT108-C06-1]] — Use futures curve shape as a real-time inventory proxy because inventory data is delayed, revised and imprecise.
- [[MT108 - Mastering Trend Following, Arbitrage, and Carry with J8 Capital - Open Interest 12#^MT108-C06-5|MT108-C06-5]] — Realised volatility is stable while correlations and recent performance are unstable, so no correlation or performance tilts.
- [[MT108 - Mastering Trend Following, Arbitrage, and Carry with J8 Capital - Open Interest 12#^MT108-C09-1|MT108-C09-1]] — Short-term trading is not simply counter-trend; each strategy uses its own horizon context including volatility.

## [[MT109 - Mike Pompeo, Former US Secretary of State & CIA Director, on Geopolitics, Leadership & the]]
- [[MT109 - Mike Pompeo, Former US Secretary of State & CIA Director, on Geopolitics, Leadership & the#^MT109-C03-X1|MT109-C03-X1]] — Caveat on forecasting: outsiders, including self-described experts, cannot reliably know what is happening inside Russian leadership (e.g. what the Wagner mutiny means), so confident predictions about Putin's stability should be discounted.
- [[MT109 - Mike Pompeo, Former US Secretary of State & CIA Director, on Geopolitics, Leadership & the#^MT109-C05-X1|MT109-C05-X1]] — Evidence caveat on deterrence: the only proof offered is that Putin took no further Ukrainian territory during 2017-2021; critics attribute that to luck, and Pompeo himself says he is agnostic whether Russia saw the Trump team as crazy or serious.

## [[MT110 - Most Investors Fear Uncertainty, But Systematic Traders Don’t - Systematic Investor - Ep]]
- [[MT110 - Most Investors Fear Uncertainty, But Systematic Traders Don’t - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT110-C10-3|MT110-C10-3]] — Large BLS revisions make fundamental reactions hard; economic-data trend models face data-reliability risk.
- [[MT110 - Most Investors Fear Uncertainty, But Systematic Traders Don’t - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT110-C11-5|MT110-C11-5]] — As data quality declines optimised weights move toward the price-based system.
- [[MT110 - Most Investors Fear Uncertainty, But Systematic Traders Don’t - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT110-C12-1|MT110-C12-1]] — Study design: measure hedge fund strategy sensitivity to risk regime (VIX level) and risk shocks (VIX change), separate from beta.
- [[MT110 - Most Investors Fear Uncertainty, But Systematic Traders Don’t - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT110-C12-2|MT110-C12-2]] — Null hypothesis: skilled managers' returns are independent of risk regime and shocks.
- [[MT110 - Most Investors Fear Uncertainty, But Systematic Traders Don’t - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT110-C12-X1|MT110-C12-X1]] — An allocator chooses among more than ten hedge fund strategies, so managed futures should be evaluated against the whole strategy universe, not only against other CTAs.
- [[MT110 - Most Investors Fear Uncertainty, But Systematic Traders Don’t - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT110-C15-X3|MT110-C15-X3]] — The host's own firm analysis of alternative strategies versus trend following reached similar conclusions to Mark's VIX-regime study, which is independent supporting evidence.

## [[MT111 - Navigating Market Chaos - Systematic Investor 336]]
- [[MT111 - Navigating Market Chaos - Systematic Investor 336#^MT111-C05-1|MT111-C05-1]] — Design choices (lookback, market set, measurement) create huge dispersion between trend managers, who can be up and down over the same period.
- [[MT111 - Navigating Market Chaos - Systematic Investor 336#^MT111-C05-3|MT111-C05-3]] — Diversification across markets pays off in long-run simulation but over short periods anything can happen, a communication problem with investors.
- [[MT111 - Navigating Market Chaos - Systematic Investor 336#^MT111-C09-2|MT111-C09-2]] — The SG Trend Indicator uses a 20/120-day moving average crossover, chosen as best fit to the index over years, but manager dispersion is huge so one tool cannot represent them.
- [[MT111 - Navigating Market Chaos - Systematic Investor 336#^MT111-C10-2|MT111-C10-2]] — Crossover design choices include fixed windows versus weighting recent observations, which makes the signal more reactive.
- [[MT111 - Navigating Market Chaos - Systematic Investor 336#^MT111-C13-X1|MT111-C13-X1]] — Evidence that trend following protects against bond sell-offs is thin because inflation-driven bond crises are rare (1970s and 2022), unlike the several well-known equity crashes since 2000, so the bond-hedge record needs much longer history to assess.

## [[MT112 - Navigating Uncertainty- Trend Following and Market Distortions - Systematic Investor - Ep]]
- [[MT112 - Navigating Uncertainty- Trend Following and Market Distortions - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT112-C08-2|MT112-C08-2]] — Market states can be described by risk (VIX), dispersion, correlation network, uncertainty indices and financial stress, each affecting trend models differently.
- [[MT112 - Navigating Uncertainty- Trend Following and Market Distortions - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT112-C12-2|MT112-C12-2]] — Trend followers face a tension between longer lookbacks (better in data) and fast reaction for risk control, handled via multiple time frames or other methods.
- [[MT112 - Navigating Uncertainty- Trend Following and Market Distortions - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT112-C16-2|MT112-C16-2]] — Recent machine-learning work on currency trading finds momentum and carry the two most important features across all models.

## [[MT113 - Navigating a New World Order- Macro Strategies for Uncertain Times - Pablo Calderini]]
- [[MT113 - Navigating a New World Order- Macro Strategies for Uncertain Times - Pablo Calderini#^MT113-C02-4|MT113-C02-4]] — Method: compare the forward curve on the first day of each hiking cycle with what the Fed delivered to judge how prepared (fragile) markets were; the less priced, the more fragile.
- [[MT113 - Navigating a New World Order- Macro Strategies for Uncertain Times - Pablo Calderini#^MT113-C04-5|MT113-C04-5]] — The 'stocks for the long run' argument relies on mean reversion and lower long-horizon variance, but variance forecasts forward must also estimate the unknown mean.
- [[MT113 - Navigating a New World Order- Macro Strategies for Uncertain Times - Pablo Calderini#^MT113-C05-5|MT113-C05-5]] — Investors focus too much on Sharpe, returns and standard deviation; skewness and conditional correlation should enter efficient portfolio construction.

## [[MT114 - New CTA Index being revealed! - Systematic Investor 311]]
- [[MT114 - New CTA Index being revealed! - Systematic Investor 311#^MT114-C04-4|MT114-C04-4]] — Replication has two limitations: it misses some trades (Jan-Feb 2023) and is slower at inflection points; 2023 was the only year it underperformed the index.
- [[MT114 - New CTA Index being revealed! - Systematic Investor 311#^MT114-C05-2|MT114-C05-2]] — In Jan-Feb 2023 several CTAs claimed replication only worked in 2019-2022 due to specific macro conditions and would fail; the author rebuts it.
- [[MT114 - New CTA Index being revealed! - Systematic Investor 311#^MT114-C05-3|MT114-C05-3]] — The replication model uses 10 factors: 2y/10y/30y Treasuries, three equity indices, euro, yen, gold and oil, estimated from recent CTA index returns and implemented each Monday.
- [[MT114 - New CTA Index being revealed! - Systematic Investor 311#^MT114-C05-4|MT114-C05-4]] — Replication is a one-week bet that is self-correcting over time if slightly wrong, with simplicity contrasting the increasing complexity of actual funds.
- [[MT114 - New CTA Index being revealed! - Systematic Investor 311#^MT114-C06-1|MT114-C06-1]] — Complexity (many instruments) is claimed to give better diversification and alpha and so justify higher fees, but there is little data behind it.
- [[MT114 - New CTA Index being revealed! - Systematic Investor 311#^MT114-C06-2|MT114-C06-2]] — The replication models have run unchanged since July 2016, with a very high bar for changes, unlike other quant strategies.
- [[MT114 - New CTA Index being revealed! - Systematic Investor 311#^MT114-C07-3|MT114-C07-3]] — Trading hundreds of markets is not shown to be better than trading 60 in the data; 10-15 markets may suffice.
- [[MT114 - New CTA Index being revealed! - Systematic Investor 311#^MT114-C07-X2|MT114-C07-X2]] — The roughly 300bp annual edge of the replicator over the index is a long-run average, not something to expect every year; in the last couple of years the outperformance was smaller, so the right question is how much to expect, not whether replication works.
- [[MT114 - New CTA Index being revealed! - Systematic Investor 311#^MT114-C08-1|MT114-C08-1]] — Rolling replication correlation to the CTA index varies but does not fall to 20%, because the weekly fit is self-correcting; streaks of misses are expected.
- [[MT114 - New CTA Index being revealed! - Systematic Investor 311#^MT114-C08-X1|MT114-C08-X1]] — A very simple 10-instrument trend model is not the ideal way to run a trend-following business, but as a one-week bet on the broad exposures of the CTA space, 10 liquid instruments suffice; so few markets work for replication, not for a real trend system.
- [[MT114 - New CTA Index being revealed! - Systematic Investor 311#^MT114-C09-2|MT114-C09-2]] — Some managers exclude interest income from composite returns, so the SG index may not include much interest while replication products do, mixing apples with apples of a different colour.
- [[MT114 - New CTA Index being revealed! - Systematic Investor 311#^MT114-C10-2|MT114-C10-2]] — A late-2000s bank replicated only funds with Sharpe above 2.5 using a daily-liquidity factor model and ended up holding every mortgage-backed securities fund, showing naive replication fails.
- [[MT114 - New CTA Index being revealed! - Systematic Investor 311#^MT114-C10-3|MT114-C10-3]] — Bottom-up replication (calls on moving-average crossovers per market) does not add value over simple top-down models, though new entrants (KFA, Alpha Simplex ETF) may add some.
- [[MT114 - New CTA Index being revealed! - Systematic Investor 311#^MT114-C11-X1|MT114-C11-X1]] — Given 24 years of SG CTA Index data, at least a 5% allocation to managed futures in every portfolio is a no-brainer, well below the 25-40% some propose, yet allocators do not do it.
- [[MT114 - New CTA Index being revealed! - Systematic Investor 311#^MT114-C13-1|MT114-C13-1]] — The SG CTA Index is the equal-weighted net-fee return of the 20 largest open CTAs since January 2000, reconstituted each January, with daily data and little selection bias.
- [[MT114 - New CTA Index being revealed! - Systematic Investor 311#^MT114-C13-2|MT114-C13-2]] — The SG Trend Index (10 trend-heavy of the 20) is about 1.3 times the CTA index with 98% correlation, with more leverage, higher return and larger drawdowns.
- [[MT114 - New CTA Index being revealed! - Systematic Investor 311#^MT114-C14-1|MT114-C14-1]] — Hedge fund indices serve as long-term track record for capital market assumptions in allocation and for manager benchmarking.
- [[MT114 - New CTA Index being revealed! - Systematic Investor 311#^MT114-C15-1|MT114-C15-1]] — SG launched the CTA Investable Hedge Fund Index based on the replication model, backtested to 2002 and available on Bloomberg.
- [[MT114 - New CTA Index being revealed! - Systematic Investor 311#^MT114-C15-2|MT114-C15-2]] — Index backtests suffer from dial-turning (alternative risk premia were built by quants tuning rebalancing and definitions), so long backtests of new indices need Caveat Emptor; this one rests on eight years of live unchanged model.

## [[MT116 - Nick Baltas on Trend Following in 2026- Signals, Structure & Strategy - Systematic Investo]]
- [[MT116 - Nick Baltas on Trend Following in 2026- Signals, Structure & Strategy - Systematic Investo#^MT116-C03-2|MT116-C03-2]] — Quantifying signal-to-noise in a market, beyond return divided by volatility, is important for avoiding V-shaped reversals, and non-price information may help.
- [[MT116 - Nick Baltas on Trend Following in 2026- Signals, Structure & Strategy - Systematic Investo#^MT116-C06-4|MT116-C06-4]] — A medium-sized universe is best built thoughtfully (e.g. a few major ex-US equity indices, more tenors in rates) instead of many currencies and few equities.
- [[MT116 - Nick Baltas on Trend Following in 2026- Signals, Structure & Strategy - Systematic Investo#^MT116-C07-X1|MT116-C07-X1]] — Reading Baltas's ranking data, small trend universes have mostly been the better performer since about 2016, alternating with very large universes, which flatters replicators that use small universes.
- [[MT116 - Nick Baltas on Trend Following in 2026- Signals, Structure & Strategy - Systematic Investo#^MT116-C08-X2|MT116-C08-X2]] — Ranking trend speeds or universes by annual return is incomplete without the return spread between the top and bottom ranks, since the spread may be too small to matter.
- [[MT116 - Nick Baltas on Trend Following in 2026- Signals, Structure & Strategy - Systematic Investo#^MT116-C10-1|MT116-C10-1]] — Trend program design differences that matter: universe, speed, diversifying non-trend signals, stop-loss and profit-taking, static vs dynamic risk allocation, and volatility targeting leverage.
- [[MT116 - Nick Baltas on Trend Following in 2026- Signals, Structure & Strategy - Systematic Investo#^MT116-C11-2|MT116-C11-2]] — The nonlinear time series momentum paper proposes letting the data (via a neural network) determine how past returns map to positions instead of imposing a sigmoid.
- [[MT116 - Nick Baltas on Trend Following in 2026- Signals, Structure & Strategy - Systematic Investo#^MT116-C13-2|MT116-C13-2]] — Keeping the sign of the signal (long if positive, short if negative) preserves a trend strategy; letting the model flip sign at extremes mixes in a reversion engine that is better run separately for allocation and attribution.
- [[MT116 - Nick Baltas on Trend Following in 2026- Signals, Structure & Strategy - Systematic Investo#^MT116-C13-3|MT116-C13-3]] — The paper uses a neural network to estimate the transfer function per asset class and finds tail flattening and even reversion at extremes.
- [[MT116 - Nick Baltas on Trend Following in 2026- Signals, Structure & Strategy - Systematic Investo#^MT116-C14-1|MT116-C14-1]] — A neural network transfer function agrees with the sigmoid theory, so it is open whether its extra complexity adds value over a parametric sigmoid.
- [[MT116 - Nick Baltas on Trend Following in 2026- Signals, Structure & Strategy - Systematic Investo#^MT116-C14-3|MT116-C14-3]] — The ATP paper derives a trend follower's expected payoff conditional on the cumulative past return over a horizon, with a directional and a timing component.

## [[MT117 - Nick Baltas talks Signal Strength, Trend Following & Portfolio Strategies - Systematic Inv]]
- [[MT117 - Nick Baltas talks Signal Strength, Trend Following & Portfolio Strategies - Systematic Inv#^MT117-C03-1|MT117-C03-1]] — A replication product can only perform if the CTA strategy it copies performs, and replicating faster programs is harder because of style drift and repositioning.
- [[MT117 - Nick Baltas talks Signal Strength, Trend Following & Portfolio Strategies - Systematic Inv#^MT117-C03-2|MT117-C03-2]] — Replication can capture broad macro trends efficiently with a limited market set (10 to 40 markets), though how many are enough is debated.
- [[MT117 - Nick Baltas talks Signal Strength, Trend Following & Portfolio Strategies - Systematic Inv#^MT117-C04-1|MT117-C04-1]] — High daily correlation is not the same as matching performance: replicators can track closely then underperform and later outperform, so lower fees do not guarantee outperformance.
- [[MT117 - Nick Baltas talks Signal Strength, Trend Following & Portfolio Strategies - Systematic Inv#^MT117-C04-X1|MT117-C04-X1]] — A CTA replicator kept pace with the SG CTA index in 2024 even though many CTAs made money in cocoa, a market the replicator does not trade.
- [[MT117 - Nick Baltas talks Signal Strength, Trend Following & Portfolio Strategies - Systematic Inv#^MT117-C05-3|MT117-C05-3]] — A listener asks whether signal direction should always dominate signal strength in portfolio construction, e.g. long a strong-signal asset and slightly short a correlated weak-signal asset to cut volatility.
- [[MT117 - Nick Baltas talks Signal Strength, Trend Following & Portfolio Strategies - Systematic Inv#^MT117-C07-2|MT117-C07-2]] — Scaling positions by signal strength instead of binary already introduces cross-sectional tilts (more contracts in the stronger of two correlated markets) without forcing shorts.
- [[MT117 - Nick Baltas talks Signal Strength, Trend Following & Portfolio Strategies - Systematic Inv#^MT117-C08-X1|MT117-C08-X1]] — Larsen disagrees with 'trend plus': adding non-trend components may cannibalize rather than enhance trend, since he expects pure trend to outperform them over the long run; Baltas also prefers keeping signal direction.
- [[MT117 - Nick Baltas talks Signal Strength, Trend Following & Portfolio Strategies - Systematic Inv#^MT117-C13-1|MT117-C13-1]] — Trend is univariate (an appreciating market continues), whereas momentum is cross-sectional (relative winners keep beating relative losers regardless of sign).

## [[MT118 - Non-Trend Strategies, Trader Flows, and Risk Management at DCM - Open Interest 11]]
- [[MT118 - Non-Trend Strategies, Trader Flows, and Risk Management at DCM - Open Interest 11#^MT118-C03-X2|MT118-C03-X2]] — The term-structure carry-trader anticipation signal, first found in FX, worked across many other asset classes with only risk-system adjustments, which gave confidence that it is a genuine principle and not a narrow data-mined result.
- [[MT118 - Non-Trend Strategies, Trader Flows, and Risk Management at DCM - Open Interest 11#^MT118-C05-X1|MT118-C05-X1]] — A bad episode (the August 2024 yen carry unwind) is treated as a data point to improve the risk system, with changes made through balanced discussion with the team rather than as a reaction to the last loss.
- [[MT118 - Non-Trend Strategies, Trader Flows, and Risk Management at DCM - Open Interest 11#^MT118-C12-1|MT118-C12-1]] — Machine-learning models fitted to daily data usually recover a noisier version of known signals (risk premium, carry, momentum), so they add little beyond hand-built versions; higher-frequency data is more promising.
- [[MT118 - Non-Trend Strategies, Trader Flows, and Risk Management at DCM - Open Interest 11#^MT118-C12-2|MT118-C12-2]] — Financial data has far fewer observations than the parameters of modern AI models, and signal-to-noise is weak (e.g. an earnings surprise offset by a Fed cut), so models cannot disentangle effects.
- [[MT118 - Non-Trend Strategies, Trader Flows, and Risk Management at DCM - Open Interest 11#^MT118-C13-X2|MT118-C13-X2]] — Because market participants and structure keep changing, the fund treats research as a continuous process and expects its model set to have new components and removed ones over time.

## [[MT119 - Nothing Good Happens Below the 200-Day Moving Average - Systematic Investor 341]]
- [[MT119 - Nothing Good Happens Below the 200-Day Moving Average - Systematic Investor 341#^MT119-C04-1|MT119-C04-1]] — Risk (countable volatility) differs from uncertainty (uncountable, policy-ambiguity), and volatility is backward-looking whereas uncertainty indices measure forward ambiguity.
- [[MT119 - Nothing Good Happens Below the 200-Day Moving Average - Systematic Investor 341#^MT119-C07-2|MT119-C07-2]] — Quants search large databases and adapt/optimise factors, while discretionary traders act as experts using uncountable information; with high uncertainty, systematic links may break.
- [[MT119 - Nothing Good Happens Below the 200-Day Moving Average - Systematic Investor 341#^MT119-C07-X1|MT119-C07-X1]] — Risk premia and factors that work overall perform better or worse by regime, with the business cycle and inflation cycle as the simplest regimes and an uncertainty regime as a further one.
- [[MT119 - Nothing Good Happens Below the 200-Day Moving Average - Systematic Investor 341#^MT119-C08-X1|MT119-C08-X1]] — Research caveat: it is unclear how many high-policy-uncertainty regimes exist in history, so regime-conditional behaviour cannot be well tested or adapted to.
- [[MT119 - Nothing Good Happens Below the 200-Day Moving Average - Systematic Investor 341#^MT119-C13-3|MT119-C13-3]] — A research rubric: price trends first, then fundamental trends, then regime, then valuation extremes and potential mistakes, then signal-to-noise (trend-to-noise ratio), while setting political names aside.
- [[MT119 - Nothing Good Happens Below the 200-Day Moving Average - Systematic Investor 341#^MT119-C14-1|MT119-C14-1]] — Ask one more why until answers are reasonable; trend followers stop at price because the link from fundamentals to price is unknown, and others go deeper to second-order effects.
- [[MT119 - Nothing Good Happens Below the 200-Day Moving Average - Systematic Investor 341#^MT119-C15-2|MT119-C15-2]] — Return-stacking results look strong partly because the S&P 500 has returned about 15% annualised for years, so durability is uncertain.

## [[MT120 - October Market Recap- Missing Data, Strong Equities, and more - Systematic Investor - Ep.3]]
