- [[MT185 - Toby Crabel on Vanishing Edges, AI Limits, and Future of Markets - Top Traders Unplugged -#^MT185-C09-3|MT185-C09-3]] — Over-optimization is self-deception; generalized strategies hold up (some 1990s strategies still run), and the speaker looks for strategies that could last 50 years.
- [[MT185 - Toby Crabel on Vanishing Edges, AI Limits, and Future of Markets - Top Traders Unplugged -#^MT185-C10-2|MT185-C10-2]] — Markets are non-linear and only probabilistic, so AI cannot give a reliable 5-minute forecast and anything can happen; the mood among systematic and discretionary traders is humility.
- [[MT185 - Toby Crabel on Vanishing Edges, AI Limits, and Future of Markets - Top Traders Unplugged -#^MT185-C11-2|MT185-C11-2]] — Short-term strategies are hard to replicate because of nuance and heavy execution investment; trend following products are easier to replicate but the firm's version has done slightly better.
- [[MT185 - Toby Crabel on Vanishing Edges, AI Limits, and Future of Markets - Top Traders Unplugged -#^MT185-C12-1|MT185-C12-1]] — The relationship between volume and price is under-researched; many stick to price alone and miss nuance, and a deep database remains unexplored.
- [[MT185 - Toby Crabel on Vanishing Edges, AI Limits, and Future of Markets - Top Traders Unplugged -#^MT185-C12-X1|MT185-C12-X1]] — Quantifying price behaviour is an endless and slow process: databases are so deep that each answered question raises about 50 more, so research has barely scratched the surface even with AI tools.
- [[MT185 - Toby Crabel on Vanishing Edges, AI Limits, and Future of Markets - Top Traders Unplugged -#^MT185-C12-X2|MT185-C12-X2]] — Volume is hard to use consistently because its scale has changed enormously (15 million NYSE shares a day was huge in the 1970s), so tracking volume per market across eras requires care, one reason the volume-price relation is under-researched.
- [[MT185 - Toby Crabel on Vanishing Edges, AI Limits, and Future of Markets - Top Traders Unplugged -#^MT185-C13-1|MT185-C13-1]] — Technical analysis lacks a philosophical justification; charts (bars and candles) hold dense information through a cognitive element, which is the topic of the speaker's next book.

## [[MT186 - Trading the Fragmented World of 2026 - Systematic Investor - Ep.382]]
- [[MT186 - Trading the Fragmented World of 2026 - Systematic Investor - Ep.382#^MT186-C07-3|MT186-C07-3]] — Combining several lookback windows (ensemble) gives a better average outcome than picking one, and avoids luck in selecting the single best.
- [[MT186 - Trading the Fragmented World of 2026 - Systematic Investor - Ep.382#^MT186-C08-4|MT186-C08-4]] — Backtests describe how a system behaved in the past and should not be used as a map of the future.
- [[MT186 - Trading the Fragmented World of 2026 - Systematic Investor - Ep.382#^MT186-C12-1|MT186-C12-1]] — Macro forecasts should be treated as hypotheses and traded only when the market confirms them (e.g. go short bonds only if bonds actually start to sell off).
- [[MT186 - Trading the Fragmented World of 2026 - Systematic Investor - Ep.382#^MT186-C14-1|MT186-C14-1]] — Choose loosely optimised parameters from a plateau of similar results (e.g. 50 vs 200 day) rather than a precise historical optimum like 47 days.

## [[MT187 - Treasury Markets not as “Safe” as You Think - Systematic Investor - Ep.414]]
- [[MT187 - Treasury Markets not as “Safe” as You Think - Systematic Investor - Ep.414#^MT187-C12-1|MT187-C12-1]] — The LGT paper (Arthur Sepp) classifies trend followers as European (high signal to noise, dynamic position adjustment), American (breakout with digital positions held until exit) and academic time-series momentum (scaled by return).
- [[MT187 - Treasury Markets not as “Safe” as You Think - Systematic Investor - Ep.414#^MT187-C12-X1|MT187-C12-X1]] — The European/American trend-follower labels are historical rather than geographic: the digital-position 'American' style reflects the era before dynamic sizing tools existed, and today many US firms use dynamic sizing and hybrid breakout-plus-dynamic approaches, so due diligence must examine actual sizing and signal rules, not the label.
- [[MT187 - Treasury Markets not as “Safe” as You Think - Systematic Investor - Ep.414#^MT187-C13-2|MT187-C13-2]] — Fewer trend followers are needed to replicate the trend benchmark than managers in other hedge fund styles.
- [[MT187 - Treasury Markets not as “Safe” as You Think - Systematic Investor - Ep.414#^MT187-C15-2|MT187-C15-2]] — Short-term 'trend following' is typically volatility breakout with different exit rules, not true trend following, so results are not comparable with longer-term trend.
- [[MT187 - Treasury Markets not as “Safe” as You Think - Systematic Investor - Ep.414#^MT187-C15-3|MT187-C15-3]] — Market microstructure (tick size, player mix, financing) affects strategy returns as much as model details like breakout length.
- [[MT187 - Treasury Markets not as “Safe” as You Think - Systematic Investor - Ep.414#^MT187-C15-X1|MT187-C15-X1]] — Niederhoffer treated markets as an evolving ecosystem of participants rather than as an efficient pricing machine, so understanding who the players are and how they behave (what is now called market plumbing) is a source of edge.
- [[MT187 - Treasury Markets not as “Safe” as You Think - Systematic Investor - Ep.414#^MT187-C16-2|MT187-C16-2]] — Empiricism: verify with numbers and rigorous backtests; quality of results depends on quality of testing.
- [[MT187 - Treasury Markets not as “Safe” as You Think - Systematic Investor - Ep.414#^MT187-C16-X1|MT187-C16-X1]] — The Niederhoffer versus Taleb contrast frames a core research dilemma: the empiricist trusts data and rigorous backtests while the skeptic argues that history and statistical data can mislead, and Niederhoffer's blowups show the cost of over-trusting the numbers while his comebacks show the value of improving process.

## [[MT188 - Trend ETFs, CTA Replication, and the Fall of 60-40 - Systematic Investor - Ep.376]]
- [[MT188 - Trend ETFs, CTA Replication, and the Fall of 60-40 - Systematic Investor - Ep.376#^MT188-C05-3|MT188-C05-3]] — Replication using fund exposures yields tracking error, so it is closer to an alternative-data CTA than a true tracker.
- [[MT188 - Trend ETFs, CTA Replication, and the Fall of 60-40 - Systematic Investor - Ep.376#^MT188-C05-X1|MT188-C05-X1]] — A third source of divergence from the CTA index is pure timing noise: a fixed weekly rebalance day sometimes gains and sometimes loses a few days of signal drift relative to peers, so some relative performance is luck rather than design.
- [[MT188 - Trend ETFs, CTA Replication, and the Fall of 60-40 - Systematic Investor - Ep.376#^MT188-C06-1|MT188-C06-1]] — The replication design assumed hedge-fund index costs (management, incentive, implementation) of about 200bp conservatively, targeting index plus 400bp, and it delivered that or more.
- [[MT188 - Trend ETFs, CTA Replication, and the Fall of 60-40 - Systematic Investor - Ep.376#^MT188-C06-2|MT188-C06-2]] — If 10 major markets reproduce CTA beta, crisis alpha and zero stock-bond correlation, the CTA signal is a major-market signal and extras (shorter-term models, vol controls, exotic markets) may detract.
- [[MT188 - Trend ETFs, CTA Replication, and the Fall of 60-40 - Systematic Investor - Ep.376#^MT188-C07-3|MT188-C07-3]] — The same 10-factor replication has run weekly (Mondays) since July 2016 without change, now with a 9.5-year track record in different vehicles.
- [[MT188 - Trend ETFs, CTA Replication, and the Fall of 60-40 - Systematic Investor - Ep.376#^MT188-C07-X1|MT188-C07-X1]] — Whether the extras that most CTAs add (short-term models, vol controls, exotic markets) destroy value relative to a 10-market major-market signal is not yet known and will take several more years of live evidence to resolve; the 2024-2025 outperformance is suggestive, not conclusive.
- [[MT188 - Trend ETFs, CTA Replication, and the Fall of 60-40 - Systematic Investor - Ep.376#^MT188-C07-X2|MT188-C07-X2]] — Derivative-based strategy indices can be created overnight and closed or replaced if they fail, so a bank-published index with a backfilled history carries survivorship and selection bias unless the rules were fixed and live before the history was shown.
- [[MT188 - Trend ETFs, CTA Replication, and the Fall of 60-40 - Systematic Investor - Ep.376#^MT188-C08-X1|MT188-C08-X1]] — Allocators are rightly sceptical of hypothetical backtests, so credibility for an index comes from running the identical rules live for years without changes before building a benchmark on them.
- [[MT188 - Trend ETFs, CTA Replication, and the Fall of 60-40 - Systematic Investor - Ep.376#^MT188-C09-X1|MT188-C09-X1]] — Simplify's existing CTA ETF is a different product from the DBMF-style replication (it trades only two of four asset classes and had a very different return profile, up about 24% in 2024), so the same label covers materially different exposures.
- [[MT188 - Trend ETFs, CTA Replication, and the Fall of 60-40 - Systematic Investor - Ep.376#^MT188-C10-2|MT188-C10-2]] — Versus multi-manager Abby Capital, about 350bp better over time: roughly 100bp from performance differential in the ETF, extra volatility, and 150bp not explained by management fees.
- [[MT188 - Trend ETFs, CTA Replication, and the Fall of 60-40 - Systematic Investor - Ep.376#^MT188-C12-2|MT188-C12-2]] — US fund reporting rules allow zero reported transaction costs in some CTA mutual funds, hiding true implementation costs.
- [[MT188 - Trend ETFs, CTA Replication, and the Fall of 60-40 - Systematic Investor - Ep.376#^MT188-C16-1|MT188-C16-1]] — At the Stockholm roundtable CTAs debated short-term vs long-term models, esoteric vs core markets, risk controls, and AI/alternative data (AQR); equally smart people disagreed on the same data.
- [[MT188 - Trend ETFs, CTA Replication, and the Fall of 60-40 - Systematic Investor - Ep.376#^MT188-C16-2|MT188-C16-2]] — Enhancements such as added short-term models are presented with false certainty, backed by selective evidence, while the business is ultimately human judgement.
- [[MT188 - Trend ETFs, CTA Replication, and the Fall of 60-40 - Systematic Investor - Ep.376#^MT188-C16-X1|MT188-C16-X1]] — CTA design choices are highly sensitive to small changes in assumed implementation costs or signal speed, which is why equally smart managers reach opposite conclusions on the same data; the heterogeneity of approaches across the space is therefore a healthy feature.

## [[MT189 - Trend Following Is Becoming Something Bigger Than Crisis Alpha - Systematic Investor - Ep]]
- [[MT189 - Trend Following Is Becoming Something Bigger Than Crisis Alpha - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT189-C06-2|MT189-C06-2]] — UCITS commodity P&L settles with a lag, making daily comparisons with other vehicles unreliable; use weekly data to avoid time-zone and settlement mismatches.
- [[MT189 - Trend Following Is Becoming Something Bigger Than Crisis Alpha - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT189-C10-1|MT189-C10-1]] — A paper on the Turtles' maths suggests risk management, not the entry breakout length, was the key edge of trend followers.
- [[MT189 - Trend Following Is Becoming Something Bigger Than Crisis Alpha - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT189-C11-X1|MT189-C11-X1]] — The investment decision (what strategy to run) should be kept separate from the delivery decision (ETF, UCITS, low-cost, ex-commodity or managed account versions); offering many vehicles of one strategy expands the investor base without engineering the strategy itself.
- [[MT189 - Trend Following Is Becoming Something Bigger Than Crisis Alpha - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT189-C13-1|MT189-C13-1]] — Short-term strategies are highly parameter dependent, i.e. fragile.
- [[MT189 - Trend Following Is Becoming Something Bigger Than Crisis Alpha - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT189-C13-X1|MT189-C13-X1]] — SG classifies a CTA as short-term if its average holding period is about two weeks or ten days or less, versus trend followers who typically hold weeks to months; the non-trend CTA universe has three main groups: short-term, systematic/quant macro (forward-looking fundamental data) and commodity specialists.
- [[MT189 - Trend Following Is Becoming Something Bigger Than Crisis Alpha - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT189-C16-3|MT189-C16-3]] — Roughly half of CTA index contributors report managed-account composites with no cash return, biasing the index downward when rates are high.
- [[MT189 - Trend Following Is Becoming Something Bigger Than Crisis Alpha - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT189-C17-1|MT189-C17-1]] — Index reconstitution drops underperformers late and adds winners only after they are large, so the index captures their weaker large-scale performance and misses their growth phase.
- [[MT189 - Trend Following Is Becoming Something Bigger Than Crisis Alpha - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT189-C18-1|MT189-C18-1]] — Backfilling current index constituents gives much stronger history (survivorship bias), as in Efficient Capital's 'Blinded by Bias'.
- [[MT189 - Trend Following Is Becoming Something Bigger Than Crisis Alpha - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT189-C18-X1|MT189-C18-X1]] — The SG CTA/Trend index is an index of the largest, most sophisticated active managers rather than a passive benchmark, so a manager should not automatically be expected to beat it; the S&P 500 has similar selection bias.
- [[MT189 - Trend Following Is Becoming Something Bigger Than Crisis Alpha - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT189-C18-X2|MT189-C18-X2]] — SG's practice of reconstituting in January without restating history is analytically correct; early hedge fund indices backfilled new constituents' histories, which is a statistical cardinal sin and was driven by competition to promote the industry with flattering base performance.
- [[MT189 - Trend Following Is Becoming Something Bigger Than Crisis Alpha - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT189-C19-1|MT189-C19-1]] — 17 of 20 current SG CTA constituents beat the index over 10 years (index at 15th percentile, Sharpe at 35th percentile), making manager selection look easy and understating single-manager risk; only 9 of 20 from 10 years ago still exist.
- [[MT189 - Trend Following Is Becoming Something Bigger Than Crisis Alpha - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT189-C20-2|MT189-C20-2]] — Beer's research finds short-term models add no Sharpe: they look better on a graph but get whipsawed and overtrade; the trend sub-index has the same Sharpe as the broad index, so non-trend adds little diversification.
- [[MT189 - Trend Following Is Becoming Something Bigger Than Crisis Alpha - Systematic Investor - Ep#^MT189-C20-X2|MT189-C20-X2]] — Non-trend CTAs cannot be analysed as one bucket because they do not share a common risk factor, so their contribution to the broad CTA index is hard to decompose.

## [[MT190 - Trend Following Is Changing Faster Than Ever - Systematic Investor - Ep.409]]
- [[MT190 - Trend Following Is Changing Faster Than Ever - Systematic Investor - Ep.409#^MT190-C11-X1|MT190-C11-X1]] — The CFM tick-size result is driven mainly by cross-asset differences (equities now have a much smaller volatility-adjusted tick than bonds); within each asset class the relationship is more nuanced, so the equity index finding should not be generalised to every market.
- [[MT190 - Trend Following Is Changing Faster Than Ever - Systematic Investor - Ep.409#^MT190-C14-1|MT190-C14-1]] — Thematic ETFs and bank QIS baskets are mostly exposed to industry, country or standard factors and fail to give pure theme exposure; build pure theme baskets yourself.
- [[MT190 - Trend Following Is Changing Faster Than Ever - Systematic Investor - Ep.409#^MT190-C14-2|MT190-C14-2]] — Decide how much extra risk single-stock futures, further contracts or themes add before trying to predict returns.
- [[MT190 - Trend Following Is Changing Faster Than Ever - Systematic Investor - Ep.409#^MT190-C15-2|MT190-C15-2]] — Estimating stock-to-narrative beta is noisy when only about 5% of volatility is narrative-driven, so basket construction has large estimation error.
- [[MT190 - Trend Following Is Changing Faster Than Ever - Systematic Investor - Ep.409#^MT190-C16-X1|MT190-C16-X1]] — Because statistical stock-to-narrative betas are noisy, one bank's QIS theme baskets add a human overlay (for example judging that Coca-Cola benefits from the World Cup even if it is not visible in price), which is sensible economically but non-quantitative and hard for a systematic manager to trade.

## [[MT191 - Trend Following Rewards Investors Who Wait - Systematic Investor - Ep.422]]
- [[MT191 - Trend Following Rewards Investors Who Wait - Systematic Investor - Ep.422#^MT191-C04-X1|MT191-C04-X1]] — A risk such as geopolitical risk can only be managed in a portfolio once it is measured and analysed, so a quant turns to academic literature and quantitative indicators rather than intuition.
- [[MT191 - Trend Following Rewards Investors Who Wait - Systematic Investor - Ep.422#^MT191-C06-5|MT191-C06-5]] — Testing bonds over very long histories (800 years) rather than only the recent decades shows bonds are good but vulnerable to inflation, which corrects the bias of recent data.
- [[MT191 - Trend Following Rewards Investors Who Wait - Systematic Investor - Ep.422#^MT191-C08-2|MT191-C08-2]] — A rules-based strategy has no view on direction, so it can be short bonds as easily as long, which is an underappreciated benefit for a total portfolio.
- [[MT191 - Trend Following Rewards Investors Who Wait - Systematic Investor - Ep.422#^MT191-C09-3|MT191-C09-3]] — The AQR paper studies assets and strategies across rising, stable and falling inflation, since different inflation regimes need different protection.
- [[MT191 - Trend Following Rewards Investors Who Wait - Systematic Investor - Ep.422#^MT191-C10-X2|MT191-C10-X2]] — One way to explain trend's value to investors is to quote its annualised alpha versus the S&P 500 (a correlation-adjusted measure) rather than raw returns; Niels floats it as a possible new language.

## [[MT192 - Trend Following Still Works — So Why Do Allocators Ignore It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.3]]
- [[MT192 - Trend Following Still Works — So Why Do Allocators Ignore It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.3#^MT192-C05-8|MT192-C05-8]] — A defensive strategy with a long record of actual data is more convincing to a committee than one backed by simulations.
- [[MT192 - Trend Following Still Works — So Why Do Allocators Ignore It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.3#^MT192-C08-1|MT192-C08-1]] — Quantica tests trend, gold and Treasuries as equity companions in the best and worst 10 percent of equity periods and in a 60 percent equity plus one-third each mix.
- [[MT192 - Trend Following Still Works — So Why Do Allocators Ignore It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.3#^MT192-C08-X1|MT192-C08-X1]] — Quantica's 1990 to 2025 trend results rely on the SG Trend Index but use a proxy for the 1990s because the index does not exist before then, so the full-period trend Sharpe of 0.64 partly depends on a constructed series.
- [[MT192 - Trend Following Still Works — So Why Do Allocators Ignore It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.3#^MT192-C10-1|MT192-C10-1]] — The SG Trend Indicator is a simple moving-average crossover with +1/-1 positions, live out of sample for 15 years.
- [[MT192 - Trend Following Still Works — So Why Do Allocators Ignore It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.3#^MT192-C10-5|MT192-C10-5]] — A trivial trend indicator is about 80 percent correlated with the SG CTA index, so replication papers that reach similar correlations add little.
- [[MT192 - Trend Following Still Works — So Why Do Allocators Ignore It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.3#^MT192-C11-1|MT192-C11-1]] — Almost any CTA is 80 to 90 percent correlated with the CTA index, unlike the 99.99 percent between SPY and the S&P 500, so replication correlation is not informative.
- [[MT192 - Trend Following Still Works — So Why Do Allocators Ignore It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.3#^MT192-C11-2|MT192-C11-2]] — The simple SG trend indicator returned only 0.43 percent a year with 16 percent volatility (Sharpe about 0.03) since 2002 despite high correlation to the index.
- [[MT192 - Trend Following Still Works — So Why Do Allocators Ignore It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.3#^MT192-C11-5|MT192-C11-5]] — Correlation to a benchmark ignores the mean, so the question for a manager is the added mean return over the high-correlation, zero-alpha index.
- [[MT192 - Trend Following Still Works — So Why Do Allocators Ignore It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.3#^MT192-C12-1|MT192-C12-1]] — The American Turtle approach opens positions on breakouts and exits with an ATR trailing stop, flipping short on the reverse breakout.
- [[MT192 - Trend Following Still Works — So Why Do Allocators Ignore It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.3#^MT192-C12-2|MT192-C12-2]] — The European school (AHL and descendants) uses continuously adjusted positions from moving-average crossovers with volatility and risk scaling.
- [[MT192 - Trend Following Still Works — So Why Do Allocators Ignore It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.3#^MT192-C13-1|MT192-C13-1]] — Time-series momentum (Moskowitz-Ooi-Pedersen) takes the sign of volatility-adjusted past year return.
- [[MT192 - Trend Following Still Works — So Why Do Allocators Ignore It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.3#^MT192-C13-6|MT192-C13-6]] — The paper derives the optimal trend speed for known processes (white noise, AR(1), fractional ARMA), which gives a benchmark though the true process is unknown.
- [[MT192 - Trend Following Still Works — So Why Do Allocators Ignore It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.3#^MT192-C14-2|MT192-C14-2]] — Test a strategy with a negative lag (look-ahead) and compare its P&L to the theoretically expected Sharpe; a mismatch signals a bug.
- [[MT192 - Trend Following Still Works — So Why Do Allocators Ignore It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.3#^MT192-C14-3|MT192-C14-3]] — Run the strategy on random noise; if some variants make money the strategy is overfitted.
- [[MT192 - Trend Following Still Works — So Why Do Allocators Ignore It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.3#^MT192-C14-4|MT192-C14-4]] — Simulating known processes gives a benchmark for the best achievable performance to judge whether a new trend indicator extracts what it should.
- [[MT192 - Trend Following Still Works — So Why Do Allocators Ignore It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.3#^MT192-C15-2|MT192-C15-2]] — A 12-month TSMOM equal-weights returns and is optimal for capturing drift unless there is a strong view that autocorrelation varies through the year, so beginners can stop there.
- [[MT192 - Trend Following Still Works — So Why Do Allocators Ignore It- - Systematic Investor - Ep.3#^MT192-C16-3|MT192-C16-3]] — Breakout, moving-average and TSMOM managers end up with similar long-run performance; no evidence that one style is better.

## [[MT193 - Trend Following as a Return Enhancement Overlay - Systematic Investor 297]]
- [[MT193 - Trend Following as a Return Enhancement Overlay - Systematic Investor 297#^MT193-C08-1|MT193-C08-1]] — A SocGen paper asks whether systematic quant macro programs (83 programs, 24 used for PCA) are heterogeneous and how different they are from trend followers.
- [[MT193 - Trend Following as a Return Enhancement Overlay - Systematic Investor 297#^MT193-C09-1|MT193-C09-1]] — The first principal component explains 24 percent of quant macro return variance (second about 13 percent) versus 57 percent for trend followers.
- [[MT193 - Trend Following as a Return Enhancement Overlay - Systematic Investor 297#^MT193-C12-3|MT193-C12-3]] — Test design: S&P 500 versus S&P 500 plus a notionally funded 30 percent SG Trend excess-return overlay with matching volatility, compared by quarterly return deciles since 2000.

## [[MT194 - Trend Following during Trump Tariff Turmoil! - Systematic Investor 344]]
- [[MT194 - Trend Following during Trump Tariff Turmoil! - Systematic Investor 344#^MT194-C05-X1|MT194-C05-X1]] — Katie's drawdown study is used as a diagnostic: by comparing the depth and duration of an S&P drawdown with past episodes one can judge whether the current move is a short-term dislocation or the start of a structural regime change.
- [[MT194 - Trend Following during Trump Tariff Turmoil! - Systematic Investor 344#^MT194-C06-1|MT194-C06-1]] — Katie's paper 'Trade War: Crisis or Correction?' measures managed futures returns across S&P 500 peak-to-trough drawdowns by depth and length.
- [[MT194 - Trend Following during Trump Tariff Turmoil! - Systematic Investor 344#^MT194-C07-1|MT194-C07-1]] — Deep drawdown episodes are split into short sharp recoveries and extended crises to compare trend paths.
- [[MT194 - Trend Following during Trump Tariff Turmoil! - Systematic Investor 344#^MT194-C08-1|MT194-C08-1]] — A chart by Tyler Lovingood splits CTA performance in the first 10 percent of an S&P drawdown versus from 10 to 20 percent.
- [[MT194 - Trend Following during Trump Tariff Turmoil! - Systematic Investor 344#^MT194-C11-1|MT194-C11-1]] — A paper showed a 100-day vs 105-day moving average could differ by about 20 percent in one year, so parameter choice can drive large short-run differences.
- [[MT194 - Trend Following during Trump Tariff Turmoil! - Systematic Investor 344#^MT194-C12-1|MT194-C12-1]] — What differentiates one trend engine from another is trading speed, market universe and weights, and risk management.
- [[MT194 - Trend Following during Trump Tariff Turmoil! - Systematic Investor 344#^MT194-C14-2|MT194-C14-2]] — Tilting trend speed by asset class has little statistical evidence and should be done only on the margin; there is no magic speed.
- [[MT194 - Trend Following during Trump Tariff Turmoil! - Systematic Investor 344#^MT194-C14-X1|MT194-C14-X1]] — Market-universe analysis tends to flag markets that look poor in backtests (cocoa looked poor for about 15 years and then trended strongly), so dropping markets on past performance risks missing later trends.
- [[MT194 - Trend Following during Trump Tariff Turmoil! - Systematic Investor 344#^MT194-C16-1|MT194-C16-1]] — A single combined return stream in portable alpha hides how good the trend engine is, so its contribution should be tracked.
- [[MT194 - Trend Following during Trump Tariff Turmoil! - Systematic Investor 344#^MT194-C17-4|MT194-C17-4]] — Correlation cannot be measured reliably over short windows, so being too precise on correlations in chaotic periods may not help.
- [[MT194 - Trend Following during Trump Tariff Turmoil! - Systematic Investor 344#^MT194-C18-2|MT194-C18-2]] — Volatility enters signal strength as signal-to-noise as well as position sizing.
- [[MT194 - Trend Following during Trump Tariff Turmoil! - Systematic Investor 344#^MT194-C18-3|MT194-C18-3]] — Even perfect volatility foresight barely improves trend performance because volatility gives the range not the direction.

## [[MT195 - Trend Following in 2025- New Challenges, New Strategies! - Systematic Investor 326]]
- [[MT195 - Trend Following in 2025- New Challenges, New Strategies! - Systematic Investor 326#^MT195-C02-4|MT195-C02-4]] — CTA style factors perturb one design decision of a plain trend system versus a benchmark to isolate speed, size, co-movement and correlation tilts, explaining return dispersion across managers.
- [[MT195 - Trend Following in 2025- New Challenges, New Strategies! - Systematic Investor 326#^MT195-C02-5|MT195-C02-5]] — The speed factor measures whether a trend system is faster or slower than the peer set.
- [[MT195 - Trend Following in 2025- New Challenges, New Strategies! - Systematic Investor 326#^MT195-C02-6|MT195-C02-6]] — The size factor measures allocation to smaller versus larger markets, analogous to the equity size factor.
- [[MT195 - Trend Following in 2025- New Challenges, New Strategies! - Systematic Investor 326#^MT195-C02-8|MT195-C02-8]] — The correlation factor measures leaning into less correlated versus more correlated markets.
- [[MT195 - Trend Following in 2025- New Challenges, New Strategies! - Systematic Investor 326#^MT195-C07-3|MT195-C07-3]] — Quantica's paper tests static contract sizing versus dynamic volatility sizing over 30 years.
- [[MT195 - Trend Following in 2025- New Challenges, New Strategies! - Systematic Investor 326#^MT195-C07-X2|MT195-C07-X2]] — The finding that volatility rose more in winning trades is partly endogenous, because profitable trends mechanically have higher volatility; only the size of the gap between winners and losers (1.2 versus 1.05) is informative.
- [[MT195 - Trend Following in 2025- New Challenges, New Strategies! - Systematic Investor 326#^MT195-C08-5|MT195-C08-5]] — Static versus dynamic sizing is a spectrum determined by how volatility is measured and how reactive the system is.
- [[MT195 - Trend Following in 2025- New Challenges, New Strategies! - Systematic Investor 326#^MT195-C09-2|MT195-C09-2]] — Classic trend followers want concentration in volatile winners to capture outliers, while statistical portfolio managers see no reason to concentrate there.
- [[MT195 - Trend Following in 2025- New Challenges, New Strategies! - Systematic Investor 326#^MT195-C10-4|MT195-C10-4]] — The Quantica 30-year sample (1994 to 2024) relies on infrequent outliers, so results could differ in another period; blends with equities and skewness were not analysed.

## [[MT196 - Trend Following in Crisis- - Systematic Investor 346]]
- [[MT196 - Trend Following in Crisis- - Systematic Investor 346#^MT196-C04-4|MT196-C04-4]] — Dispersion of returns across trend followers is not unusual historically (CFM research); speed, risk management and markets traded are the usual sources of differentiation.
- [[MT196 - Trend Following in Crisis- - Systematic Investor 346#^MT196-C05-1|MT196-C05-1]] — Portable alpha products (trend overlay on equity beta) had negative alpha and flat beta in April 2025, so looked bad though doing what they are designed to do.
- [[MT196 - Trend Following in Crisis- - Systematic Investor 346#^MT196-C08-X1|MT196-C08-X1]] — Implementing systematic futures strategies needs practical market-structure knowledge (quoting conventions such as US bonds in 32nds/64ths, how futures trade and settle), and early-career quant work is mostly data cleaning and inspecting numbers; ignoring contract mechanics causes data and execution errors.
- [[MT196 - Trend Following in Crisis- - Systematic Investor 346#^MT196-C10-3|MT196-C10-3]] — Selecting positions dynamically from a larger instrument universe shows noisy return differences year to year versus the earlier set, but gives better risk targeting, which is a reason to keep it.
- [[MT196 - Trend Following in Crisis- - Systematic Investor 346#^MT196-C10-X1|MT196-C10-X1]] — Redesigning a system after a bad year is a dangerous path (it fits recent noise); the post-year design review should be symmetric and be asked after an exceptional year too, with changes only if the evidence is clear.
- [[MT196 - Trend Following in Crisis- - Systematic Investor 346#^MT196-C11-2|MT196-C11-2]] — The longer a strategy runs, the larger its worst drawdown is expected to be, since expected worst drawdown grows with time, so veteran funds hitting new worst drawdowns is mathematically unsurprising.
- [[MT196 - Trend Following in Crisis- - Systematic Investor 346#^MT196-C12-1|MT196-C12-1]] — Compare the current drawdown to the probability of such a drawdown in a comparable period (about 30% over 12 years) rather than just to the backtest max; a 50% drawdown would be well beyond the backtest and expectation and be a real concern.
- [[MT196 - Trend Following in Crisis- - Systematic Investor 346#^MT196-C12-2|MT196-C12-2]] — Because trend signals have a modest Sharpe ratio, it takes a very long time to statistically tell that a strategy has stopped working, so continuing is largely a matter of faith.
- [[MT196 - Trend Following in Crisis- - Systematic Investor 346#^MT196-C13-1|MT196-C13-1]] — Attempts to meta-time trend following with quantitative conditioning variables found very few useful predictors; best response is providing visibility by showing historical analogues where trend did fine.
- [[MT196 - Trend Following in Crisis- - Systematic Investor 346#^MT196-C13-2|MT196-C13-2]] — Rob checks commissions plus slippage annually (about 100 bp, in line with backtest) because costs are predictable while P&L is nearly random; slippage is the fill price versus mid at order time, and passive limit orders then switching give lower costs than the conservative half-spread backtest.
- [[MT196 - Trend Following in Crisis- - Systematic Investor 346#^MT196-C15-3|MT196-C15-3]] — Different trend indicators are highly correlated, so no one is uniquely best, but once automated the cost of adding another signal is zero, so you might as well include several.
- [[MT196 - Trend Following in Crisis- - Systematic Investor 346#^MT196-C15-4|MT196-C15-4]] — A single calibrated exponential moving average can summarise the whole universe of trend indicators (a representative filter), using the mathematical link between exponential and simple moving averages.
- [[MT196 - Trend Following in Crisis- - Systematic Investor 346#^MT196-C16-1|MT196-C16-1]] — The paper reports an exponential moving average with a 112-day parameter is optimal at capturing trends; Rob would not replace his multi-indicator system but values it as first-principles thinking.
- [[MT196 - Trend Following in Crisis- - Systematic Investor 346#^MT196-C16-2|MT196-C16-2]] — There is a fine balance between simplicity and over-complication in trend systems and it makes a big difference.

## [[MT197 - Trend Following over the next few years - Systematic Investor 314]]
- [[MT197 - Trend Following over the next few years - Systematic Investor 314#^MT197-C05-2|MT197-C05-2]] — Separate three things: what I think they should do, how the market perceives it, and what the market's price reaction says; the third drives the trade (continuation, reversal or mean reversion).
- [[MT197 - Trend Following over the next few years - Systematic Investor 314#^MT197-C07-1|MT197-C07-1]] — The KUU (known, unknown, unknowable) framework: act on what is measurable, avoid what cannot be handicapped, and diversify against the unknowable.
- [[MT197 - Trend Following over the next few years - Systematic Investor 314#^MT197-C10-2|MT197-C10-2]] — Several managers overrode models in March 2020 for risk management; a backtest is worth little if you tinker with the model.
- [[MT197 - Trend Following over the next few years - Systematic Investor 314#^MT197-C10-3|MT197-C10-3]] — Systematic managers explain what they do poorly, and investors ask poor questions; the key is stating when and why a model should do well or poorly, not the tautology 'when there are trends'.
- [[MT197 - Trend Following over the next few years - Systematic Investor 314#^MT197-C10-X1|MT197-C10-X1]] — Explaining when trend following works needs fundamental research into exogenous, non-price conditions (macro and environmental factors necessary or sufficient for positive trend returns), which is an open research agenda beyond the tautology 'it does well when there are trends'.
- [[MT197 - Trend Following over the next few years - Systematic Investor 314#^MT197-C11-2|MT197-C11-2]] — Detailed parameter questions (20 vs 30 day volatility lookback) do not help investors understand future performance.
- [[MT197 - Trend Following over the next few years - Systematic Investor 314#^MT197-C12-1|MT197-C12-1]] — Attribution (why money was made in crude or bonds, and which conditions allowed it) is more informative than contribution (how much was made per market).
- [[MT197 - Trend Following over the next few years - Systematic Investor 314#^MT197-C12-X1|MT197-C12-X1]] — Investors often hold untested beliefs about system design (the best trend speed is three months; trading 500 markets must beat 100) that clash with a manager's evidence, so claims about speed and market count need evidence, not assertion.
- [[MT197 - Trend Following over the next few years - Systematic Investor 314#^MT197-C13-2|MT197-C13-2]] — Reaction patterns are reliable only within a sector (e.g. financials); beyond that few have high confidence, and trend following's strength is not needing to know how things play out.
- [[MT197 - Trend Following over the next few years - Systematic Investor 314#^MT197-C16-1|MT197-C16-1]] — The SG index is built from the largest managers by AUM, reconstituted yearly; a diversified portfolio of selected CTAs should be able to beat it.
- [[MT197 - Trend Following over the next few years - Systematic Investor 314#^MT197-C16-2|MT197-C16-2]] — The new SG investable CTA index is a giant backtest of a model, and buying a CTA replicator index is not the same as buying a stock index.
- [[MT197 - Trend Following over the next few years - Systematic Investor 314#^MT197-C16-X1|MT197-C16-X1]] — Citing Transtrend and Michael Green's 'Why bother being better': investors should look for managers who strive to differ from and beat the index rather than simply buying the CTA index, since passive concentration distorts markets and an index is not risk-free.
- [[MT197 - Trend Following over the next few years - Systematic Investor 314#^MT197-C17-2|MT197-C17-2]] — Replicators aim for median manager returns, not a benchmark; median replication is suitable for low-cost investors with no manager skill, while most managers underperform their benchmarks.

## [[MT198 - Trend Following to Super Determinism - Systematic Investor 342]]
- [[MT198 - Trend Following to Super Determinism - Systematic Investor 342#^MT198-C04-1|MT198-C04-1]] — Bill Dunn used a fractal wave algorithm that took the fractal structure of Elliott wave but rejected the wave counting.
- [[MT198 - Trend Following to Super Determinism - Systematic Investor 342#^MT198-C07-1|MT198-C07-1]] — Moritz Seibert's point: it is the economic models that are uncertain, while markets are definite, so models used to explain the system for 30-40 years no longer work but trend following relies on robustness over many cycles.
- [[MT198 - Trend Following to Super Determinism - Systematic Investor 342#^MT198-C11-1|MT198-C11-1]] — Markets are open, nested systems of subsystems so prediction is futile beyond a very short horizon (perhaps two days with the biggest computers).

## [[MT199 - Trend Following too complicated- - Systematic Investor 296]]
- [[MT199 - Trend Following too complicated- - Systematic Investor 296#^MT199-C10-2|MT199-C10-2]] — Some trend managers (AQR) use roughly 50/50 price and economic signals; systematic macro that combines story and rules was popular ten years ago, worked for a while and then many stopped working.
- [[MT199 - Trend Following too complicated- - Systematic Investor 296#^MT199-C11-2|MT199-C11-2]] — Niels prefers price because there is only one price while economic data and the yield curves are ambiguous; COVID shutdown followed by 50-60% equity gains made no sense economically.
- [[MT199 - Trend Following too complicated- - Systematic Investor 296#^MT199-C11-X1|MT199-C11-X1]] — The inverted yield curve (Campbell Harvey's recession signal) had not produced a recession after about two years, and defenders then say a different yield curve was the right one; such shifting of the indicator shows how ambiguous macro signals are, which is why Niels prefers price, the one unambiguous series.
- [[MT199 - Trend Following too complicated- - Systematic Investor 296#^MT199-C12-1|MT199-C12-1]] — The inverted yield curve and the COVID economic data would have cut equity exposure and missed gains, but blending macro and price trends gives smoother returns, lower risk and higher Sharpe; systematic macro is still in its infancy.
- [[MT199 - Trend Following too complicated- - Systematic Investor 296#^MT199-C13-1|MT199-C13-1]] — Volatility is countable risk; uncertainty is non-countable (a pandemic, a recession with fewer than a dozen post-WWII samples versus 30 needed), so yield-curve recession signals are subjective; political uncertainty indices were lower than two years earlier.
- [[MT199 - Trend Following too complicated- - Systematic Investor 296#^MT199-C15-1|MT199-C15-1]] — The industry is adding complexity to adapt to new phenomena (pandemic, zero rates) and for marketing, to create an alpha gap versus managers running the same models; whether complexity helps is unproven.
- [[MT199 - Trend Following too complicated- - Systematic Investor 296#^MT199-C15-2|MT199-C15-2]] — Model builders behave like engineers designing under uncertainty (unlike bridge builders who know the stresses), adding complexity to cope with market uncertainty rather than from physics envy.

## [[MT200 - Trend Following- How much to Allocate- - Systematic Investor 316]]
- [[MT200 - Trend Following- How much to Allocate- - Systematic Investor 316#^MT200-C09-1|MT200-C09-1]] — Man Group paper 'Risky Business: why the smart money forecasts risk not returns': CTAs are first and foremost risk managers; returns cannot be forecast but risks can.
- [[MT200 - Trend Following- How much to Allocate- - Systematic Investor 316#^MT200-C10-1|MT200-C10-1]] — Most bigger managers use dynamic position sizing, but long-term returns of static and dynamic managers are hard to tell apart, as is 500 vs 50 markets; Niels does not buy that 500 markets is better than 50.
- [[MT200 - Trend Following- How much to Allocate- - Systematic Investor 316#^MT200-C10-3|MT200-C10-3]] — Return prediction models constrained to positive forecasts look better over the long run (equity returns are positive by design) but may not keep the investor solvent at the times returns are negative.
- [[MT200 - Trend Following- How much to Allocate- - Systematic Investor 316#^MT200-C13-2|MT200-C13-2]] — The carry-optimized trend benefit is based on 1998-2023, exactly when the carry regime started, so the enhancement may overfit; the next 20 years may differ (e.g. rates at 25%) and purity is more robust.
- [[MT200 - Trend Following- How much to Allocate- - Systematic Investor 316#^MT200-C14-2|MT200-C14-2]] — Conventional sizing (mean-variance optimisation) does not work for deciding how much to allocate to trend following; the 2019 paper and a recent large-CTA paper reach the same conclusion.
- [[MT200 - Trend Following- How much to Allocate- - Systematic Investor 316#^MT200-C15-X1|MT200-C15-X1]] — Asked what expected return to put into a mean-variance optimiser for trend following, Nick would say zero (no ex ante basis and it should not work in theory); yet using its historical Sharpe gives about 70% allocation, so optimiser inputs for trend are either unusable or absurd, which is why a different sizing method is needed.

## [[MT201 - Trend Following...Austrian Style - Open Interest 8]]
- [[MT201 - Trend Following...Austrian Style - Open Interest 8#^MT201-C03-X1|MT201-C03-X1]] — SMN chose trend following (after testing technical indicators on the bank desk) because it was the concept it found most robust, with the Turtles' and local CTAs' success as extra motivation.
- [[MT201 - Trend Following...Austrian Style - Open Interest 8#^MT201-C04-1|MT201-C04-1]] — Adding discretionary overlays was tested only in a side fund, never in the flagship, because changing style in a live track record is to be avoided; the discretionary plus systematic mix failed.
- [[MT201 - Trend Following...Austrian Style - Open Interest 8#^MT201-C04-2|MT201-C04-2]] — Dispersion among trend-following CTAs is explained more by market universe and position-sizing logic than by signal design.
- [[MT201 - Trend Following...Austrian Style - Open Interest 8#^MT201-C05-1|MT201-C05-1]] — Some CTAs bias signals toward long positions in bonds and equities, which is another source of dispersion in trend following results.
- [[MT201 - Trend Following...Austrian Style - Open Interest 8#^MT201-C05-3|MT201-C05-3]] — Tilting signals to what worked in the past (for example removing short signals) is overfitting; SMN keeps equal risk on long and short signals in every market.
- [[MT201 - Trend Following...Austrian Style - Open Interest 8#^MT201-C07-3|MT201-C07-3]] — SMN dropped short-term systems around 2007-08 and concentrated on medium/long-term trend following as the most robust horizon.
- [[MT201 - Trend Following...Austrian Style - Open Interest 8#^MT201-C08-1|MT201-C08-1]] — Backtests of illiquid markets use deliberately high slippage assumptions, plus broker execution costs, to avoid overstating results.
- [[MT201 - Trend Following...Austrian Style - Open Interest 8#^MT201-C08-5|MT201-C08-5]] — SMN has standard rules to judge whether a backtest result is realistic because over-optimisation of the past is the main research risk.
- [[MT201 - Trend Following...Austrian Style - Open Interest 8#^MT201-C10-1|MT201-C10-1]] — Pure momentum on the VIX behaved poorly in the 2009-2019 lost decade, so SMN built an enhanced momentum model.

## [[MT202 - Trump 2.0 & The Unpinning of Volatility - Systematic Investor 307]]
- [[MT202 - Trump 2.0 & The Unpinning of Volatility - Systematic Investor 307#^MT202-C04-4|MT202-C04-4]] — Long-vol returns are wide-ranging; passive put buying lost more than it made, while skill in timing leverage up/down and in relative-value (dispersion, cross-index) pays.
- [[MT202 - Trump 2.0 & The Unpinning of Volatility - Systematic Investor 307#^MT202-C05-4|MT202-C05-4]] — The Barclay Hedge Volatility Trading index (23 funds, +3% end of June 2024) was found as a benchmark; Jim prefers more specific Cboe strategy indexes to benchmark vol managers.
- [[MT202 - Trump 2.0 & The Unpinning of Volatility - Systematic Investor 307#^MT202-C07-3|MT202-C07-3]] — Jim's framework: combine macro (multi-year structural distribution and incentives) with flows/market microstructure to align short, medium and long term.

## [[MT203 - Trump's NEW Treasury Secretary on Macro Strategy ft. Scott Bessent]]
- [[MT203 - Trump's NEW Treasury Secretary on Macro Strategy ft. Scott Bessent#^MT203-C01-1|MT203-C01-1]] — The Soros-Druckenmiller style begins from equity research and company conversations about the real economy, then builds a macro mental model, rather than forecasting data prints.
- [[MT203 - Trump's NEW Treasury Secretary on Macro Strategy ft. Scott Bessent#^MT203-C02-1|MT203-C02-1]] — Process: look for systems that are accelerating or about to break down, form a theory, prove it empirically, then check framing.
- [[MT203 - Trump's NEW Treasury Secretary on Macro Strategy ft. Scott Bessent#^MT203-C02-X1|MT203-C02-X1]] — Bessent distrusts false certainty in macro: he treats economics as a social science, uses the rule 'never say never and never say always', and holds theories loosely while following a process (theory, empirical proof, framing).
- [[MT203 - Trump's NEW Treasury Secretary on Macro Strategy ft. Scott Bessent#^MT203-C03-2|MT203-C03-2]] — The achievable edge today is inferential or framing, not data or information; patience and saying no (Dr No) are part of the process.
- [[MT203 - Trump's NEW Treasury Secretary on Macro Strategy ft. Scott Bessent#^MT203-C07-2|MT203-C07-2]] — Gauges for China: trade balances with developed versus global South, repackaged exports, US tech sanctions, PBoC and yield curve, internal stress, external indicators, fund flows.
- [[MT203 - Trump's NEW Treasury Secretary on Macro Strategy ft. Scott Bessent#^MT203-C09-5|MT203-C09-5]] — Implementation: find the one fulcrum trade and keep the portfolio simple.
- [[MT203 - Trump's NEW Treasury Secretary on Macro Strategy ft. Scott Bessent#^MT203-C10-2|MT203-C10-2]] — A portfolio should be expressible simply (Soros: does it mean up or down); complexity (swaptions) is discouraged.
- [[MT203 - Trump's NEW Treasury Secretary on Macro Strategy ft. Scott Bessent#^MT203-C10-4|MT203-C10-4]] — The yen was 40% undervalued but valuation does not matter for timing currency moves.
- [[MT203 - Trump's NEW Treasury Secretary on Macro Strategy ft. Scott Bessent#^MT203-C14-2|MT203-C14-2]] — Career advice: take risk, avoid fixed obligations, and find mega trends (e.g. leisure and gaming as AI frees time).
- [[MT203 - Trump's NEW Treasury Secretary on Macro Strategy ft. Scott Bessent#^MT203-C15-1|MT203-C15-1]] — Being in a big trend covers up many mistakes in execution.

## [[MT204 - Unpacking QIS- Structure, Scale, and Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.372]]
- [[MT204 - Unpacking QIS- Structure, Scale, and Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.372#^MT204-C05-1|MT204-C05-1]] — QIS size figures are uncertain because notional is reported inconsistently (unfunded swaps vs options, leverage and delta adjustment) and asset-manager and bank numbers may double count.
- [[MT204 - Unpacking QIS- Structure, Scale, and Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.372#^MT204-C06-1|MT204-C06-1]] — Part of QIS growth is substitution: exposure that was in a CDA or swap is repackaged as a QIS index, not genuine new strategy demand.
- [[MT204 - Unpacking QIS- Structure, Scale, and Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.372#^MT204-C06-X1|MT204-C06-X1]] — The $1.3tn survey could also be too low, because hedge funds and other managers running risk-premium strategies do not report to it, so the true size is uncertain in both directions.
- [[MT204 - Unpacking QIS- Structure, Scale, and Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.372#^MT204-C07-3|MT204-C07-3]] — Clients use QIS for implementation and add their own value through timing and sizing models.
- [[MT204 - Unpacking QIS- Structure, Scale, and Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.372#^MT204-C08-2|MT204-C08-2]] — QIS development has four drivers: academic research, shifting client appetite from hedge fund replacement to specific outcomes (defensive, yield, inflation, regime), technology, and market evolution (new commodities, listed short options).
- [[MT204 - Unpacking QIS- Structure, Scale, and Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.372#^MT204-C08-3|MT204-C08-3]] — New tradable markets (frontier commodities, listed options) allow out-of-sample tests net of cost on universes that could not be tested before.
- [[MT204 - Unpacking QIS- Structure, Scale, and Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.372#^MT204-C09-1|MT204-C09-1]] — There are thousands of QIS indices mostly because of client customisation (tax treatment net vs gross total return, no equity upside, portfolio exclusions) not because designers kept replacing indices after overfitting.
- [[MT204 - Unpacking QIS- Structure, Scale, and Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.372#^MT204-C09-X1|MT204-C09-X1]] — QIS indices are run under governance: European benchmark rules require an independent calculation agent, and banks use either an internally separated calculation unit or an external agent, a business choice as long as governance holds.
- [[MT204 - Unpacking QIS- Structure, Scale, and Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.372#^MT204-C12-1|MT204-C12-1]] — Data mining cannot be avoided; its control is product culture: call it out, look at tweaks as data-mined, and ensure new tweaks are not designed to make the Sharpe look better after a drawdown.
- [[MT204 - Unpacking QIS- Structure, Scale, and Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.372#^MT204-C12-X1|MT204-C12-X1]] — Many 'new' risk-premium indices pitched to a quant desk are small twists on existing strategies, a sign of pressure to launch products for their own sake and of over-optimisation.
- [[MT204 - Unpacking QIS- Structure, Scale, and Systematic Investing - Systematic Investor - Ep.372#^MT204-C13-1|MT204-C13-1]] — Explainable underperformance builds client trust; unexplained underperformance is worst.

## [[MT205 - Unpacking the Chaos- What Today’s Market Signals Really Mean - Systematic Investor 343]]
- [[MT205 - Unpacking the Chaos- What Today’s Market Signals Really Mean - Systematic Investor 343#^MT205-C06-2|MT205-C06-2]] — Study by Tyler Loving: SG CTA index was only down 3.56% when the S&P first fell 10% (19 Feb to 11 Mar 2025); across about ten 10% S&P drawdowns since 2000 the average was -3.18%, median -2.81%.
- [[MT205 - Unpacking the Chaos- What Today’s Market Signals Really Mean - Systematic Investor 343#^MT205-C08-X1|MT205-C08-X1]] — The April 2025 tariff shock lies outside historical data, so it is a genuine out-of-sample stress test; Niels proposes testing whether CTAs trading 400-600 markets did better or worse than those with a more concentrated portfolio.
- [[MT205 - Unpacking the Chaos- What Today’s Market Signals Really Mean - Systematic Investor 343#^MT205-C10-X1|MT205-C10-X1]] — The extra volatility seen when a trend is extended cannot be attributed to CTAs alone: closet trend followers, macro traders and ordinary de-risking investors also exit at the same time, so it measures the combined impact of trend-following behaviour (Yo Kit accepts this).
- [[MT205 - Unpacking the Chaos- What Today’s Market Signals Really Mean - Systematic Investor 343#^MT205-C11-1|MT205-C11-1]] — Performance of different trend predictors over time diverges widely (100% to 400% cumulative), so predictor mix matters, and tilting to the predictor suitable for a market could add return.
- [[MT205 - Unpacking the Chaos- What Today’s Market Signals Really Mean - Systematic Investor 343#^MT205-C16-1|MT205-C16-1]] — A 1-3 month trend signal can be played forward since half the window is known, allowing smoother, cheaper trading into the likely future position.
- [[MT205 - Unpacking the Chaos- What Today’s Market Signals Really Mean - Systematic Investor 343#^MT205-C16-2|MT205-C16-2]] — Playing forward positions can cause the model to predict; the host prefers acting on today's signal.
- [[MT205 - Unpacking the Chaos- What Today’s Market Signals Really Mean - Systematic Investor 343#^MT205-C17-2|MT205-C17-2]] — Trend predictors are mean-reverting (OU-like): the stronger the signal, the stronger the pullback to zero, so one must fight costs more at high signal strength; committing at signal 2 to 2.3 persists longer than at 3 to 3.1.
- [[MT205 - Unpacking the Chaos- What Today’s Market Signals Really Mean - Systematic Investor 343#^MT205-C17-X1|MT205-C17-X1]] — The cost-aware 'pull back to zero' logic applies to moving-average type predictors, which need fresh price support to hold a strong signal; a breakout system simply puts on the position and stays in it, so it does not manage cost this way.
- [[MT205 - Unpacking the Chaos- What Today’s Market Signals Really Mean - Systematic Investor 343#^MT205-C17-X2|MT205-C17-X2]] — Niels warns that skipping a trade because a spiking short-term signal 'will come back' risks missing the trend that does not revert (a type I versus type II error); Yo Kit replies that it is a cost-versus-alpha balance based on mean reversion of the predictor, not a price forecast.

## [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315]]
- [[MT206 - Upcoming Big Changes in Global Markets - Systematic Investor 315#^MT206-C16-X2|MT206-C16-X2]] — The populist-election result rests on only four to six hand-classified elections; Cham admits it may look cherry-picked but says the answer is the same with 4, 5 or 6 data points, which is a very small sample with subjective classification.

## [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364]]
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C07-4|MT207-C07-4]] — Across all systems and markets, delayed exits (next-day execution) give slightly better returns than stop orders.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C08-2|MT207-C08-2]] — A manager found in 2014 that delaying execution by a whole week gave better overall returns than reacting immediately.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C08-4|MT207-C08-4]] — Simple rules (Occam's razor) are more robust; extra filters and parameters mainly improve backtests, not future returns.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C09-1|MT207-C09-1]] — Entries use derivative-free indicators (price vs price N days ago, new 200-day high), avoiding moving averages though those also work.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C09-2|MT207-C09-2]] — The entry signal is both most and least important: whether MA, breakout, today or three days later matters little for a long-term trend system.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C09-X1|MT207-C09-X1]] — Large managers may prefer smoother indicators (moving averages) because they let risk be scaled in and out gradually; Moritz replies that the same smoothing can be achieved with several look-back windows on simple indicators.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C10-3|MT207-C10-3]] — A random entry plus cut-losses and let-winners-run rules makes money (Tom Basso); entries on 100/200-day highs improve on random entry.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C10-X1|MT207-C10-X1]] — Lowering the share of losing trades from 70% to 60% or 50% would lift returns sharply, but nothing statistically significant has been found that beats entering on a new high, so entry improvement is the research bottleneck.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C11-1|MT207-C11-1]] — Dynamic position sizing has two forms: vol/correlation-based (vol targeting) and risk/heat-based (loss if all stops hit); the firm uses neither except initial sizing.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C11-X1|MT207-C11-X1]] — Differences between position-sizing approaches (no resizing, vol-based, risk-based) are hard to see in very long-run manager performance, though they differ along the way.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C11-X2|MT207-C11-X2]] — The market universe a CTA trades is a major driver of its returns, so differences between managers often come from what they trade rather than sizing details.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C14-2|MT207-C14-2]] — Don Capital paper (caption spelling) compares 14 hedge fund strategies and whether leverage or vol changes their value, using the Barclay CTA index back to 1987.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C15-1|MT207-C15-1]] — Over 25 years of SG Trend data, Sharpe is middling for trend following.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C15-2|MT207-C15-2]] — Max drawdown relative to volatility is better for trend following than for all other strategies, including the S&P 500 and world government bonds.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C15-3|MT207-C15-3]] — On correlation, convexity and skew (timing of returns) trend following ranks much better than on Sharpe.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C15-4|MT207-C15-4]] — Sharpe is a portfolio measure: a 20% allocation (50/30/20) of trend raises returns a lot and, with leverage, improves drawdowns though Sharpe is a bit lower.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C16-1|MT207-C16-1]] — Four equity crises since 2000 (S&P drawdown above 20-25%): trend following cut portfolio drawdown more than any other strategy.
- [[MT207 - What Makes an Alternative Investment Truly Valuable- - Systematic Investor - Ep.364#^MT207-C16-X1|MT207-C16-X1]] — Crisis windows were defined by a fixed rule (S&P 500 drawdown of 20-25% or more), which gives only four episodes since 2000, so the result is rule-based but a very small sample.

## [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390]]
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C03-X1|MT210-C03-X1]] — Short-term CTAs differ far more in models and approaches than long-term trend managers, so the weakness of the short-term index does not describe every manager and several did well over 12 months.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C04-3|MT210-C04-3]] — Niels thinks fixed vs dynamic sizing matters little in the long run, but changes how strategies perform from time to time.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C05-1|MT210-C05-1]] — Trend underperforms 60/40 in many years but outperforms massively in a few (2008 and 2022 had about a 40-point differential), which is its diversifying value.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C05-5|MT210-C05-5]] — Attribution differs by crisis: commodities led in 2008, while 2022 was mainly dynamic bond/equity adjustment plus long dollar.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C05-X1|MT210-C05-X1]] — Splitting trend's edge over 60/40 into dynamic bond/equity positioning versus commodity/currency trading needs year-by-year P&L attribution against a static 60/40; the two effects are hard to disentangle.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C06-2|MT210-C06-2]] — A large CTA's sector attribution study showed crisis returns came mainly from commodities.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C07-2|MT210-C07-2]] — New hedge fund allocators should pick strategies with a record in major equity downturns (trend, macro, commodities, vol), not recent best performers in a bull market.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C08-1|MT210-C08-1]] — Return stacking and portable alpha packaging make strategies look good only because equities are doing well.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C08-5|MT210-C08-5]] — Niels's firm equaled a 1999 record of eight consecutive positive months in February 2026.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C08-X1|MT210-C08-X1]] — At the conference managers said they use AI in their business processes, but its impact so far is not in alpha generation; the uses are novel workflows even at macro managers.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C10-X1|MT210-C10-X1]] — Manager closures raise an index-construction question: AlphaQuest is in the SG CTA Index and it is unclear how a manager dropping out mid-year is treated, which matters for survivorship bias in CTA benchmarks.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C11-5|MT210-C11-5]] — Three unknowns for AI: size of impact, who benefits, and demand response.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C14-2|MT210-C14-2]] — Niels argues investing near a trend fund's worst drawdown is attractive, but Alan notes CTAs make the same case at highs and that investors struggle to hold.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C14-5|MT210-C14-5]] — Resulting: April 2025 adaptation to equity sell-off cost money when Trump reversed, but the decision was still sound.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C16-1|MT210-C16-1]] — Winton paper: two randomly chosen trend managers give about 5% annual tracking error versus SG Trend index (up to about plus or minus 3% performance gap).
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C16-2|MT210-C16-2]] — Matching volatility levels or picking the most correlated managers reduces tracking error; an equal-weight index gives high-vol managers more risk, so alternatively blend less-correlated managers at slightly higher vol.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C16-3|MT210-C16-3]] — Winton paper: CTAs differ by speed, market universe, non-trend signals and risk management, so blending managers averages speed and one manager may choose optimal speed better.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C16-X1|MT210-C16-X1]] — Even replication strategies cannot track a CTA index tightly; tracking error stays material unless one holds all (about ten) index managers, so investors must accept it.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C17-1|MT210-C17-1]] — The SG non-trend CTA index performed well in aggregate, but non-trend managers churn far more than trend managers; benefits of one manager with non-trend signals are fee netting and capital efficiency.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C17-3|MT210-C17-3]] — Replicating or blending to smooth an index can remove the convexity that makes CTAs valuable.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C17-X1|MT210-C17-X1]] — Adding alternative markets expands trend's opportunity set, but the Winton paper raises whether to hold one portfolio or two distinct ones; it is a value call on whether alternative-market access needs a specialist manager.
- [[MT210 - When Market Narratives Flip Overnight - Systematic Investor - Ep.390#^MT210-C18-3|MT210-C18-3]] — Whether a stable CTA beta exists to benchmark is doubtful; they dismiss the ChatGPT question.

## [[MT211 - When Prices Stop Making Sense - Systematic Investor - Ep.378]]
- [[MT211 - When Prices Stop Making Sense - Systematic Investor - Ep.378#^MT211-C03-3|MT211-C03-3]] — A trend signal's quality can be measured as signal strength divided by volatility, a signal-to-noise ratio, which scales how much to trust it.
- [[MT211 - When Prices Stop Making Sense - Systematic Investor - Ep.378#^MT211-C04-1|MT211-C04-1]] — When fundamental data is noisy, the central design choice for a systematic model is how much information beyond price to include.
- [[MT211 - When Prices Stop Making Sense - Systematic Investor - Ep.378#^MT211-C05-4|MT211-C05-4]] — 'Prices are primal': whatever other data says, the price reveals the current supply-demand balance, so price is the anchor for systematic models.
- [[MT211 - When Prices Stop Making Sense - Systematic Investor - Ep.378#^MT211-C06-3|MT211-C06-3]] — Smoothing prices (e.g. moving average) extracts a cleaner signal from noisy price processes.
- [[MT211 - When Prices Stop Making Sense - Systematic Investor - Ep.378#^MT211-C07-3|MT211-C07-3]] — Prices from thin days such as the Friday after Thanksgiving may be ignored or downweighted in models, although month-end volume complicates this.
- [[MT211 - When Prices Stop Making Sense - Systematic Investor - Ep.378#^MT211-C07-4|MT211-C07-4]] — After the 11-hour CME outage, a modeler must decide whether to trust post-outage prices, smooth, or drop signals; there is no rule and each choice is a model judgement.
- [[MT211 - When Prices Stop Making Sense - Systematic Investor - Ep.378#^MT211-C07-X1|MT211-C07-X1]] — Every systematic model has a 'personality' because the modeler must make many judgement calls on what data and prices to include or exclude, so two sensible builders produce different signals from the same markets.
- [[MT211 - When Prices Stop Making Sense - Systematic Investor - Ep.378#^MT211-C07-X2|MT211-C07-X2]] — National holidays close some markets while others stay open, so cross-market price series become asynchronous and the modeler must choose how to treat the missing or stale prices.
- [[MT211 - When Prices Stop Making Sense - Systematic Investor - Ep.378#^MT211-C12-2|MT211-C12-2]] — Aggregating stocks into industries reduces noise, so trends are cleaner at industry level than individual stocks.
- [[MT211 - When Prices Stop Making Sense - Systematic Investor - Ep.378#^MT211-C12-5|MT211-C12-5]] — A stochastic optimization text lists ten uncertainty types (observational, prognostic, experimental, transitional, inferential, model, exogenous, control implementation, algorithmic, goal), each of which affects signals.
- [[MT211 - When Prices Stop Making Sense - Systematic Investor - Ep.378#^MT211-C13-2|MT211-C13-2]] — Falling survey response rates and partisan splits in consumer surveys add measurement uncertainty to macro data.
- [[MT211 - When Prices Stop Making Sense - Systematic Investor - Ep.378#^MT211-C15-X1|MT211-C15-X1]] — A total portfolio approach still needs a reference benchmark (70/30 at CalPERS), and the origin of that benchmark is arbitrary (likely consultant-driven); Mark replies the strategic starting allocation and TPA process need better definition before the adaptive part can be judged.

## [[MT212 - Why Cooperation Is Essential in Crisis - IMF Managing Director Kristalina Georgieva]]
- [[MT212 - Why Cooperation Is Essential in Crisis - IMF Managing Director Kristalina Georgieva#^MT212-C01-1|MT212-C01-1]] — The IMF has a unique right to collect economic data from all 191 members, giving it a consistent global view (Article IV consultations, vital signs of economies).

## [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35]]
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C01-1|MT213-C01-1]] — Model users must continuously check whether a model's assumptions still hold and whether the data regime has shifted.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C01-2|MT213-C01-2]] — Pure model output should be combined with market awareness and a multi-disciplinary team (economics, finance, computer science, math).
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C02-2|MT213-C02-2]] — Web-scraped prices, behavior tracking and geospatial data now give a much tighter real-time view of consumers than monthly GDP.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C03-1|MT213-C03-1]] — The macro drivers (inflation, growth) are the same, but NLP on news feeds and foreign-language sources gives faster proxies than monthly or quarterly government data.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C04-3|MT213-C04-3]] — PGIM uses language models to track how inflation is discussed plus flow monitoring, to treat inflation as a risk factor.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C05-1|MT213-C05-1]] — Mid-horizon regime views come from fundamentals (forward rates, GDP, profits, jobs) and are combined with a short-term tactical view.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C06-1|MT213-C06-1]] — A Gaussian mixture model treats the economy as a latent state and classifies regimes from many data points.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C06-2|MT213-C06-2]] — The regime model avoids market prices because they move faster than the economy and would contaminate the medium-term economic state.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C06-4|MT213-C06-4]] — No single model is best; combine multiple regime and valuation models rather than trusting one scenario.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C07-1|MT213-C07-1]] — Winners of Kaggle model competitions use multi-model ensembles, supporting the multi-model approach.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C07-4|MT213-C07-4]] — About 80% of the macro model is fundamentals-based, with a small price/statistical component.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C08-1|MT213-C08-1]] — Macro has few independent observations, so models must be built with explicit priors and insight rather than regression coefficients dropped in.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C08-2|MT213-C08-2]] — A small, shorter-term statistical component complements the fundamental macro model.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C09-4|MT213-C09-4]] — Language models are a rich source of data and alpha for both quant equity and multi-asset.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C09-5|MT213-C09-5]] — Text-signal methods evolved from bag-of-words counts to BERT/FinBERT sentiment to LLM theme detection, while keeping traceability to raw data.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C10-1|MT213-C10-1]] — Text (reports, websites, 10-Ks) is a rich data source, and extraction advances matter more for risk than for alpha.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C10-2|MT213-C10-2]] — BERT/FinBERT are surprisingly good at sentiment, and LLMs are effective at assessing tone of speeches and text versus past patterns.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C10-3|MT213-C10-3]] — LLMs are statistical next-token predictors only as good as their training data, so evaluation tasks must fit that.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C12-2|MT213-C12-2]] — Quant models have only a small edge (right slightly more than 50%); many small bets add up and give a smoother ride than big calls (Moneyball).
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C12-3|MT213-C12-3]] — Prefer just enough model complexity to represent the world, leaning simple.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C12-X1|MT213-C12-X1]] — Quant researchers are debating simplicity versus complexity: the long-held rule of parsimonious, few-input models has been challenged by research arguing that more complex models can be justified.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C13-1|MT213-C13-1]] — Optimizing in-sample performance is the worst error; ask what the sample captured and whether it will persist.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C13-2|MT213-C13-2]] — Researchers must state what assumptions would make their research fail, with a devil's-advocate debate, to guard against overfitting.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C13-3|MT213-C13-3]] — With noisy data you can always find a period supporting any thesis, so economic findings must hold across periods.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C13-4|MT213-C13-4]] — Freeze parameters at research time and compare live out-of-sample performance; signals are typically positive but weaker than expected.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C13-X1|MT213-C13-X1]] — Researchers who want to make a name push to lift a strategy's information ratio (for example from 1 to 1.5), which creates an incentive to overfit in sample, so the team lead asks what would kill the research.
- [[MT213 - Why Macro Investing Is Becoming More Systematic - Allocator - Ep.35#^MT213-C14-1|MT213-C14-1]] — Live signal performance is usually still positive but below the in-sample estimate.

## [[MT214 - Why Macro Investing Matters More Than Ever - Kenneth Tropin on Markets, Volatility & Oppor]]
- [[MT214 - Why Macro Investing Matters More Than Ever - Kenneth Tropin on Markets, Volatility & Oppor#^MT214-C03-5|MT214-C03-5]] — Alpha decays, so sustaining performance requires continually innovating.
- [[MT214 - Why Macro Investing Matters More Than Ever - Kenneth Tropin on Markets, Volatility & Oppor#^MT214-C04-2|MT214-C04-2]] — Quant strategies using fundamentals such as yield, carry and relative value are complementary to price-based trend if the architecture is well done.
- [[MT214 - Why Macro Investing Matters More Than Ever - Kenneth Tropin on Markets, Volatility & Oppor#^MT214-C04-3|MT214-C04-3]] — Adding non-correlated alpha sources to trend following improves the information ratio and is a firm priority.
- [[MT214 - Why Macro Investing Matters More Than Ever - Kenneth Tropin on Markets, Volatility & Oppor#^MT214-C04-5|MT214-C04-5]] — Each poor period is used as a catalyst to improve the models for that market character.
- [[MT214 - Why Macro Investing Matters More Than Ever - Kenneth Tropin on Markets, Volatility & Oppor#^MT214-C04-6|MT214-C04-6]] — AI tools accelerate research and idea testing.
- [[MT214 - Why Macro Investing Matters More Than Ever - Kenneth Tropin on Markets, Volatility & Oppor#^MT214-C04-7|MT214-C04-7]] — AI-designed trading systems are not trusted; the process must be closely supervised.

## [[MT215 - Why Most Investors Fail at Trend Following - Systematic Investor - Ep.404]]
- [[MT215 - Why Most Investors Fail at Trend Following - Systematic Investor - Ep.404#^MT215-C03-4|MT215-C03-4]] — Niche markets (platinum, Japan, palm oil) are nice-to-haves; performance attribution shows they do not matter much, so reducing dimensionality for an ETF costs little.
- [[MT215 - Why Most Investors Fail at Trend Following - Systematic Investor - Ep.404#^MT215-C07-X2|MT215-C07-X2]] — A managed-futures product built without hedge-fund fees, scalable, and combined with low-fee tax-efficient equity exposure solves the product-market fit problem and a set-and-forget holding that the manager would use with his own money.
- [[MT215 - Why Most Investors Fail at Trend Following - Systematic Investor - Ep.404#^MT215-C11-X1|MT215-C11-X1]] — Large-firm trend products are credible when built by experienced trend teams (JP Morgan's quant team, Fidelity's hire of Rob Crochet) rather than by generalists given an engineering degree, which is the diligence test between serious and opportunistic entrants.
- [[MT215 - Why Most Investors Fail at Trend Following - Systematic Investor - Ep.404#^MT215-C12-2|MT215-C12-2]] — Complex trend programs look better in hindsight and in presentations, but simple durable rules hold up much better out of sample.
- [[MT215 - Why Most Investors Fail at Trend Following - Systematic Investor - Ep.404#^MT215-C13-1|MT215-C13-1]] — Most trend follower returns map to 4-6 principal components (energies, fixed income, grains, USD), about 90/10 Pareto, so adding many markets adds little.
- [[MT215 - Why Most Investors Fail at Trend Following - Systematic Investor - Ep.404#^MT215-C14-2|MT215-C14-2]] — Published research hides discarded models, so readers only see the final results (selection bias).
- [[MT215 - Why Most Investors Fail at Trend Following - Systematic Investor - Ep.404#^MT215-C14-3|MT215-C14-3]] — Trend profits may come from clusters (energy, metals, equities) moving together rather than 70 independent instruments.
